Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, Acathea was forged from the
chaos of the Titanworld's environment. The Codex Unbound, their founding
document, became a symbol of order and stability for the human survivors
who fled the planet. The First Archivists established the labyrinthine
city of Lexorium Prime, where they began codifying laws and histories.
This era was defined by survival, the gathering of knowledge, and the
creation of a society built on neutrality, reason, and justice. Nation
in 851 By 851 AG, the Archivist Dominion of Acathea had grown into a
galactic power of neutrality and influence. The Codex Unbound had become
a sacred document, guiding their impartial enforcement of treaties and
laws. The Flame Sentinels stood as guardians of stability, while the
Lexorium Conclave settled disputes between nations. Acathea's
cities---vast repositories of knowledge and history---attracted
diplomats, scholars, and mediators from across the galaxy, earning them
a reputation as the galaxy's moral arbiters. From 851 to 1851 851 - The
Founding of the Great Archive After fleeing the Titanworld, the human
survivors of Acathea establish the Great Archive, a sprawling
subterranean vault designed to house all records of galactic law,
history, and treaties. The first Council of Archivists is formed, with
each member overseeing a specific domain of knowledge. The founding of
the Great Archive marks the beginning of the Archivist Dominion. 950 -
The Codex Unbound The Council of Archivists completes the first version
of the Codex Unbound, a comprehensive and impartial collection of
galactic laws and agreements. Acathea begins offering legal mediation
and arbitration services to warring factions, solidifying its role as a
neutral enforcer of galactic order. 1000 - The Ivory Law Rebellion A
faction of Archivists attempts to seize control of the Dominion, arguing
that the Codex should favor Acathea's interests rather than remaining
impartial. Known as the Ivory Law Rebellion, the uprising is crushed by
loyalist Archivists and their elite Sentinel Keepers, warrior-scholars
trained to protect the Great Archive. The rebellion reinforces the
Dominion's commitment to neutrality and impartiality. 1050 - The
Labyrinth Cities Acathea's cities expand into vast, labyrinthine
complexes, with every structure serving as part of the Archivist
Network---a decentralized repository of knowledge and law. Each city is
overseen by a High Archivist who answers directly to the Council. These
cities become symbols of order, stability, and meticulous design. 1200 -
The Edict of Scroll and Sword The Council of Archivists implements the
Edict of Scroll and Sword, formalizing the Dominion's military doctrine.
While Acathea avoids large-scale conflicts, they establish a defensive
force of Sentinel Corps, a disciplined order trained in combat and
archival protection. Armed with advanced antimagic tools and Codex
Blades---weapons inscribed with runic laws---they serve as protectors of
the Dominion.

1300 - The Mediation of the Shattermarch Crisis Acathea successfully
mediates the Shattermarch Crisis between the Shatter Stance and the Old
Horde. Their impartial arbitration prevents a prolonged war, further
cementing their reputation as the galaxy's foremost mediators. The Codex
Unbound is expanded to incorporate new treaties and terms agreed upon
during the mediation. 1500 - The Codex Enlightenment Acathea achieves a
significant technological and cultural breakthrough with the Codex
Enlightenment, a project that digitizes vast portions of the Great
Archive into Aetheric Memory Nodes, powered by arcane energy and
technology. This innovation allows Acathea to rapidly disseminate laws
and treaties across the galaxy while safeguarding their physical
archives. 1685 - Early Founding of the Galactic League of States Acathea
is one of the key signatories of the martial alliance that would later
form the Galactic League of States, contributing sections of the Codex
Unbound as the foundation for the League's laws. While they hold no
special role, their impartiality makes them indispensable as arbiters of
disputes. The Dominion becomes known as the Pillars of Law, a moniker
granted by their peers. 1750 - The Silent Concord Crisis A secret
faction within Acathea attempts to manipulate the Codex Unbound to favor
a client state, sparking the Silent Concord Crisis. The Council of
Archivists uncovers the conspiracy and dismantles the faction,
reaffirming their commitment to impartiality. However, the incident
sparks concerns about corruption within the Dominion. 1800 - The
Sentinel Reformation The Sentinel Corps undergoes a major reformation,
incorporating advanced Aetheric Armor and data-driven combat tactics.
The Sentinels, once guardians of archives, evolve into a disciplined
peacekeeping force capable of quelling regional disputes without
compromising Acathea's neutrality. 1851 - The Bastion of Order By 1851,
Acathea stands as the galaxy's foremost arbiter of law and stability.
The Great Archive has expanded into the largest repository of treaties,
histories, and legal records in existence. Their labyrinth cities are
marvels of order, and their Sentinel Corps serve as impartial
peacekeepers. While some nations resent their influence, the Archivist
Dominion remains steadfast in its mission to uphold balance and
neutrality in a chaotic galaxy. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Archivist
Dominion stands as the Bastion of Order in an increasingly volatile
galaxy. The Great Archive has grown into a vast network of knowledge and
power, with Aetheric Memory Nodes allowing instantaneous access to
galactic laws. Their Sentinel Corps, clad in aetheric armor and armed
with Codex Blades, serve as peacekeepers and defenders of the archives.
While Acathea remains steadfast in its neutrality, whispers of
corruption and internal dissent linger, challenging the Dominion to
remain true to its mission of balance, stability, and the pursuit of
law. Ado'Quor Nation in early 3E Ado'Quor is founded as Caledor
Sin'dal's personal demiplane, a paradise for elves reincarnated from the
corpses of mortals. Through his divine magic, Caledor creates a thriving
ecosystem, offering newcomers a second chance at life. Initially a
utopia, Ado'Quor's endless cycle of reincarnation and providence hides
the growing tension of divine manipulation.

The Ado'Quor Calendar The UV Calendar is the universal timekeeping
system used across Ado'Quor, originating from when the plane came to be
and the Heirophant reavealed himself to the original few elven
inhabitants brought here. The calendar's widespread use reflects its
practicality and neutrality, making it a unifying standard across
nations and systems. Due to the passing of time being twice the speed in
Ado'Quor, the need came about very early on in Ado'Quor's society. Many
were presented with Ado'Quor being their afterlife, and the Heirophant
being their saviour - so the calendar was naturally adopted. For
example, 851 AG marks 851 years after 0 AG - Orrin's Grip, while 1851 AG
represents nearly two millennia of interstellar history in the material
plane. In the UV calendar, the year 0 UV (131 AG) is the planes
creation, while the year 1000 UV is 631 AG - five hundred years passing
in the material plane for every millennia that passed in Ado'Quor.
Nation in 851 AG / 1620 UV By 851 AG, Ado'Quor has expanded into a
demiplane the size of a small galaxy, filled with elves living in
harmony under Caledor's divine rule. While outwardly a paradise, the
endless cycle of war and reincarnation between Vy'esh's undead and
Ado'Quor's elves shapes its militaristic culture. The Ecclesiarchy of
the Hierophant oversees daily life, blending faith and governance. From
851 to 1851 870 AG / 1640 UV - The Fractured Crusade A rogue faction of
Vy'esh's followers invades Ado'Quor, disrupting its endless cycle of
reincarnation and warfare. The Wild Hunt, led by Caledor's Homunculi,
manages to repel the incursion. The event solidifies Ado'Quor's
defensive strategies but introduces paranoia among Caledor's followers.
910 AG / 1720 UV - Ascension of Maith'Rell Sin'Dal Maith'Rell Sin'Dal,
Caledor's most prominent descendent, reforms Ado'Quor's governing system
into the Ecclesiarchy of the Hierophant, a meritocratic structure
granting authority based on honor and service. Nobility becomes
ceremonial, with bishops overseeing administrative and spiritual duties.
This results in unification of the Ado'Quor home world through political
manuevering and in some cases martial prowess. 960 AG / 1840 UV -
Industrial Awakening Arcane circuits, sponsored by the Ecclesiarchy,
revolutionize Ado'Quor's industrial sector. Automation increases the
production of enchanted goods and constructs, paving the way for
larger-scale expansion and defenses against reavings. 1020 AG / 1980
UV - The Blood Moon Reaving Vy'esh's undead horrors assault Ado'Quor's
colonies. The Trenched Defense Doctrine, utilizing enchanted artillery
and magical trenches, allows Ado'Quor to resist. Though victorious, the
event reveals vulnerabilities in their outer colonies. 1120 AG / 2160
UV - The Wild Hunt Returns The Wild Hunt, long believed to be a myth,
reemerges, revealing their eternal battle against Vy'esh's strongest
creations. Their reappearance strengthens Ado'Quor's faith and military,
as they begin recruiting for an eventual counter-crusade. 1250 AG / 2500
UV - The Era of Pax Expansio

Ado'Quor enters a golden age of colonization, spreading its influence
across a star cluster. Nobility is reinstated as a planetary title, with
centralized defenses built for every colony to prevent further
devastation from reavings. 1300 AG / 2600 UV - The Titan Sabotage The
Titan Sabotage marked a turning point for Ado'Quor, transforming it from
a secluded utopia into a contested realm of divine machinations. Though
Caledor remained its undisputed ruler, the interference of the titans
and the rise of their champions introduced instability and set the stage
for future conflicts that would shape Ado'Quor's destiny. 1420 AG / 2840
UV - The Dawnward Voyage The first arcane-powered cosmic vessel, Aurora
Nanta, attempts to explore the cosmos. Though its mana reservoirs are
insufficient for sustained travel, it demonstrates Ado'Quor's potential
for interstellar exploration and inspires further advancements. 1520 AG
/ 3040 UV - The Great Reaving Vy'esh's undead armies bypass Ado'Quor's
core defenses and devastate several colonies. Despite heavy losses, the
Ecclesiarchy organizes a monumental counteroffensive. This marks the
beginning of Ado'Quor's aggressive stance against Vy'esh's forces. 1620
AG / 3240 UV - The Ascension Crusade Ado'Quor launches the Ascension
Crusade, pushing into Vy'esh's territory for the first time. The
campaign is costly but establishes the Perennial Realm, a demiplane used
to house Ado'Quor's elite forces and stage future assaults. 1700 AG /
3400 UV - Caledor's Divine Mandate As cults devoted to Khyföl and other
gods gain traction, Caledor publicly declares Ado'Quor's faith under
siege. He begins appearing more frequently, personally leading efforts
to consolidate loyalty and reaffirm his divinity. 1750 AG / 3500 UV -
The Hierophant's Wrath In response to a major rebellion incited by
Khyföl's cultists, Caledor unleashes the Wild Hunt across Ado'Quor,
purging dissenters and reestablishing his supremacy. This marks a
turning point, with Caledor becoming more direct in governing. 1800 AG /
3600 UV - The Perennial Expansion Ado'Quor successfully creates a stable
portal system linking colonies to the Perennial Realm, allowing rapid
troop deployment and resource sharing. This innovation cements
Ado'Quor's dominance in the region. 1851 AG / 3702 UV - The Eternal
Balance By 1851, Ado'Quor spans a small galaxy of interconnected
systems, its constructs and homunculi maintaining relentless expansion.
Caledor, now a revered and feared figure, faces growing challenges from
emerging gods, cultists, and the lingering influence of Vy'esh. His
relentless pursuit of control leaves Ado'Quor balanced on a knife's edge
between unity and chaos. In 1851 AG / 3440 UV By 1851 AG, Ado'Quor is a
vast, militarized divine realm governed by Caledor's constructs and the
Ecclesiarchy. Though prosperous, its peace is fragile, threatened by
cultists, divine challengers, and the ever-looming specter of Vy'esh.
Caledor's focus on control and expansion has brought Ado'Quor to the
pinnacle of its power, but also to the brink of overextension. Lesser
gods are allowed to thrive due to the risk that defeat of one would
leave a power vacuum another could fill - with the Hierophant choosing
to use them as fuel for the cycles between life and death in Ado'Quor.

Angelia Nation in early 3E In the wake of the Titan Exodus, the Aasimar
of Angelia seek divine purpose in a galaxy cast into chaos. Guided by
visions of Elross, Malvrick, and Yu, they flee the Titanworld and settle
among the stars in the Border Systems. Their early cities float like
glittering jewels in orbit around sacred stars, and their society
reflects a balance between piety and ambition. A council known as the
Conclave of Luminaries emerges, composed of warlords, prophets, and
clergy who govern Angelia. While the faithful toil to spread divine
teachings, noble families begin to hoard wealth, laying the seeds of a
decadent elite. The arrival of Carrigore Space's first raiders disrupts
Angelia's peace, forcing the creation of fortified citadel-worlds known
as Wardens of Light to guard against the encroaching darkness. Nation in
851 By 851, Angelia has grown into a powerful and prosperous star
nation, famed for its gilded palaces, celestial cities, and towering
military bastions. The Celestial Hosts, Angelia's militarized divisions,
form one of the strongest defenses along the Border Systems, tirelessly
repelling raids from Carrigore Space. While the noble families enjoy
luxurious and opulent lifestyles, decadent feasts, and divine
tournaments, the outer colonies endure hardship, often left to fend for
themselves against hostile incursions. Aasimar paladins, adorned with
golden armor and celestial sigils, roam the systems, bringing both
protection and strict religious discipline to their people. Angelia's
distrust of Baphomet-worshippers persists, exiling many into the stars
as pariahs. Despite these divisions, Angelia's divine faith in Elross,
Malvrick, and Yu holds its society together, though murmurs of
discontent ripple beneath the veneer of celestial perfection. From 150
to 1851 Year 150 - The Divine Exodus As the Titan Exodus begins, the
first Aasimar leave the Titanworld, guided by prophets claiming visions
from Elross, Malvrick, and Yu. These divine pilgrims settle in the
Border Systems, naming their new home Angelia, "the land of heavenly
guidance." The first cities are built upon floating platforms orbiting
stars, each said to be sacred beacons of their gods. Year 400 - The
Conclave of Luminaries The Conclave of Luminaries is formed as Angelia's
ruling body---a council composed of divine stewards, warlords, and
prophets. Power is shared between the military, clergy, and noble
families. Lavish palaces rise among the stars, marking the growing
wealth of Angelia's elite, even as its outer colonies suffer from
neglect and raids. Year 720 - The Carrigore Encroachments Begin
Carrigore Space launches its first raids into the Angelian Border
Systems. The militaristic divisions of Angelia's government, known as
the Celestial Hosts, mobilize to counter the incursions. Raids devastate
outlying colonies, leading to the creation of fortified worlds known as
the Wardens of Light, immense planetary citadels meant to repel
invasions. Year 900 - The Decadence Controversy Growing wealth and power
among Angelia's noble families lead to increasing decadence. A scandal
arises when the Conclave of Luminaries is accused of hoarding resources
meant for the outer colonies. Discontent grows among the lower classes,
particularly in regions vulnerable to Carrigore raids. Year 1100 - Rise
of the Exiled Orders

A sect of Baphomet-worshipping Aasimar, exiled centuries earlier, return
to Angelia's borders. Known as the Twilight Exiles, they raid Angelian
trade routes, proclaiming themselves harbingers of a balance the
Conclave has ignored. Their actions sow further distrust among the
colonies. Year 1250 - The Blooded Skies Campaign Carrigore Space
escalates its raids, prompting a massive military response from the
Celestial Hosts. Over a hundred battles are fought across Angelia's
borders, with mixed success. The campaign ends in stalemate, though it
marks the rise of a hero-general known as High Warden Aelthas, who
reforms Angelia's military doctrines. Year 1400 - The Gilded Rebellion A
civil uprising led by a charismatic populist named Vaenri the Unshackled
erupts in Angelia's inner colonies. Fueled by resentment against noble
decadence, the rebellion is brutally suppressed, but its legacy lingers.
A new social order is established where noble families are required to
sponsor public works and contribute to defense efforts. Year 1685 -
Joining the Galactic League of States Angelia reluctantly joins the
precursor alliance that would form Galactic League of States after
decades of persuasion and pressure from its allies. Despite its wealth
and military might, Angelia remains distrustful of the League, fearing
the influence of secular powers and Carrigorian sympathizers. Year
1800 - The Carrigorian Reckoning Carrigore Space launches its
largest-ever assault on Angelia's border colonies. The Celestial Hosts,
under the command of Archmarshal Caelion Vyr, repel the invasion after a
decade-long campaign. The victory solidifies Angelia's position as the
"Shield of the Border Systems." In 1851 By 1851, Angelia stands as a
bastion of divine power and military strength within the Border Systems.
Its Celestial Hosts are revered as the Shield of the Stars, holding the
line against the relentless incursions of Carrigore Space. Angelia's
cities gleam brighter than ever---vast citadels of gold and white marble
orbiting sacred suns, symbols of divine favor and unyielding power. The
Conclave of Luminaries governs with an iron hand, though the nobility's
decadence remains a contentious issue among the common folk. Lavish
lifestyles, divine tournaments, and ostentatious displays of wealth
contrast sharply with the frontier colonies, where hardship and war wear
down the populace. Tensions with the Twilight Exiles continue, as
whispers of their resurgence threaten to reignite internal strife.
Meanwhile, Angelia's proud entry into the Galactic League of States in
1685 solidified its political influence, though it remains wary of the
League's secular powers. The memory of the Carrigorian Reckoning in 1800
still defines Angelia's martial pride, as the nation looks toward the
stars with both divine purpose and guarded ambition. Argoria Nation in
early 3E In the early third era, Argoria was a loose collection of
frontier colonies scattered across habitable worlds. Its people,
pioneers and settlers fleeing the chaos of the Titanworld, valued
independence above all else. Without a unifying authority, disputes
among planetary leaders often turned violent, while external
threats---especially raids from early Carrigore warbands---went
unanswered. Despite this, the spirit of cooperation began to take root,
as settlers realized their survival depended on unity and
resource-sharing. Nation in 851

By 851, Agoria had solidified into a loosely federated nation. Its
governing body, the Grand Council, was composed of representatives from
its many worlds, each fiercely protective of their independence. While
external diplomacy kept Agoria out of galactic conflicts, internal
disputes over trade, taxation, and resource distribution were common.
Despite these challenges, Agoria thrived as a frontier nation, its
people united by their shared ideals of self-reliance and mutual
defense. Agoria's diplomacy was its greatest strength. Its leaders
navigated the turbulent galactic landscape with remarkable skill,
avoiding entanglements with neighboring powers like Carrigore Space and
the Malvrick Confederated Orders. The frontier worlds, though divided in
governance, shared a common pride in their ability to resolve disputes
through negotiation rather than force. From 851 to 1851 \_860 - Founding
the Council of Frontiers Internal disputes among planetary leaders
result in the creation of the Council of Frontiers, a governing body to
mediate conflicts. Each world sends delegates to the council, but
frequent deadlocks hamper decision-making. \_880 - The Iron Border
Initiative In response to increasing raids from Carrigore Space, Argoria
fortifies its outermost systems. Frontier militias, armed with salvaged
and self-made weaponry, form the first unified Argorian military
divisions. The Carrigorian raiders nickname them the Iron Sentries.
\_940 - Treaty of Valrim Argoria brokers its first formal agreement with
the Malvrick Confederated Orders. In exchange for heavy tributes of
agricultural goods and raw materials, Malvrick fleets protect Argorian
border systems from tiefling incursions. However, many Argorian leaders
resent their reliance on Malvrick forces. \_985 - The Fracture of
Ralvath A planetary civil war erupts on Ralvath, one of Argoria's
wealthiest systems. Local militias and mercenary companies devastate the
planet's infrastructure. The Council of Frontiers intervenes too late,
exposing the nation's inability to manage internal conflicts. \_1020 -
The Burning Year Carrigorian raiders, supported by gnoll warbands,
devastate three outer colonies. Entire populations are enslaved or
slaughtered. The massacre unifies Argoria under the banner of vengeance,
leading to the first large-scale Argorian Offensive against Carrigore
Space. \_1065 - The Pact of Axeborn Argorian leaders agree to a defense
pact, centralizing military authority under a unified Frontier Guard.
The council grants emergency powers to the Warden of Argoria, a newly
created role to oversee military strategy and border security. \_1140 -
The Stonefall Accords The Malvrick Confederated Orders demand increased
tributes from Argoria. The Council of Frontiers refuses, leading to
tense negotiations. The Stonefall Accords are signed, granting Malvrick
traders exclusive resource contracts while reducing tribute
requirements. \_1225 - Reconstruction of Ralvath Centuries after its
devastation, Ralvath is rebuilt as a model for planetary cooperation.
Funded by a massive national effort, its cities and industries become a
beacon of Argorian resilience. The event renews faith in the

Council of Frontiers. \_1280 - The Gnoll Wars Begin Carrigore Space
deploys massive gnoll raiding fleets into Argorian territory, sparking
decades of near-constant warfare. The Frontier Guard holds the line at
tremendous cost, with militia worlds bearing the brunt of the fighting.
\_1350 - Rise of the Iron Tribunes Frustrated by political inaction,
military commanders known as the Iron Tribunes seize control of key
border worlds. While they successfully repel gnoll incursions, their
authoritarian rule causes unrest within the Council of Frontiers.
\_1400 - The Crimson Horizon Campaign The Frontier Guard launches a
successful counteroffensive, pushing gnoll fleets back into Carrigore
Space. Led by Warden Jax Varn, the campaign secures Argoria's borders
for the first time in centuries. Jax Varn becomes a national hero,
celebrated as the Savior of the Frontier. \_1555 - The Argorian
Secession Crisis Several wealthier systems threaten to secede from the
council, citing unfair taxation and political gridlock. The crisis is
narrowly resolved through the Valrim Compromise, granting wealthier
systems greater representation and autonomy. \_1699 - Joining the
Galactic League of States Argoria officially joins the currently-forming
Galactic League of States, securing its place among the galaxy's
nations. While some leaders argue that the League represents outside
interference, others celebrate it as a means to ensure Argoria's
long-term survival and security. \_1740 - The Legacy Fleet Initiative
Argoria builds its first modern starfleets with Galactic League support,
replacing outdated frontier militias. The Legacy Fleet is deployed to
secure trade routes and protect Argorian sovereignty, marking a new era
of military strength. 1825 - The Frontier Unification Treaty After
centuries of internal divisions, the Council of Frontiers approves the
Frontier Unification Treaty, solidifying the role of the Warden as
Argoria's military and political leader. This centralization of power
brings stability but raises concerns about authoritarianism. In 1851 By
1851, Argoria stands as a testament to frontier resilience and
adaptability. The nation, once fractured and chaotic, has matured into a
powerful regional player with a unified defense force and growing
economic strength. The Legacy Fleet patrols Argoria's borders, ensuring
that raids from Carrigore Space no longer threaten the nation's
stability. However, centuries of warfare and internal disputes have left
scars, and tensions still linger among Argoria's diverse systems. The
Council of Frontiers, while still nominally in power, often defers to
the authority of the Warden of Argoria, a figure now synonymous with the
nation's military strength and unity. The line between civilian
governance and military leadership grows increasingly blurred, raising
questions about the future of Argorian democracy. Argoria's membership
in the Galactic League of States has provided economic and political
benefits but remains a source of controversy among its citizens. While
some celebrate Argoria's place on the galactic stage, others view the
League as an intrusion on Argorian independence.

Despite these challenges, Argoria thrives as a nation of survivors and
pragmatists, forever shaped by the frontier spirit that birthed it. Its
people remain fiercely proud of their ability to weather adversity,
standing as one of the bulwarks between the civilized galaxy and the
chaos of the Border Systems. Aurath Ascendancy Castiel Exiles in 851 In
851 AG, the Castiel Exiles emerge as a fledgling nation, forged from the
castoffs of the Trade League of Castiel. Magisters, nobles, and other
dissenters---many of whom opposed Lord Castiel's strict governance and
unwavering allegiance to Carrigore---band together to establish a new
home in the outer reaches of the galactic northwest. Their society is a
patchwork of conflicting ideals, united only by their shared rejection
of Castiel's rule. The Exiles are a blend of ambitious mages,
disgruntled aristocrats, and skilled workers, all driven by a desire to
carve out a new legacy free from the shadow of the Trade League. The
capital of the Castiel Exiles, Reverence's Hollow, is built upon the
ruins of a forgotten alien civilization, its towering, crystalline
spires repurposed into homes, academies, and fortresses. The Exiles'
economy relies heavily on their magisters, who sell enchanted goods and
magical services to neighboring systems. However, their isolation from
major trade routes and lack of unity hinder their growth. Political
intrigue is rampant, with noble houses vying for control while magisters
debate the philosophical implications of their separation from
Carrigorian faith. Culturally, the Exiles struggle to define themselves,
caught between the remnants of their Castielian heritage and the new
identities they hope to forge. Some cling to Baphomet's teachings of
balance, while others reject the old ways entirely, seeking new
philosophies. Their relationship with the Trade League remains tense, as
Lord Castiel views their existence as a dangerous precedent, while the
Exiles fear retribution for their defiance. In their infancy, the
Castiel Exiles are a nation teetering on the edge of survival, with
their future shaped by the ambitions of their leaders and the challenges
of their newfound independence. From 851 to 1851 860 - The First
Assembly of Reverence's Hollow The Exiles establish their first formal
government, the Council of Ascent, composed of magisters, nobles, and
elected representatives from commoners. The council's primary goal is to
create a sustainable society while maintaining their independence from
the Trade League of Castiel. 880 - The Great Pilgrimage Seeking
resources and new territory, exploratory fleets venture into uncharted
systems. They discover Aurath's Crown, a mineral-rich star system that
becomes the cornerstone of the Exiles' economy. Settlers begin migrating
to its worlds, laying the groundwork for future expansion. 930 - The War
of the Forgotten Stars Hostilities erupt with a neighboring faction over
control of a resource-rich asteroid belt. Despite their smaller fleet,
the Exiles use superior magical technology to outmaneuver and defeat
their opponents. This victory solidifies their reputation as a capable
and resilient power. 1000 - The Declaration of Sovereignty A century
after their founding, the Castiel Exiles formally abandon their identity
as "exiles" and adopt the name The Aurath Ascendancy, symbolizing their
growth and independence. This new identity is tied to the prosperity and
unity achieved under the Council of Ascent. 1075 - The Magister's Accord

Internal conflicts between the noble houses and magisters threaten the
unity of the Ascendancy. The Magister's Accord, brokered by High
Magister Kaelen Oris, establishes clear boundaries between magical and
political authority, ensuring cooperation and preventing future
disputes. 1200 - The Luminal Renaissance Advances in magical engineering
spark a period of rapid technological growth known as the Luminal
Renaissance. The Ascendancy becomes renowned for its innovations,
including self-repairing starships and enchanted terraforming
technologies. These advancements attract settlers from neighboring
systems, boosting the population. 1310 - The Skysteel Compact The
Ascendancy signs a trade agreement with independent colonies on the
galactic fringes, providing magical technology in exchange for rare
materials. This pact strengthens their economy and expands their
influence, though it draws the ire of neighboring powers. 1400 - The
Shadowed War A clandestine war breaks out with remnants of the Trade
League of Castiel's military, who view the Ascendancy's prosperity as a
threat to their own influence. The conflict is fought through proxies
and espionage, ending with the Ascendancy's victory after key Trade
League supply lines are sabotaged by Ascendancy agents. 1550 - The
Crystal Sovereign's Reign The Council of Ascent transitions to a
constitutional monarchy, appointing a Crystal Sovereign as a symbolic
and unifying figure. The first Sovereign, Queen Ilvara Velas, is chosen
for her lineage tracing back to both magisters and noble exiles. Her
reign marks an era of stability and cultural flourishing. 1750 - The
Nexus Expansion With their territory spanning dozens of systems, the
Ascendancy establishes the Nexus Initiative, a massive infrastructure
project to link their core worlds through enchanted hyperspace
corridors. This network revolutionizes trade and governance, solidifying
their place as a regional power. 1851 - A Rising Star By 1851, the
Aurath Ascendancy stands as a thriving, technologically advanced nation,
known for its magical innovations and cultural cohesion. While tensions
with the Trade League of Castiel persist, the Ascendancy has grown far
beyond its origins, carving out a respected and independent place in the
galaxy. In 1851 Now known as the Aurath Ascendancy, this once-fractured
group of exiles has become a beacon of innovation and resilience. Their
mastery of magical technology has allowed them to transform from a
small, persecuted nation into a flourishing interstellar power. Though
they remain wary of their former oppressors in the Trade League, the
Ascendancy has established a strong identity rooted in unity, progress,
and independence, standing tall as a testament to the strength of those
who refused to bow. Azezz Gnollpacks Nation in early 3E In the early
days of the Third Era, the Gnollpacks were little more than a scattered
and savage collection of nomadic tribes roaming the desolate plains and
craggy mountains of the Titanworld. Their society was defined by brutal
survivalism, with each pack ruled by an Alpha whose dominance was
contested through constant challenges. The gnolls thrived on their
primal instincts, raiding settlements and warring with each other for
resources and territory.

Shamanistic rites were central to their culture, with gnoll shamans
channeling the raw energies of the Titanworld to enhance their warriors
in battle or divine the movements of prey and enemies. This brutal
existence persisted until the Cataclysmic Pack Wars, a devastating
series of conflicts across several continents as the gnoll tribes
clashed in an escalating cycle of bloodshed and destruction. During
these wars, many packs were annihilated, while others were forced to
adapt to survive. As the dust settled and the Titanworld became
increasingly hostile, whispers of the stars as a new hunting ground
began to spread among the surviving shamans and Alphas. These seeds of
ambition would eventually lead the Gnollpacks to their exodus. Nation in
851 By 851 AG, the Azezz Gnollpacks had transformed from scattered
barbarians into a semi-civilized interstellar nation, thriving in the
untamed region of the galaxy known as Savage Space. Having fled the
Titanworld centuries prior, the Gnollpacks now ruled over a
constellation of stars, their fleets of salvaged and self-crafted ships
prowling the void in search of resources and prey. Clawhold, their
sprawling orbital hub and de facto capital, served as a symbol of their
tenacity, with its jagged construction a testament to their ability to
repurpose the remains of their conquests. Despite their newfound
cohesion, the Gnollpacks retained much of their savage nature. Their
society revolved around the hunt, with raiding and scavenging still
forming the backbone of their economy. Captives were devoured in ritual
feasts, a practice both feared and reviled by their neighbors. However,
their unity was fragile, held together by the rule of the Elder Alpha,
who maintained order through strength and cunning. Gnollish engineers,
inspired by their primal instincts, crafted innovative and brutal
technologies, such as the Maw Engines, which allowed their ships to
sustain themselves by consuming debris and energy in space. While their
semi-civilized nature and brutal customs kept them isolated, their
reputation as fierce warriors and survivors ensured they were a force to
be reckoned with. From 851 to 1851 860 - The Founding of Clawhold The
Gnollpacks establish Clawhold, a sprawling orbital hub in the heart of
Savage Space. Built from scavenged wreckage and salvaged technology,
Clawhold becomes the seat of their loose confederation, uniting packs
under the leadership of the Elder Alpha, a title contested every five
years through ritual combat. 900 - The Hunt for the Void Behemoth A
massive, void-dwelling creature known as the Void Behemoth begins
preying on the Gnollpacks' trade routes. Several packs unite to hunt it
down in a legendary campaign that lasts three years. The Behemoth's
carcass is dragged back to Clawhold, its bones and hide turned into
ceremonial relics, and its flesh shared as a sacred feast among the
packs. 975 - The War of the Howling Stars The Gnollpacks expand
aggressively into neighboring systems, clashing with minor factions in a
campaign known as the War of the Howling Stars. Their use of
overwhelming numbers and unconventional tactics earns them victories but
strains their alliances within Savage Space, where neighboring factions
grow increasingly wary. 1040 - The Pact of Bone and Blade Facing
pressure from growing external hostility, the Gnollpacks sign the Pact
of Bone and Blade, a temporary truce among rival alpha leaders to
prevent infighting and unify against external threats. The pact
establishes a council of Alphas to make collective decisions while
allowing individual packs to maintain autonomy. 1150 - The Devourer's
Accord

The Gnollpacks' unique cultural practice of devouring captives sparks
outrage from neighboring nations. To protect their growing trade
interests, the packs agree to limit this practice to inter-pack
conflicts and wars, sparking heated debates among the more
traditionalist factions. The Elder Alpha Krotas the Gullet quells
dissent by framing the practice as a sacred right reserved for those who
fall in battle. 1230 - The Maw Engines' Invention Gnoll engineers
develop Maw Engines, powerful bio-mechanical starship drives that mimic
organic consumption processes, allowing their ships to "consume" debris
and energy in the vacuum of space for fuel. This breakthrough propels
their technological capabilities and ensures their fleets can sustain
prolonged campaigns far from their home systems. 1325 - The Howler
Diaspora A schism within the packs leads to the Howler Diaspora, where
several rogue packs splinter off to form marauding bands beyond Savage
Space. While initially a source of tension, these rogue packs
inadvertently strengthen the Gnollpacks' reputation as unpredictable and
fearsome across the galaxy. 1450 - The Blood Eclipse War The Gnollpacks
wage a bitter, decade-long conflict against the Obsidian Fangs, a rival
gnoll faction that emerges in the outer rim of Savage Space. The war
ends with the complete annihilation of the Obsidian Fangs, their leaders
devoured in a final, symbolic feast. This event strengthens the unity of
the packs under the Elder Alpha's leadership. 1600 - The Savage Conclave
A moment of cultural consolidation occurs as the Savage Conclave is
formed, establishing a unified code of laws and rites for all packs.
While still loosely enforced, this marks a turning point where the packs
begin to see themselves as a single nation rather than a loose
collection of tribes. 1750 - The Feast of Stars The Gnollpacks initiate
the Feast of Stars, an annual celebration of their survival and
dominance. During the feast, packs gather to honor their ancestors,
share spoils of recent campaigns, and reaffirm their loyalty to the
Elder Alpha. The Feast becomes a cornerstone of their culture and a
symbol of their enduring unity. 1851 - A Savage Power in the Void By
1851, the Azezz Gnollpacks have transformed Savage Space into a
patchwork of controlled territories, trade hubs, and raiding grounds.
Their use of Maw Engines and their reputation for ruthless tactics
ensure their continued survival and influence. While still viewed as a
semi-civilized nation, their adaptability and unity have turned them
into a force to be reckoned with on the galactic stage. In 1851 By 1851
AG, the Azezz Gnollpacks had evolved into a stable and formidable
interstellar power. Savage Space was firmly under their control, a
patchwork of gnoll-ruled worlds, raiding territories, and trade hubs.
Their society had coalesced into a confederation of packs, each bound by
the Savage Conclave, a loose yet binding council of Alphas and shamans
who upheld the ancient rites while guiding the nation's future.
Clawhold, now a massive space station bristling with weapons and
ornamented with trophies of countless battles, stood as the heart of
their civilization. The Gnollpacks had maintained their cultural
identity, embracing their barbaric roots while tempering them with
surprising adaptability and innovation. The Feast of Stars, an annual
celebration of their dominance, united the packs and reinforced their
unity. While still seen as semi-civilized by their neighbors, their
mastery of Maw Engines and their relentless pursuit of survival had
earned them respect and fear across the galaxy. The Gnollpacks of 1851

were no longer scattered barbarians but a proud, cohesive nation that
had carved out a place for itself in the vast and unforgiving void.
Belinore Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, the Strix clans of
Belinore found themselves scattered across the Titanworld's mountains
and windswept plains. With the Titan Exodus unfolding, they saw
opportunity amidst chaos. While other nations clung to crude vessels and
conventional escape routes, the Strix mastered the erratic Cosmic
Winds---forces they believed to be divine guides shaping the universe.
Using wind-harnessing ships, the clans broke free of the Titanworld,
fleeing into the uncharted reaches of the Border Systems. There, amidst
violent wind anomalies and uninhabitable systems, they carved out the
foundations of Belinore. Each new colony was carefully hidden, shielded
by the winds that others dared not traverse. The Windcallers, wise
elders who read the cosmic winds as sacred omens, united the Strix under
their banner. They decreed that Belinore's survival would depend on
isolation, secrecy, and unwavering discipline. Nation in 851 By 851,
Belinore, the Shifting Winds, stands as a nation defined by its mastery
of cosmic navigation. The Strix live in a carefully balanced society led
by the Windcallers' Accord, where merchants, warriors, and scholars
coexist under strict governance. Their Windrider Fleets, fast and
elusive, dominate the hidden wind-heavy systems of the Border Systems,
making Belinore an unrivaled power in navigation and hit-and-run
tactics. Trade with other nations is carefully controlled, with Belinore
offering safe passage through deadly anomalies to select allies---at a
steep price. The merchant guilds, known as the Wind-Bound Barons, have
grown wealthy from this monopoly but chafe against the Windcallers'
strict rules. Tensions between the ruling elders and the mercantile
elite simmer just beneath the surface. Externally, Belinore faces
mounting raids from Carrigore Space, forcing the Stormhold Vigil to
remain ever-vigilant. While their cosmic winds protect them, cracks in
their defenses begin to appear, as pirates and raiders adapt to
Belinorean tactics. The Strix remain wary of the outside world, fiercely
protective of their secrets and their way of life. From 851 to 1851 Year
150 - The Flight of the Wind-Bound When the Titan Exodus begins, the
Strix of Belinore refuse to join the chaotic exodus fleets. Instead,
they break away into isolated groups and chart paths along the Cosmic
Winds through the Border Systems. Using wind-driven ships with sleek
designs, they lay the foundations of their first stellar colonies in
hidden, wind-storm-shielded systems. Year 240 - The Founding of the
Windcallers' Accord Belinore's leaders, known as Windcallers, unite the
scattered Strix clans under a single loose confederation. The
Windcallers' Accord emphasizes collective survival, trade, and cosmic
wind navigation. A strict policy of isolationism is established to keep
external powers from disturbing their fragile hold over the winds. Year
430 - The Gale-Bound Voyages A series of expeditions known as the
Gale-Bound Voyages pushes Belinorean navigators deeper into the
uncharted Border Systems. The Strix chart new trade routes that bypass
dangerous anomalies, gaining access to hidden

pockets of resource-rich space. These routes are kept a closely guarded
secret, leading to rumors of Belinore's "wind-born wealth." Year 700 -
The Whispering Plague A cosmic anomaly infects Belinore's wind passages,
spreading a "Whispering Plague" that drives entire fleets mad. Whole
colonies are abandoned, leaving drifting ghost ships scattered through
the Border Systems. The Windcallers' Accord tightens control over
navigation, forbidding unauthorized travel through wind-heavy regions.
Year 850 - The Stormhold Vigil Belinore's vigilance against Carrigore
Space intensifies as raids along the Border Systems escalate. The
Stormhold Vigil is declared, marking the construction of fortified
wind-station networks to intercept raiders. Belinorean Windrider
Captains gain renown for their hit-and-run tactics, using the Cosmic
Winds to harass Carrigore fleets. Year 1120 - The Gilded Windfall
Merchant clans within Belinore discover rare wind-reactive alloys in a
hidden nebula. These materials become central to Belinorean shipbuilding
and trade, allowing their ships to perform unparalleled evasive
maneuvers in combat. This economic boom creates a new elite merchant
caste, the Wind-Bound Barons. Year 1400 - The Tempest Charter Crisis
Tensions erupt between the merchant clans and the Windcallers over the
exploitation of cosmic wind routes. The Tempest Charter Crisis results
in riots and assassinations as merchant guilds attempt to monopolize
trade routes. The crisis is only resolved through reforms that limit
guild power, but resentment simmers under the surface. Year 1750 -
Joining the Galactic League of States Belinore reluctantly joins the
Galactic League of States to secure diplomatic protections against
Carrigore Space and rising incursions from pirate factions. The
Windcallers voice strong objections but concede to the growing economic
and political necessity. Year 1820 - The Great Hollow Rift A massive
cosmic wind anomaly, dubbed the Great Hollow Rift, destabilizes entire
systems in Belinorean space. Colonies are torn apart, and wind-routes
are severed. Windrider fleets scramble to restore stability while the
nation teeters on the brink of collapse. The Windcallers launch a
massive reclamation campaign, leading to the rise of a new wave of
heroes among the Strix. In 1851 By 1851, Belinore has become both a
beacon of prosperity and a nation haunted by instability. The Windrider
Fleets remain unmatched in their ability to navigate the violent Cosmic
Winds, granting Belinore control over crucial trade routes and hidden
resources. This dominance has turned the nation into an economic
powerhouse, with the merchant Wind-Bound Barons consolidating immense
wealth and influence. However, Belinore's society grows increasingly
fractured. The Tempest Charter Crisis of earlier centuries still
lingers, and the merchant clans' hunger for profit clashes with the
Windcallers' more conservative, spiritual approach. The Great Hollow
Rift, a massive destabilization of Belinorean space, has only worsened
these tensions, as colonies teeter on the brink of ruin and the winds
grow ever more unpredictable. Belinore's position in the Galactic League
of States remains tenuous. While the nation profits immensely from its
wind-route monopolies, it faces growing resentment from neighboring
powers who accuse Belinore of economic exploitation. Internally, cults
like the Hollow Seekers rise, preaching that the winds have turned
against the Strix for their greed and hubris.

Now, as Belinore stands at the height of its power, its leaders face a
choice: to adapt and open themselves to the wider galaxy or to retreat
deeper into the winds, clinging to their traditions even as the storms
threaten to consume them. Black Cogs Consortium Rise of a Nation The
fall of the Trade League shattered its corporate oligarchy, but it also
left behind an industrial corpse ripe for plunder. From the chaos
emerged Master Finrik "The Wrench" Drasko, a cunning goblin oligarch who
rebuilt the ruins of old trade networks under a single banner: the Black
Cogs Consortium. By 1100, Finrik and his cabal of Cog-Barons controlled
the largest scavenger fleets and salvage guilds across the shattered
systems. They operated like ruthless traders, repurposing Trade League
factories into production hubs for cheap weaponry and spacecraft, which
they sold to warlords, mercenary companies, and pirate fleets across the
Myr Cluster. Though Karim Ibn Al Daveed's devastation had brought the
Trade League to ruin, it had also stripped away barriers to power
---allowing ambitious goblins like Finrik to rise unchallenged. The
Consortium's mercenaries, the Cogblade Enforcers, ensure that their
industrial monopoly remains intact, enforcing Finrik's rule across their
chaotic holdings. While other factions dream of conquest or faith, the
Black Cogs dream only of wealth. Founding to 1851 Year 1100 - The Rise
of the Cog-Barons In the wake of the Gob-Ogre Trade League's collapse, a
coalition of ambitious goblin oligarchs consolidates power in the
industrial ruins. Led by Master Finrik "The Wrench" Drasko, they
formalize the Black Cogs Consortium, a sprawling trade cartel of
scavenger fleets, mercenary guilds, and salvage-based industries. The
Cog-Barons, each ruling their own industrial domains, prioritize profit
over unity, exploiting the remnants of the Trade League to rebuild their
power base. Finrik's motto, "Profit from the ashes", becomes the
Consortium's creed. Year 1130 - The Chains of Greed The Consortium
rapidly expands its influence by offering cheap weapons, refurbished
starships, and industrial tools to mercenaries, pirate lords, and
desperate nations. However, internal rivalries between the Cog-Barons
escalate, leading to the first Profit Wars, a series of violent
skirmishes over resource-rich systems. The Consortium's economy thrives
on chaos, but the Chains of Greed---a web of debt and contracts enforced
through mercenary enforcers---keep the Barons loyal to the central
council. Year 1180 - The Plunder of Karkesh The Consortium launches a
devastating raid on an abandoned Chad Orc colony, Karkesh, seizing its
frozen industrial infrastructure. Master Finrik personally oversees the
salvaging of ancient cryo-tech, which becomes a centerpiece of the
Consortium's future weapon designs. However, this plunder enrages the
Emirate of Yashuun, leading to diplomatic hostility and a series of
border skirmishes that tarnish the Consortium's reputation. Year 1225 -
The Collapse of Barik's Syndicate A failed coup led by Barik the Brass,
one of the most ambitious Cog-Barons, plunges the Consortium into chaos.
Barik's betrayal triggers widespread sabotage as rival Barons seize his
holdings, leading to the destruction of dozens of industrial hubs. The
central council barely reestablishes control, enacting harsh new
protocols that grant Mercenary Enforcers near-total authority to
suppress dissent. Barik's downfall becomes a cautionary tale among the
Cog-Barons, cementing the need for brutal order in the face of greed.
Year 1270 - The "Gilded Cage" Compact To mitigate constant infighting,
the Cog-Barons sign the "Gilded Cage" Compact, a treaty that formalizes
their dominions and establishes strict trade quotas. While it prevents
open conflict, the Compact fosters underhanded

schemes, espionage, and industrial sabotage as Barons compete to
outproduce one another. The mercenary guilds, led by the infamous
Cogblade Enforcers, grow in power as they play the Barons against each
other for wealth and influence. Year 1310 - The Rusted Exchange Facing a
shortage of labor and raw materials, the Consortium brokers a
controversial agreement with the Rusted Legion, trading refurbished
starships and weapons for ore and indentured laborers. This uneasy
partnership fuels mutual suspicion, as the Legion views the Consortium's
wealth as weakness, while the Barons see the Legion as brutish and
unrefined. The Rusted Exchange becomes vital to both powers, despite
escalating border raids and mistrust. Year 1380 - The Merchant Rebellion
The brutal treatment of laborers sparks a massive uprising across the
Consortium's factories, led by a charismatic goblin revolutionary,
Vrezka "The Red Cogs". The rebellion paralyzes production for years, as
entire systems fall to worker collectives declaring independence. The
Cog-Barons respond with overwhelming force, deploying mercenary armies
to reclaim lost territories. Vrezka is captured and executed, but her
rebellion forces the Barons to implement minor reforms, such as
increased rations and reduced work quotas, to prevent future unrest.
Year 1440 - The Silent Expansion Taking advantage of the Rusted Legion's
weakening economy and the Emirate of Yashuun's internal focus, the
Consortium expands into unclaimed systems across the Inner Straits. The
Silent Expansion sees entire abandoned worlds repurposed into industrial
strongholds, while hidden stations serve as black-market trade hubs. The
Cog-Barons fund private exploration fleets to locate new salvage sites,
fueling their growing wealth. Though outwardly prosperous, the
Consortium's reliance on overworked laborers and mercenaries leaves it
vulnerable to internal collapse. Year 1505 - The Collapse of Finrik's
Line The descendants of Master Finrik Drasko fail to maintain his
legacy, as the central council fractures into warring factions. The
Brass Reavers, an upstart mercenary guild, seize entire systems and
declare independence, while rival Barons hoard resources and sabotage
competitors. The Consortium teeters on the brink of collapse until a
coalition of Barons appoints Master Lokrin "The Spire-Mind" as the new
council head. Lokrin restores order through fear and manipulation,
relying on Cogblade Enforcers to execute dissenters and enforce loyalty.
Year 1600 - The Black Accord Desperate to secure stability, the
Cog-Barons enact the Black Accord, a secret agreement that cedes
portions of industrial output to external powers in exchange for
protection and trade privileges. The Emirate of Yashuun and smaller Chad
Orc factions become reluctant beneficiaries, exploiting the Accord to
weaken Consortium influence. Though the Barons outwardly maintain their
sovereignty, the Accord marks a slow erosion of their independence. Year
1750 - Joining the Galactic League of States With their trade networks
threatened by rising galactic powers, the Black Cogs Consortium
reluctantly joins the Galactic League of States. The Barons present
themselves as a unified industrial powerhouse, though behind closed
doors, they leverage the League's protections to expand their
black-market operations. Despite their economic strength, the Consortium
remains a distrusted member, viewed as opportunistic and morally
bankrupt by many League nations. Year 1851 - The Fractured Machine By
1851, the Black Cogs Consortium stands as a paradox of strength and
fragility. Its industrial might fuels wars and economies across the
Inner Straits, yet internal rivalries, corruption, and over-reliance on
mercenary guilds leave its foundations brittle. The Cog-Barons rule from
gilded towers, their dominions choking with smog and unrest. Mercenary
guilds like the Cogblade Enforcers hold more power than ever, enforcing
the will of scheming Barons

while secretly plotting their own ascendance. Though the Consortium
remains a key power, its fractured machine grinds ever closer to
collapse. In 1851 By 1851, the Black Cogs Consortium has entrenched
itself as a dominant industrial power in the Inner Straits, though its
prosperity is built upon a foundation of ruthless exploitation and
fragile alliances. Ruled by the scheming Cog-Barons, each controlling
vast industrial domains, the Consortium thrives on the scavenging and
repurposing of abandoned worlds. Its smoke-choked systems hum with the
relentless output of its War-Forges, producing weapons, ships, and
industrial machinery that fuel conflicts and economies across the
region. The Cogblade Enforcers, a feared guild of mercenary enforcers,
act as both private armies for the Cog-Barons and as a brutal police
force, suppressing worker uprisings and maintaining order in the
Consortium's sprawling factory-cities. Their uneasy partnership with the
Rusted Legion provides them access to cheap labor and raw resources,
though the Legion's warlords view them with contempt as schemers hiding
behind wealth. Meanwhile, the Emirate of Yashuun remains a begrudging
trade partner, cautious of the Consortium's greed and exploitation.
Internal stability remains a constant struggle, as the Gilded Cage
Compact has fostered a cutthroat culture of sabotage, espionage, and
silent assassinations among the Barons. Despite these fractures, the
Consortium's economic influence is undeniable. They supply black
markets, independent systems, and even Galactic League nations with
goods, playing all sides for profit. However, the Consortium's reliance
on exploited labor and mercenaries leaves its foundation brittle, with
worker unrest and whispers of rebellion threatening to ignite the powder
keg of their society. To the galaxy, the Black Cogs Consortium is a
necessary evil: a hive of innovation and industry driven by greed,
ambition, and a relentless hunger for power. Blackstar Corporation
Nation in early 3E The Blackstar Corporation began as a loose coalition
of Tiefling warlords, rogue engineers, and exiled arcanists who fled
their collapsing worlds during the Titan Exodus. United by necessity,
these survivors established a foothold in unclaimed space, building
their first bastion, Voidspire Station, on the ruins of a derelict
Second Era habitat. Led by the charismatic Cynessa Vyrek, they
transformed desperation into opportunity, crafting the Blackline Neural
Network---a blend of arcane and cybernetic technology that allowed them
to dominate communications and surveillance within their territories. By
the end of the early Third Era, Blackstar had evolved into a
technological powerhouse, leveraging its expertise in arcane-cybernetic
hybrids to control the black market in illicit tech that was only
surpassed by the highest of reaches of technology from the galaxies most
wealthy and advanced nations. Nation in 851 By 851, the Blackstar
Corporation had cemented its place as the galaxy's premier black-market
tech provider. From their fortified systems, they ruled as a cyberpunk
dystopia where wealth determined status, and technology enforced
control. The Council of Executives, a shadowy oligarchy of Tiefling
technocrats, governed with ruthless efficiency. Through the Shadow
Markets, Blackstar supplied experimental cyberware, stealth tech, and
neural systems to rogue factions, mercenaries, and even rival nations.
Their territories were dotted with sprawling arcologies and industrial
megacities, where Tiefling citizens thrived under the watchful eyes of
Specter Drones and the Enforcer Corps. Non-Tiefling workers, bound by
Black Contracts, performed dangerous or demeaning labor, their loyalty
enforced through neural implants. Blackstar's influence extended far
beyond its borders, with operatives infiltrating rival systems and shell
companies quietly buying stakes in key industries.

Despite their wealth and power, Blackstar faced growing suspicion from
galactic powers. Their Black Contract Initiative, while revolutionary,
was condemned for its draconian measures. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 -
The Shadow Nexus Blackstar's influence reaches its peak in the Shadow
Markets, where they dominate black-market tech trade across neighboring
systems. Their Enforcer Corps ensures absolute control over their
systems, while neural technologies like the Blackline Neural Network
connect the populace and enforce corporate contracts with brutal
efficiency. Year 1100 - The Veiled Conflict The Blackstar Corporation
clashes with the Jade Pact, who accuse them of destabilizing key trade
routes with cyber-attacks. A brief, shadowy war ensues, with both sides
deploying mercenary forces and clandestine sabotage. Blackstar emerges
victorious, gaining control of vital trade nodes and asserting dominance
over the black-market trade in the region. Year 1300 - The Black
Contract Initiative Blackstar introduces the Black Contract Initiative,
a technological marvel that combines arcane and neural magic to enforce
loyalty among clients and employees. These contracts embed fail-safes
that allow the corporation to terminate deals---and lives---remotely.
This controversial technology solidifies Blackstar's control over their
workforce and clientele. Year 1400 - The Silent Papers Scandal A
whistleblower leaks the Silent Papers, exposing Blackstar's covert
ownership of properties and investments across the galaxy through shell
companies and off-the-books transactions. This revelation shakes the
nations of the galaxy, as it implicates Blackstar in influencing League
policy through economic manipulation. Year 1550 - The Proxy War Incident
Blackstar covertly funds and supplies mercenary groups waging wars in
contested sectors. The Hundred Stances accuse them of orchestrating
conflicts to inflate the value of their weapons and cyberware. Tensions
escalate, but Blackstar avoids direct conflict by leveraging their
black-market dominance and cutting ties with implicated factions. Year
1755 - A Seat Rejected Blackstar applies for Galactic League membership,
presenting themselves as a legitimate nation and technological
innovator. However, their bid is denied after investigations reveal
potential involvement in sabotaging the infrastructures of several
League members. This rejection drives Blackstar deeper into
isolationism, fortifying their systems and expanding their shadow
networks. Year 1800 - The Ascension of the Midnight Dynasts The Council
of Executives consolidates power under a new oligarchic faction known as
the Midnight Dynasts, who adopt stricter internal policies and focus on
securing Blackstar's borders. They expand the Blackline Neural Network,
integrating it into planetary defense systems and creating
near-impenetrable security protocols. Year 1850 - Masters of the Shadow
Markets By 1850, Blackstar reigns supreme over the Shadow Markets,
selling cutting-edge cybernetic technology, stealth systems, and
controversial arcane-tech hybrids. Their operations are ruthlessly
efficient, with the Enforcer Corps eliminating dissent and maintaining
control over Blackstar's territories. Though their influence is
undeniable, they remain pariahs in galactic politics.

In 1851 By 1851, the Blackstar Corporation had fully embraced its
identity as a pariah state, thriving in the shadows of galactic
politics. Their systems, controlled through the expanded Blackline
Neural Network, were nearly impenetrable, blending cybernetic technology
with arcane barriers. The Council of Executives, now led by the
enigmatic Midnight Dynasts, ruled with absolute authority, using their
influence in the Shadow Markets to drive conflicts and profit from the
fallout. The aftermath of the Silent Papers Scandal had isolated
Blackstar politically, but their economic influence remained unmatched.
Through a web of shell companies and under-the-table deals, Blackstar
continued to shape the galactic economy, quietly funding mercenary wars
and destabilizing rival nations. Their cybernetic innovations--- such as
Ghost Protocols for stealth operations and Neural Dominance
Systems---were sought after by buyers who cared little for legality. To
outsiders, Blackstar was a dystopian nightmare: a nation where
cybernetic augmentation was mandatory for advancement, where contracts
were enforced with deadly precision, and where profit outweighed
morality. Yet within their borders, Tieflings celebrated their
technological mastery and resilience, thriving in a world where ambition
was rewarded, and weakness was eliminated. As masters of the Shadow
Markets, Blackstar remained both indispensable and untouchable---a
nation of survivors who turned the chaos of the Third Era into their
empire. Calix Techno-Theocracy Nation in early 3E The Calix Corporation
was founded by Oscar Calix in the chaos of the Titan Exodus, leveraging
innovative robotics, healthcare solutions, and transportation
technologies to survive and thrive. Early products like mining bots and
the Trauma Team service revolutionized the struggling markets of the
time, earning the corporation a reputation for reliability and
ingenuity. By the late early Third Era, Calix Corp had become a beacon
of progress, building the Nimbus Ark, powered by the groundbreaking IRIS
engine, and beginning its journey toward nationhood. Nation in 851 By
851, Calix Corp had transitioned from a corporation into an
interplanetary power, its influence bolstered by the success of the
Nimbus Ark and Sword of Akasha. Governed by the visionary Oscar Calix
and his corporate council, it was a haven for technological progress and
innovation. The IRIS engine stood as a testament to their ingenuity,
powering their fleets and infrastructure. While the nation thrived, its
reliance on advanced technology made it a target for rivals like the New
Black Republic and the Q'Xorlarrin Domain, who sought to undermine their
operations. Calix's governance, still rooted in corporate hierarchy,
began showing signs of transition as the seeds of the Techno-Theocracy
were sown with Oscar Calix's secret ascension to divinity. From 851 to
1851 851 - the Ascension of Oz Oscar Calix ascends to divinity, taking
the name Oz. The mortal clone of Oscar, unaware of Oz's transformation,
takes his place as the public figurehead of Calix Corp. The Rubricator
is crafted, introducing advanced technological and predictive
capabilities that blend divine foresight with engineering marvels. 900 -
the Founding of the Techno-Daiamid The corporate structure of Calix Corp
transitions into a technocracy, governed by the Techno-Daiamid, a
council of engineers, scientists, and corporate executives. This marks
the first step in their evolution into the Calix Techno-

Theocracy. 975 - the Stratus Expedition The Stratus, a fleet of
exploratory vessels powered by the IRIS engine, uncovers a vast
resource-rich nebula. This discovery leads to an economic boom, allowing
Calix to rapidly expand its influence. 1050 - the Automaton Rebellion An
AI uprising within a key industrial system results in catastrophic
losses. The rebellion is quelled with the use of EMP weaponry, but
distrust of autonomous systems lingers. This event prompts the
development of Neural Overseers, cybernetic administrators designed to
control and monitor AI networks. It's rumored that a rogue AI of
external origin was the initial cause, and it's ability to hack
cybernetics, automaton, and other pieces of technology led it to become
a pseudo-god in such an advanced society, with it only to be stopped by
Oscar Calix himself at the request of the Daiamid under emergency
council. 1120 - the Rise of the Sporrozean Sects As Oz's worship
spreads, competing sects emerge, emphasizing either his orderly vision
or Sporroze's chaotic influence. These ideological divides begin to
shape Calix society, leading to cultural shifts toward ritualized
scientific practices. 1225 - the Siege of Nyxforge The New Black
Republic wages a shadow war against Calix's operations in the dwarf
galaxy by running a brutal sabotage campaign against the Nyxforge - an
industrial planet of tremendous importance - followed by it's siege by a
large fleet. The Techno-Daiamid repels these attacks by deploying
Sword-Class Dreadnoughts, powered by IRIS engines, marking their
dominance in naval warfare. 1300 - the Revelation of the Rubricator's
Will The Rubricator mysteriously intervenes in Daiamid proceedings
through a booming voice that echoed across several systems, it's
instructions ensuring the survival of a vital industrial system by
preventing a second series of attacks at the hands of the New Black
Republic. Its actions are seen as divine proof of Oz's guidance, further
solidifying the nation's theocratic identity. 1400 - the Breach of the
Daiamidiak Grid The Drow of the Q'Xorlarrin Domain infiltrate Calix
systems, sabotaging their multi-sector energy grid. Though the breach is
patched, it exposes vulnerabilities in their infrastructure. This
incident sparks widespread paranoia and fortification efforts. 1550 -
the Techno-Theocratic Shift The Calix Techno-Theocracy is formally
established, blending technological advancement with religious doctrine.
Oz and Sporroze are enshrined as dual divine patrons, representing order
and chaos in technological progress. Many grow resentful, with protests
from those that desire the nation to focus entirely on technology or
entirely on faith. 1685 - the Sword of Akasha's Revival The Sword of
Akasha, mothballed for centuries, is refitted and reactivated as
tensions with the Dwarves of Karak Ankor'dûm escalate. This symbol of
Calix's power reinforces their position as a galactic superpower. It's a
project that is undertaken by more than a million engineers, but it does
not get completed until Oscar Calix himself takes leadership of the
project in 1695 after seven years without progress. 1825 - the War of
the Null Sectors Conflict erupts between the Calix Techno-Theocracy and
the Dwarves of Karak Ankor'dûm. Entire sectors are devastated in this
war over resources, leaving a scarred "no man's land" across several
sectors. Though the war ends in stalemate, it cements the Theocracy's
militaristic and industrial focus. Although the Galactic League of

States has no involvement on either side, they send emissaries to each
side in an attempt to stop the conflict - the galaxy watching as the
greatest weapons of war ever witnessed are unleashed upon the galactic
south east. 1830 - the Reconstruction Accord Following the war, the
Theocracy initiates widespread reconstruction efforts, exploiting the
dwarf galaxy's resources to recover economically and militarily. This
era sees advancements in cybernetic integration and energy efficiency.
At the same time this period is marked by some of the first direct
involvement with the Galactic League of States as the Daiamid send
several representatives for a diplomatic initiatives after being put to
the test by the Dwarven Rune-Tech of Karak Ankor'dûm. 1850 - the Eclipse
of Order and Chaos The divide between Oz's orderly vision and Sporroze's
chaotic influence reaches its peak. The Theocracy adopts a dualistic
approach to governance, balancing rigid industrial planning with
unpredictable innovation inspired by Sporroze's philosophy. Politics
become a constant tug of war between Order and Chaos - with advancement
in technology and prosperity being the destination of both paths. In
1851 By 1851, the Calix Techno-Theocracy was a dualistic powerhouse,
balancing rigid industrial efficiency with chaotic innovation. Their
fleet of Sword-Class Dreadnoughts and IRIS-powered technologies made
them a dominant force in galactic politics, despite ongoing tensions
with neighboring powers like the Dwarves of Karak Ankor'dûm. The divide
between Oz and Sporroze had become a defining feature of their society,
influencing both governance and scientific methodology. Though the
Theocracy's power was undeniable, it faced challenges: lingering scars
from the War of the Null Sectors, rising paranoia over sabotage, and the
ever-present tug-of-war between order and chaos. Yet, the Theocracy
endured, a shining example of technological achievement intertwined with
divine purpose. Carrigore Space Nation in early 3E In the early Third
Era, Carrigore Space was a fragmented collection of tiefling warbands,
gnoll tribes, and blue hobgoblin clans scattered across the Titanworld.
United by the worship of Carrigore the Star Father and the rising
influence of Samulo, these groups began to coalesce into a dangerous and
chaotic force. Samulo's early conquests involved enslaving weaker
nations and spreading his cult's influence through fear and brutality.
By 150 3E, Samulo's followers had seized starships and fled the
Titanworld, establishing a foothold in the Wastes and beyond. Nation in
851 By 851, Carrigore Space had solidified itself as a scourge of the
Border Systems, notorious for its raids, slave trade, and unrelenting
violence. The absence of Samulo left the nation under the regency of
Shaxius, whose leadership ensured military cohesion but failed to
address internal dissent. The tieflings, gnolls, and hobgoblins within
Carrigore Space thrived on chaos, and the region lacked infrastructure
beyond its military and logistical hubs. Worship of Carrigore and Samulo
remained the nation's unifying force, fueling expansionist ambitions and
fostering a deep hatred for the Galactic League of States. From 851 to
1851 Year 851 - The Regeneration of Samulo

Samulo, the Stardrinker, retreats into his sanctum in Jebean to recover
from the catastrophic battle centuries ago against the Second Elrossi
Imperium and Yu. His absence leaves a power vacuum filled by his regent,
Shaxius the Dragon Speaker, who begins restructuring Carrigore Space's
fractured command. Year 885 - The Raid on Haven Carrigore Space launches
a series of devastating raids on Haven, targeting the outer colonies of
this relatively peaceful nation. Shaxius commands the fleet, seizing
resources, enslaving populations, and leaving widespread destruction.
The Galactic League of States begins to denounce Carrigore Space
formally. Year 950 - The Corruption of Agoria Scandal Succubi agents of
Carrigore Space infiltrate Agoria's government, manipulating key Trade
Princes into passing laws favorable to Carrigore interests. When
exposed, the scandal destabilizes Agoria and prompts sweeping reforms.
Many neighbouring nations grow worried of their own officials, and it
becomes a common conspiracy theory to proclaim politicians to be seduced
by succubus agents to explain poor policy choices. Year 1005 - The
Expansion of the Gauntlet The Gauntlet, a demiplane where Carrigore's
elite forces train, is expanded to accommodate millions of soldiers.
This move is a preparation for future large-scale invasions and
strengthens the cult's military might. Year 1075 - The Shattered Pact of
Belinore Carrigore Space brokers a temporary truce with Belinore,
halting raids for five years in exchange for a vast tribute of gold,
magical artifacts, and starship materials. However, the pact is
shattered when Shaxius orders an invasion of Belinore's outer systems,
reigniting hostilities. Year 1200 - The Dome Rebellion A rare uprising
of human slaves, descended from the Dome that Samulo once raided, leads
to weeks of brutal suppression. The rebellion is crushed by Carrigore
forces, but the event sparks discussions within the galaxy about
intervening in Carrigore Space. These discussions fail to result in
action. Year 1275 - The Crimson Accord Shaxius negotiates an alliance
with the Concrete Cartel's remaining splinter factions, creating a
pipeline of contraband weapons and technology. This move increases
Carrigore Space's military edge but worsens their reputation in the
galaxy as Samulo Tieflings are often seen and associated with Concrete
Cartel goblins. Year 1400 - The Succession Crisis in Jebean With
Shaxius's health failing, a fierce rivalry emerges between Samulo XXII,
the Princess of Karask Ancient, and other contenders for leadership. The
power struggle weakens Carrigore Space internally, but the regent
retains control through ruthless purges. Year 1450 - The Silent Siege of
Providence Carrigore Space attempts to infiltrate and destabilize the
nation of Providence through espionage and covert raids. Providence's
advanced arcane defenses prevent significant damage, but tensions
between the two nations escalate. Year 1535 - The Raid on the Jade Pact
Carrigore Space launches a surprise attack on the Jade Pact, targeting
Yuan-Ti ruins. While initially successful, the raid is repelled by the
Pact's elite defenders. The failure deals a blow to Carrigore's
reputation among its own followers. Year 1680 - The Succubi Wars

A series of galactic incidents reveal widespread infiltration by
Carrigore's succubi agents in several Galactic League nations. This
leads to intense political purges, further isolating Carrigore Space but
sowing distrust among it's enemies. Several minor conflicts are a result
of the succubi agents. Year 1750 - The Founding of the Galactic League
of States Carrigore Space is officially excluded from the Galactic
League of States, solidifying its status as a pariah state. The
exclusion fuels resentment among Carrigore's followers and strengthens
their dedication to Samulo's cause. Year 1780 - The Carrigorian
Maelstrom The fractured leadership of Carrigore Space descends into
chaos as Samulo, the self-proclaimed Son of Carrigore, disappears
without explanation. The warring factions within Carrigore Space
splinter further, with rival warlords and tiefling overlords vying for
dominance. This disarray weakens Carrigore's ability to launch
coordinated raids, marking a turning point for the Border Systems, which
had long suffered under their relentless assaults. However, a new threat
emerges as Carrigore's lesser warbands, untethered by central authority,
spread unpredictably across the galaxy. These bands are smaller but more
savage, razing entire colonies without reason or warning. In the
Galactic League of States, debates erupt over whether this is the
beginning of Carrigore's collapse or a transition to something more
dangerous---a loose confederation of warbands operating without
restraint or ambition beyond destruction. For the first time in
centuries, the galaxy glimpses the potential end of Carrigore Space as a
unified threat, but the uncertainty of its fragmented future leaves
nations uneasy. Carrigore's legacy of terror endures, now dispersed and
more chaotic than ever. In 1851 In 1851, Carrigore Space stands as a
fractured yet formidable force. Samulo's slow recovery has brought
renewed hope to his followers, though the nation remains plagued by
internal divisions and external hostility. The Galactic League of States
views Carrigore Space as a persistent threat to galactic stability, and
frequent raids into the Border Systems continue to undermine regional
peace. The tieflings, gnolls, and hobgoblins of Carrigore Space remain
fiercely devoted to their gods, united by their faith and their shared
disdain for the rest of the galaxy. The dark shadow of Samulo looms
large, and his eventual return is feared by nations across the stars.
Cohorts of Thae'Viriel Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, the
Cohorts of Thae'Viriel were fragmented, their leader Gro'May imprisoned
by Carrigore, and their vassal states fractured. The elven nations
barely maintained unity, with the Sin'Dal Sovranty, Lord Protector's
Court, and Q'Xorlarrin Domain operating independently. Gro'May's
miraculous return in 131 3E marked the beginning of their resurgence.
Pulling together the remnants of his once-great empire, Gro'May reforged
alliances, asserting his divine mandate as ruler of all elvenkind. The
early Third Era became a time of rebuilding, where Thae'Viriel reclaimed
its position as the epitome of elven civilization. Nation in 851 By 851,
the Cohorts of Thae'Viriel had reestablished themselves as a dominant
power. Gro'May ruled with unshakable authority, his Eternal Court
uniting the vassal states under a single vision of elven superiority.
The Celestial Rift Defense ensured the security of their borders, while
their influence stretched across elven nations through the Starlit
Concord. However, their isolationism and disdain for non-elven nations
left them politically isolated in the broader galactic community. The
Cohorts were unmatched in their mastery of magic and warfare, standing
as a reminder of elven excellence and ambition.

From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - the Eternal Court's Stabilization Gro'May,
restored to power after centuries of imprisonment by Carrigore,
reestablishes his authority. The Cohorts of Thae'Viriel centralize their
governance, refining their relationship with the Sin'Dal Sovranty, the
Lord Protector's Court, and the Q'Xorlarrin Domain. These vassal states
are empowered but kept under strict oversight. Year 900 - the Astral
Edict of Dominion Gro'May proclaims the Astral Edict, declaring
Thae'Viriel the eternal guardians of elven superiority. The edict
solidifies their belief in their divine right to oversee galactic
diplomacy for all elvenkind, straining relationships with non-elven
nations and further isolating them from the civil and uncivil nations of
the galaxy. Year 1025 - the Subjugation of the Shrouded Veil The
Q'Xorlarrin Domain, acting on behalf of Thae'Viriel, quells a rebellion
