in the shadowy Shrouded Veil, a rogue drow faction that sought
independence. The Lord Protector's Court provides military support,
demonstrating the strength of Thae'Viriel's alliance structure. Year
1150 - the Celestial Rift War Carrigore Space raids escalate near
Thae'Viriel territory. Gro'May orchestrates a combined defense involving
all vassal states, culminating in the Battle of the Celestial Rift,
where Thae'Viriel forces repel Carrigorian incursions. The victory
solidifies their dominance along the Border Systems but at great cost.
Year 1250 - the Starlit Concord Thae'Viriel negotiates the Starlit
Concord, a diplomatic pact with independent elven nations and enclaves
across the galaxy. The Concord positions Thae'Viriel as the de facto
leader of elven diplomacy, though the outsiders viewed it as an
isolationist move. Year 1400 - the Fracture of Sin'Dal Internal discord
erupts in the Sin'Dal Sovranty, as powerful mages clash over the
influence of Caledor Sin'Dal's descendants. Gro'May intervenes directly,
reasserting control but leaving the Sovranty weakened. Year 1500 - the
Eternal Purge Gro'May enacts the Eternal Purge, targeting corruption and
dissent within the Cohorts. The Lord Protector's Court carries out mass
purges, ensuring unwavering loyalty. This event sparks whispers of
rebellion among the Sin'Dal Sovranty and Q'Xorlarrin Domain. Year 1600 -
the Luminal Ascent Thae'Viriel unveils the Luminal Citadel, a monumental
floating fortress powered by advanced elven magic. It becomes a symbol
of their eternal vigilance and divine superiority. Year 1720 - Refusal
to Join the Galactic League of States Thae'Viriel declines an invitation
to join the Galactic League's precursor alliance, citing its
leadership's inferiority and lack of respect for elven sovereignty.
Instead, they establish direct diplomatic channels to exert influence
over League policies. Year 1750 - the Reclamation of Starreach The
Cohorts reclaim Starreach, a sacred elven artifact lost during the
Second Era. The Q'Xorlarrin Domain leads the operation, using espionage
and subterfuge to recover it from Carrigore Space. Year 1800 - the
Midnight Accord with Sin'Dal

To prevent further discord, Gro'May brokers the Midnight Accord,
granting the Sin'Dal Sovranty autonomy in magical governance while
maintaining overall loyalty. This ensures stability within the vassal
state. Year 1850 - the Eternal Supremacy Declared Gro'May proclaims the
Eternal Supremacy, a declaration reinforcing elven dominance in galactic
affairs. Thae'Viriel remains steadfast in their vision of guiding the
galaxy, standing apart as a beacon of elven excellence. At this point,
the Galactic League of States recognized the elven systems as a
competitor in galactic affairs. In 1851 By 1851, the Cohorts of
Thae'Viriel had reached the pinnacle of their power. Gro'May, the
Eternal King, ruled from the Luminal Citadel, a symbol of elven mastery
and divine favor. The vassal states, though autonomous in certain
matters, remained loyal, their combined strength ensuring Thae'Viriel's
dominance in the galaxy's elven affairs. Thae'Viriel's refusal to join
the Galactic League of States underscored their belief in their
superiority, though they maintained direct influence over League
policies. Militarily, the Lord Protector's Court patrolled their borders
with unmatched discipline, while the Q'Xorlarrin Domain acted as the
shadowy hand of elven intelligence. Internally, the Eternal Supremacy
declared by Gro'May reaffirmed their divine mandate to guide the galaxy.
While their isolationist policies and elitism garnered resentment, none
could deny the Cohorts of Thae'Viriel their place as the preeminent
elven power in the stars. Coldiron Caliphate Nation in early 3E In the
early Third Era, the Coldiron Caliphate was a burgeoning theocracy
centered on the worship of Frozt, the Ice God. Their capital,
Alceldoria, was a radiant hub of creativity and religious devotion,
where artisans and scholars worked to unify Froztan teachings with
technological advancement. The Frostforge, a legendary divine forge,
produced enchanted weapons and tools, cementing the Caliphate's
reputation as a rising power. However, the nation faced constant
challenges from internal divisions and external threats, setting the
stage for centuries of struggle and adaptation. Nation in 851 By 851,
the Coldiron Caliphate had solidified itself as a formidable theocratic
empire. With Caliph Muammar Mohammed Abu Minyar Al-David at the helm,
the Caliphate embarked on a bold campaign to claim the Myr Clusters.
Frozt, mysteriously revived, guided the Caliphate's zealots, inspiring
their warriors and strengthening their faith. Early victories brought
swift conquests, but resistance from the Warrens---a ratfolk nation led
by Skrit Grimweave---and other factions soon bogged down the jihad in a
prolonged and costly conflict. Within the Caliphate's borders,
Alceldoria shone as a beacon of faith and innovation. The Frostforge
produced relics that empowered the Caliphate's fleets, and Frozt's
presence reinvigorated religious fervor. However, tensions simmered as
rival clerics debated Frozt's revival and the jihad's escalating costs.
The seeds of future challenges were sown even as the Caliphate basked in
its golden age. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - the Proclamation of the Myr
Cluster Jihad Caliph Muammar Mohammed Abu Minyar Al-David declares a
holy jihad to claim the Myr Clusters, calling upon Froztan zeal and
military might to subjugate the fractured region. Early victories see
the Caliphate sweep through

minor systems, overwhelming their defenses with disciplined ice-powered
fleets and fervent Frostblades. Year 915 - the Siege of Chitterholm The
Caliphate's forces besiege the Warrens' capital, Chitterholm, aiming to
cripple the ratfolk's resistance. However, Skrit Grimweave leads a
daring counteroffensive, driving back the Frostblades and forcing the
Caliphate into retreat. This marks the turning point of the war - and
the death of the Caliph. Year 995 - the Storming of Shatterpeak The
Caliphate's ambitions in the Myr Clusters end with their decisive defeat
at the Storming of Shatterpeak, where their supply lines crumble under
coordinated strikes by the Warrens and local allies. The retreat
solidifies the Warrens as an independent power and marks the conclusion
of the jihad. Year 1150 - the Founding of the Frostclad Assembly After
years of internal strife following their Myr Cluster defeat, the
Caliphate establishes the Frostclad Assembly, a council of clerics and
military leaders tasked with advising the Caliph. This marks a shift
toward shared governance and a renewed focus on religious orthodoxy.
Year 1350 - the Vanishing of Frozt Frozt mysteriously disappears while
visiting Alceldoria, the Caliphate's capital. This event, known as the
Frostfade, plunges the Caliphate into spiritual uncertainty, with
clerics struggling to interpret the god's departure. Year 1400 - the
Cryo-Forged Revival In response to their waning influence, the Caliphate
embarks on a technological renaissance, blending their Froztan
traditions with salvaged Second Era relics. This era sees the
development of Cryo-Forges, machines capable of imbuing weapons and
armor with frost magic. Year 1500 - the Winterveil Pact The Caliphate
signs the Winterveil Pact, a defensive alliance with several smaller
nations threatened by the Chad Orc Emirates. Though politically
advantageous, this alliance deepens the divide between the Caliphate and
their Froztan kin. Year 1750 - the Caliphate's Declaration of Apostasy
The Caliphate officially declares the Chad Orc Emirates and the
Sultanate of El'Rûs as apostates, accusing them of corrupting Frozt's
teachings. This leads to increased hostility and sporadic border
skirmishes. This also causes brief political drama as the Caliphate is
accepted into the League of Galactic States in the same year. Year
1850 - the Frostclad Restoration By 1850, the Caliphate experiences a
cultural and economic revival under Caliph Suleiman al-Qadr, who
strengthens religious orthodoxy and renews Alceldoria's status as a
beacon of creativity. The Frostforge is reactivated, producing artifacts
that inspire both devotion and innovation. In 1851 By 1851, the Coldiron
Caliphate had reasserted itself as a thriving theocracy. Under the
leadership of Caliph Suleiman al-Qadr, Alceldoria once again became a
beacon of creativity, where scholars and artisans revived the Frostforge
to produce relics that blended tradition with innovation. The
Caliphate's Frostclad Assembly ensured stability, balancing military
ambitions with economic and spiritual needs. However, the Caliphate
remained embroiled in tensions with its Froztan neighbors, the Chad Orc
Emirates and the Sultanate of El'Rûs, whom they viewed as heretics.
Despite these conflicts, the Caliphate's cultural and

technological advancements secured its place as a formidable power in
the galaxy, a nation that carried the legacy of Frozt into a new age of
devotion and progress. Concrete Cartel Nation in early 3E In the early
years of the Third Era, the Concrete Cartel was confined to the
sprawling concrete ruins on a small remote continent of the Titanworld,
a once-thriving cityscape reduced to a desolate wasteland of towering
grey monoliths. These remnants of the Old Concrete Jungle, located on
the continent of Varrack, became the cradle of their survival and
resurgence. Among the first waves of settlers clawing for survival from
Titanworld, the cartel thrived through their mastery of salvage and
ingenuity, repurposing the ancient ruins into fortresses, marketplaces,
and smuggling hubs as they left the Titanworld for the galaxy around
them. Their grey-skinned goblin population, uniquely adapted to the
harsh environment, experienced a significant population boom, driven by
the resourcefulness of their culture and their control over the limited
resources within the ruins. The cartel's early society revolved around
scavenging ancient technology, reengineering it into weapons, ships, and
tools to fuel their growing operations. By consolidating power through
ruthless efficiency and unshakable cultural unity, the Concrete Cartel
laid the groundwork for its leap into the galactic stage, becoming a
formidable force among the early waves of interstellar expansion. Nation
in 851 By 851 AG, the Concrete Cartel had become a formidable galactic
power, rivaling nations like New Cauldron and Pillwake. Its reach
spanned multiple star systems, and its economy thrived on smuggling,
illicit trade, and shadowy alliances. The cartel's capital, still known
as the Concrete Jungle, had transformed into a sprawling urban dystopia,
where skyscrapers of salvaged metal and reinforced concrete symbolized
its industrial strength. Governed by a powerful council of syndicate
leaders, the cartel maintained its unity through fear and ambition, with
a centralized leadership enforcing loyalty among its member factions.
Despite its criminal origins, the cartel's economic and military power
granted it de facto recognition among other nations, though many viewed
it as a dangerous and unpredictable neighbor. Escalating tensions with
Pillwake and increasing scrutiny from New Cauldron hinted at challenges
ahead, but in 851, the Concrete Cartel stood as a symbol of goblin
tenacity and a testament to the potential of organized crime turned
galactic governance. From 851 to 1851 Year 870 - The Ashen Compact In an
effort to counter New Cauldron's intelligence operations, the Concrete
Cartel forms the Ashen Compact, a coalition of smaller syndicates united
under a singular leadership council. The Compact brings temporary
stability but creates power struggles among rival syndicate leaders.
Year 910 - The War of Silver Knives A betrayal within the Ashen Compact
leads to an intense three-year civil war known as the War of Silver
Knives. The conflict cripples the cartel's leadership, leaving its
operations vulnerable to raids by New Cauldron intelligence agents and
Pillwake opportunists. Year 980 - The Pillwake Gambit Pillwake launches
a coordinated assault on the cartel's smuggling routes, seizing key
assets and territories. The Concrete Cartel retaliates, but their
outdated ships and weaponry fail to match Pillwake's growing
technological edge. This sparks a decade-long conflict along their
southern border. Year 1025 - The Grey Conscription

To replenish dwindling manpower, the cartel forcibly recruits thousands
of young goblins into their ranks, a policy dubbed the Grey
Conscription. While this temporarily boosts their workforce, it also
sows dissent among the population, with many seeing it as a betrayal of
goblin independence. Year 1070 - The Shattered Spire Incident A failed
assassination attempt on a prominent Pillwake leader results in
devastating retaliation. Pillwake agents destroy the Shattered Spire, a
key orbital hub of the cartel, killing tens of thousands. This
humiliation weakens the cartel's reputation and sparks unrest within
their territory. Year 1135 - The Concrete Rebellion A faction within the
cartel's grey-skinned goblin population, led by the charismatic rebel
Jorgo Slate, demands reforms and better living conditions. When their
demands are ignored, they incite a widespread rebellion. The Concrete
Rebellion fractures cartel-controlled worlds, with many defecting to New
Cauldron for protection. Year 1180 - The End of the Ashen Compact The
Ashen Compact collapses under the weight of internal corruption and
external pressures. Smaller syndicates emerge from the chaos, fighting
for dominance but lacking the resources to maintain control over cartel
territory. Year 1250 - The Burning Veil Campaign New Cauldron launches
the Burning Veil Campaign, a covert operation targeting the remnants of
the cartel. With key smuggling routes exposed and syndicate leaders
assassinated, the cartel's influence is diminished further, leaving its
borders in disarray. Year 1310 - The Red Fang Accord Desperate for
survival, several splinter groups within the cartel align with Pillwake
in a controversial agreement known as the Red Fang Accord. While it
provides short-term security, this alliance turns many goblin leaders
against each other, further destabilizing the cartel. Year 1400 - The
Concrete Collapse By the turn of the century, the Concrete Cartel's
once-expansive territory is reduced to scattered enclaves. Many of these
regions fall under the control of independent warlords or are annexed by
Pillwake and New Cauldron. The core of the cartel's operations, the
Concrete Jungle, becomes a lawless wasteland. Year 1510 - The Rise of
the Slate Syndicate In the aftermath of the collapse, a new goblin
faction known as the Slate Syndicate rises to prominence. Led by Torik
Slate, a descendant of Jorgo Slate, the Syndicate attempts to reclaim
the cartel's legacy but struggles to unite the remaining splinter
groups. Year 1650 - The Goblin Exodus With living conditions
deteriorating, millions of grey-skinned goblins flee cartel-controlled
space, seeking asylum in neighboring nations. Many settle in New
Cauldron, where they become a significant minority population, further
integrating into its society. Year 1750 - The Cartel Remnant By the
formation of the Galactic League of States, the Concrete Cartel is no
longer recognized as a sovereign entity. A few rogue factions persist in
remote areas, but their influence is minimal. The Slate Syndicate
operates as a criminal network, but it lacks the resources to reclaim
its former glory. Year 1851 - A Broken Legacy In 1851, the Concrete
Cartel exists only in fragmented form, with small criminal enclaves
scattered across the galaxy. The grey-skinned goblins, once the backbone
of the cartel, are now a diaspora, with many integrating into

other nations. The Concrete Jungle, once the heart of the cartel, is a
decayed ruin, its broken spires a testament to the rise and fall of the
goblin syndicates. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Concrete Cartel was a
fractured shadow of its former glory, its dominance eroded by centuries
of internal conflict and external pressures. A series of devastating
wars with Pillwake had stripped the cartel of much of its territory,
while relentless intelligence operations by New Cauldron had exposed
smuggling routes and crippled key operations. The once-powerful
syndicate had splintered into smaller factions, with rogue groups
struggling for relevance in the lawless remnants of cartel space. The
Concrete Jungle, once the heart of their power, now lay in ruins, its
decayed spires and broken infrastructure a haunting reminder of the
cartel's rise and fall. The grey-skinned goblin population, once
fiercely united, had largely dispersed across the galaxy, seeking asylum
or new opportunities in neighboring nations. Pillwake controlled much of
the cartel's former territory, and New Cauldron absorbed many of its
displaced citizens, further diminishing the cartel's influence. While
rogue remnants like the Slate Syndicate persisted in remote sectors,
they lacked the resources and cohesion to reclaim the cartel's legacy.
In 1851, the Concrete Cartel's name remained a faint echo of its
past---a cautionary tale of ambition undone by greed, disunity, and the
ruthless calculus of galactic politics. Consortium of Stars Nation in
early 3E In the early Third Era, the Ethereals were scattered nomadic
fleets navigating the Wastes beyond the Titancore - having been gifted
passage through Orrin's Grip by Carrigore himself, albeit for only a
handful of ships. Their existence was one of constant movement,
survival, and servitude to Carrigore and Samulo. Champions like Viscardi
filled their people with thoughts of greatness, the tales of the art of
stardrinking inspiring generations. Often the greatest among them would
immortalizing their knowledge through sacred tomes. Grand Magister
Talaal Orion emerged as a unifying figure, guiding the Ethereals toward
stability as the Wastes grew increasingly dangerous but some claimed he
was a puppet of outsiders. They retained their cultural identity as
nomadic devotees but planted the seeds of a future civilization through
shared knowledge and rituals. Nation in 851 By 851, the Ethereal Nomads,
led by Talaal Orion, had begun to abandon perpetual nomadism in favor of
orbital city-stations and controlled star systems at the Wastes' edge.
Their new government, the Consortium of Stars, brought order and purpose
to their fractured fleets. The Aurora Spire became a hub of worship,
knowledge, and diplomacy among Carrigore-aligned factions. While still
isolationist, their growing stability and control over stellar rituals
earned them a cautious respect in galactic circles. Outsiders viewed
them with skepticism, particularly as rumors of Viscardi's star-drinking
legacy continued to spread. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - Reform of the
Ethereal Nomads Under the leadership of Grand Magister Talaal Orion, the
Ethereal Nomads abandon much of their perpetual movement within the
Wastes. The hardships of traveling through the debris-choked Titancore
have drained resources and morale for centuries. A new government, the
Consortium of Stars, is founded to stabilize the fleets and allow for
settlements in permanent orbital clusters. Basic diplomacy with aligned
outsiders, particularly Carrigore worshippers and the Cult of the
Cosmos, begins to take shape. Year 870 - The First Starport Conclave

The Ethereal Nomads unveil the Aurora Spire, a city-station orbiting a
star system at the edge of the Wastes. It serves as a trade hub and
meeting place for Carrigore-aligned factions. While outsiders are still
restricted, the Aurora Spire becomes the first visible "capital" of the
Nomads. Year 920 - Viscardi's Tomes Rediscovered Ancient Tomes of
Viscardi, detailing the methodologies of stardrinking, are recovered
from the remnants of a forgotten Ethereal fleet adrift in the Wastes.
The tomes are enshrined as relics of worship and power, attracting
cultists and researchers seeking to harness their dangerous knowledge.
Year 980 - The Grand Schism of Magisters A faction within the
Consortium, led by Magister Azael Rai, denounces Talaal Orion's
diplomatic overtures. Claiming the Ethereals must remain pure in their
servitude to Carrigore and Samulo, Rai's fleet splinters away to form
the Nebular Ascendancy, a radical stardrinking order. This schism
creates tension, as Rai's followers destabilize nearby regions in the
Wastes through erratic raids and star manipulation. Year 1100 - The
Confluence Accord Grand Magister Talaal Orion leads negotiations with
Providence and Hundred Stances to establish mutual agreements on trade
routes and avoidance of contested regions. The Accord improves the
Consortium's legitimacy in the galactic east and begins formalizing
their growing influence. Year 1220 - The Eclipse Ascension A magister of
prodigious power successfully replicates Viscardi's Ritual of
Stardrinking and consumes a dying star. This act solidifies the Ethereal
reputation as unparalleled stardrinkers, but it also increases
skepticism among other nations who now fear their abilities. Whispers of
Ethereal star-harvesting operations spread across the Galactic East.
Year 1355 - The Conquest of the Hollow Drift To expand their resource
base, the Consortium seizes control of the Hollow Drift, a derelict
system located between Providence and the Wastes. Its debris fields and
abandoned stations are stripped of valuable materials, fueling the
Nomads' economic and military growth. It becomes a site of combat as
Hobgoblin crews attempt to raid the system for the same resources. Year
1400 - The Great Astral Reformation A new generation of Ethereals under
the leadership of Magister Selora Vaen begins introducing cultural
reforms. While still deeply aligned with Carrigore and Samulo, Selora's
leadership opens more controlled access to Ethereal settlements for
non-believers. Her reforms split traditionalist factions but usher in an
age of scientific advancement. \_Year 1550 - The Emergence of the
Ascended Fleet A splinter faction of star-harvesting Nomads begins
operating in the Devoid Systems, deep within Orrin's Grip. The fleet's
activities are shrouded in secrecy, and reports emerge of stars
vanishing overnight. Many suspect these rogue Ethereals have taken
Viscardi's teachings to dangerous extremes - but it's often dismissed as
rumors of conspiracy theories. Year 1620 - The Starforged Armada The
Consortium completes construction of the Starforged Armada, a fleet of
dreadnought-class warships powered by stardrinker rituals. These ships
cement the Consortium's dominance in the Wastes and nearby sectors,
ensuring no nation dares challenge their sovereignty. Although they are
infamous across much of the galaxy, these ships have never seen combat
against a formidable force. Year 1690 - The Gathering of Magisters The
Ethereals begin covert diplomatic efforts to join the Galactic League of
States, citing their stability and contributions to stellar knowledge.
This move sparks internal conflict, as radical stardrinkers oppose any
attempt to

integrate with non-Carrigore powers. Year 1750 - Joining the Galactic
League of States The Ethereal Nomads formally enter the Galactic League
of States. While their inclusion is met with resistance, it marks a
turning point as the Consortium of Stars adopts galactic diplomacy on a
broader scale. Outsiders remain wary, but the Nomads' advanced
stardrinking technology is seen as both a blessing and a threat. Year
1775 - The Silent Star Controversy It is exposed that Ethereal Magisters
have been harvesting stars in the Devoid Systems for centuries. The
Silent Star Controversy ignites outrage within the League, with
accusations of resource theft and galactic destabilization. While the
Ethereals downplay the situation, their reputation remains tarnished.
\_Year 1851 - The Death of Grand Magister Talaal Orion After centuries
of leadership and unmatched mastery of the Ethereals' arcane arts, Grand
Magister Talaal Orion--- the hero of his people and champion of the Cult
of the Cosmos---meets his end. In a climactic ritual deep within the
Devoid Systems, Talaal Orion attempts to drink a dying star in a
desperate effort to prolong its light and save the Ethereal fleets from
a creeping energy famine. The ritual, once thought perfected by
Viscardi's teachings, proves unstable. The star collapses into itself
prematurely, unleashing a gravitational pulse that shatters surrounding
ships and consumes Talaal Orion's essence in a final, blinding burst of
light. His last words, transmitted across the void in a haunting echo,
are said to have been, "Even in death, I serve the light of Carrigore.
Drink deeply, for power lies beyond the dying flame." The death sends
ripples across the Consortium of Stars. His loss marks the end of an era
and leaves a power vacuum within the Magisterium, the ruling council of
the Ethereals. Some mourn him as a martyr who sought to save his people,
while others question whether his obsession with star-drinking pushed
their race too far into dangerous pursuits. His death sparks renewed
debate over the morality of star-drinking and the Ethereals' alignment
with the Cult of the Cosmos. Talaal Orion's shattered remains are said
to drift eternally within the star's collapsed husk, now a black void
known as The Oracle's Fall. This location becomes a somber pilgrimage
site for Ethereal worshippers of Carrigore, a dark mirror to the glory
of Viscardi's conquests. It also reignites tension within the Galactic
League of States, as neighboring powers demand stricter oversight of the
Consortium's activities in the Devoid Systems. In 1851 By 851, the
Ethereal Nomads, led by Talaal Orion, had begun to abandon perpetual
nomadism in favor of orbital city-stations and controlled star systems
at the Wastes' edge. Their new government, the Consortium of Stars,
brought order and purpose to their fractured fleets. The Aurora Spire
became a hub of worship, knowledge, and diplomacy among
Carrigore-aligned factions. While still isolationist, their growing
stability and control over stellar rituals earned them a cautious
respect in galactic circles. Outsiders viewed them with skepticism,
particularly as rumors of Viscardi's star-drinking legacy continued to
spread. Emirate of Ghurzhem Rise of a Nation By 851, the Chad Orc
warbands that would form the Emirate of Ghurzhem were still under the
banner of Karim Ibn Al Daveed, waging a relentless campaign against the
Stellar Compact. These nomadic warriors, renowned for their frost-imbued
weaponry and ruthless efficiency, dismantled Compact defenses and
claimed industrial hubs. Though bound by Karim's leadership, the
warbands' internal rivalries foreshadowed the struggles that would
define Ghurzhem's early years.

In the chaos following Karim Ibn Al Daveed's conquest of the Stellar
Compact in 885, his Chad Orc warbands settled in the shattered remnants
of the Compact's territory. Initially divided into competing clans,
these warbands waged brutal skirmishes over resources and sacred relics.
The rise of Emir Khalmuk ibn Grashaan, a charismatic warlord and devout
Froztan, marked the turning point. Khalmuk united the clans under a
shared vision of faith and survival, founding the Emirate of Ghurzhem.
The Emirate's early years were marked by struggle. The integration of
salvaged Compact infrastructure into Ghurzhem's cryo-industrial economy
clashed with Froztan traditions. The Steelhand Compact of 900 resolved
these tensions, creating a hybrid society where industrial innovation
served divine principles. By the late early Third Era, Ghurzhem was a
fledgling theocracy, its people driven by faith and resilience in the
face of adversity. Founding to 1851 Year 885 - the Founding of the
Emirate of Ghurzhem In the aftermath of Karim Ibn Al Daveed's campaign
against the Stellar Compact, his Chad Orc warbands settle in the
conquered territories. Amidst infighting and conflicting claims, Emir
Khalmuk ibn Grashaan rises as a unifying figure. Declaring the birth of
the Emirate of Ghurzhem, Khalmuk establishes the Frostwrit, a legal
codex blending Froztan theology with pragmatic governance. The Emirate
sets its capital at Ghur-Zhaal, atop the ruins of the Compact's
industrial hub Forgehold Delta. Year 900 - the Steelhand Compact The
Emirate faces logistical challenges in integrating salvaged Compact
technology into its Froztan society. The Steelhand Compact, an agreement
between Ghurzhem's engineers and spiritual leaders, standardizes the use
of hybrid cryo-industrial systems while ensuring Froztan teachings are
not overshadowed by technological pragmatism. Year 1100 - the War of
Split Frosts Tensions between Ghurzhem's central government and
semi-autonomous Chad Orc clans escalate into civil war. Known as the War
of Split Frosts, this internal conflict stems from disagreements over
the distribution of resources and religious authority. Emir Khaldar ibn
Vrosham restores unity by granting greater autonomy to clan leaders,
creating the Shards of Ghurzhem, a council of clans that advises the
Emir. Year 1200 - the Icebound Accord Faced with growing pressure from
Chad Orc splinter groups and external threats, Ghurzhem signs the
Icebound Accord with the Stellar Compact, establishing a tenuous peace.
The Accord defines borders and permits limited trade of Compact relics
to Ghurzhem in exchange for cryo-industrial resources. This agreement
stabilizes the region but creates tensions within Ghurzhem, as some see
it as compromising Froztan principles. Year 1400 - the Famine of Frozen
Iron A massive collapse of Ghurzhem's cryo-industrial systems triggers
the Famine of Frozen Iron, leaving key territories without functional
infrastructure. Emir Ralzak ibn Kharum enacts sweeping reforms,
including the Frosttithe Mandate, forcing clans to contribute resources
to centralized restoration projects. The Mandate alienates smaller
clans, but the Emirate survives the crisis, albeit weakened. Year 1685 -
Joining the Galactic League of States The Emirate of Ghurzhem joins the
Galactic League of States by joining it's precursor alliance, presenting
itself as a vital trade partner offering cryo-industrial innovations and
skilled warriors. While their membership is accepted, other nations
remain wary of their aggressive history and Chad Orc ambitions. Year
1850 - the Shard Ascendancy The Shard Ascendancy, a period of reform and
decentralization, transforms Ghurzhem into a semi-federal theocracy. The
Shards of Ghurzhem, composed of clan leaders, gain greater influence
over local governance. The Emirate thrives as a patchwork of industrial
hubs, sacred Frostspire temples, and militarized settlements, all united
by a shared devotion to Frozt and loyalty to the Emir.

In 1851 By 1851, the Emirate of Ghurzhem had matured into a semi-federal
theocracy, balancing its Froztan faith with the remnants of Stellar
Compact technology. Under the leadership of the Shard Ascendancy, the
Emirate operates as a patchwork of semi-autonomous clans governed by the
Shards of Ghurzhem, who answer to the Emir. This decentralization has
brought relative stability, allowing Ghurzhem to thrive as both a
military and industrial power. The capital, Ghur-Zhaal, serves as the
Emirate's cultural and industrial heart. Its skyline is dominated by
Frostspire temples and salvaged Compact factories, reflecting the
duality of Ghurzhem's identity. The Frostborn Legions, armed with
cryo-industrial weapons and clad in hybrid armor, remain a formidable
force, patrolling borders and ensuring loyalty. Externally, Ghurzhem is
a respected member of the Galactic League of States, though its
aggressive history and ongoing tensions with the Stellar Compact cast a
shadow over its reputation. Internally, the Emirate is a testament to
Chad Orc resilience and ingenuity, blending ancient Froztan traditions
with salvaged innovation to forge a unique identity in the fractured
galaxy. Emirate of Yashuun Rise of a Nation In the aftermath of the
Trade League's destruction, a prominent Chad Orc warleader, Emir Amjad
ibn Grokshar, led his people to settle the frozen ruins of Shar-Zuun, a
critical industrial world stripped bare by Karim Ibn Al Daveed's forces.
Naming their new state The Emirate of Yashuun, Amjad united the
disparate Chad Orc warbands under the banner of Froztan faith, declaring
their conquest divinely ordained. The Emirate grew rapidly through
discipline, cultural cohesion, and spiritual guidance, as Imams of the
Ice established centers of worship and knowledge. By 1020, Yashuun
became a beacon of stability in Savage Space, carving great Frost
fortresses into the glaciers and establishing cryo-forged cities of
austere beauty. While the legacy of Karim still looms over their frozen
domains, Amjad's vision transformed the destruction into a new cultural
renaissance. Yashuun's warrior elite, the Iceblade Legionnaires, patrol
their borders with an unmatched sense of purpose, ensuring their
enemies---and their own kin---remain loyal to Frozt's will. Founding to
1851 Year 1020 - The Frostbound Dawn The Emirate of Yashuun is formally
established under Emir Amjad ibn Grokshar, who leads his Chad Orc
warbands to settle the frozen remnants of Shar-Zuun, a critical
industrial world abandoned after Karim Ibn Al Daveed's conquest.
Declaring their rise to be Frozt's will, Amjad unites the Chad Orcs into
a disciplined emirate, establishing the Frostfortresses, cryo-carved
cities built to endure the region's harsh environments. Froztan faith
becomes the cornerstone of their culture, and the Emirate begins to
rebuild from the ruins of the Gob-Ogre Trade League. Year 1035 - The
Treaty of Sharqara Seeking stability, the Emirate establishes trade
agreements with the Black Cogs Consortium, exchanging raw resources from
newly claimed worlds for industrial equipment and war supplies. This
alliance allows the Emirate to expand its Frostblade Legions, while the
Consortium profits from a steady stream of ore and frost-forged
minerals. However, some Imams express concern over the Consortium's
unchecked greed, calling it "a bargain with corruption." Year 1070 - The
Ironbound Skirmishes Tensions escalate between the Emirate and the
Rusted Legion as General Irgash the Ironbound begins raiding outer
Emirate mining colonies for labor and materials. Emir Khalid ibn
Sharaqal, Amjad's successor, launches retaliatory strikes, deploying the
Dervishes of Frost---fast-moving cryo-armored ships---to harass the
Legion's war-

fleets. The Ironbound Skirmishes mark the beginning of centuries of
distrust between the two factions, as ideological differences---faith
versus raw conquest---fuel their enmity. Year 1115 - The Frostbound
Pilgrimage Driven by devotion, a large contingent of Chad Orc pilgrims
departs Yashuun to travel to the Froztan holy sites deep within the
Coldiron Caliphate and Border Systems. The pilgrimage becomes an annual
tradition, with pilgrims escorted by Frostblade Legions to protect them
from raiders and mercenaries. This holy migration cements Yashuun's
cultural identity, strengthening ties between clans and ensuring Froztan
teachings remain central to their society. Year 1200 - The War of the
Fractured Foundries The Emirate is drawn into conflict with the Black
Cogs Consortium when several Cog-Barons seize mining colonies claimed by
Yashuun. Emir Razan ibn Thulmar declares war, leading to a decade of
battles over the industrial ruins of Shar-Zuun's outer systems. The War
of the Fractured Foundries ends with the Emirate reclaiming much of the
territory, though at great cost. The resulting peace treaty limits the
Consortium's influence in the Inner Straits but does not sever trade
relations entirely. Year 1285 - The Cryomagi Concord Emir Almar ibn
Yashuun commissions the formation of the Cryomagi Orders,
warrior-priests trained in both Froztan cryomancy and military strategy.
These orders are tasked with defending holy pilgrimages, guarding key
Frostfortresses, and ensuring the teachings of Frozt remain untarnished.
Their creation enhances the Emirate's military strength, providing a
balance between faith and martial discipline as external threats
continue to rise. Year 1340 - The Legion Incursion The Rusted Legion,
under a new warlord-general, launches a large-scale invasion into
Yashuun territory, overwhelming the border colonies with mechanized
forces. Emir Kalim ibn Grokhaal rallies the Frostblade Legions and
Cryomagi Orders to stage a counter-offensive, reclaiming the lost worlds
through brutal attrition and decisive strikes. The Legion Incursion ends
in stalemate, as neither side has the strength to claim total victory.
The Inner Straits remain divided, with hostile borders defined by blood
and frost. Year 1405 - The Silver Frost Edict To rebuild after decades
of war, Emir Shariz ibn Amjad enacts the Silver Frost Edict, encouraging
the development of agriculture, trade, and industry within the Emirate.
The cryo-domed cities expand, and frozen worlds are slowly terraformed
into self-sustaining colonies. The Black Cogs Consortium benefits from
renewed trade agreements, though their growing corruption creates
dissent within Yashuun's councils. Year 1530 - The Frostgate Revolt A
separatist movement led by disillusioned warlords and merchant clans
erupts in the outer systems of the Emirate, threatening to fracture
Yashuun's unity. Emir Tariq ibn Faruk crushes the rebellion with the aid
of the Cryomagi Orders and Frostblade Legions, though the conflict
exposes economic and political cracks within the emirate. The Frostgate
Revolt reinforces the need for stronger central governance and sparks
reforms to empower the Emir's authority. Year 1600 - The Path of Frost
Eternal The Emirate begins expanding deeper into uncharted regions of
the Inner Straits, sending exploration fleets guided by Froztan mystics.
These fleets discover valuable frozen systems and ancient ruins, which
are converted into new colonies and sacred sanctuaries. The Path of
Frost Eternal becomes a cultural doctrine, encouraging Chad Orcs to
embrace both exploration and devotion as twin pillars of their faith.
Year 1750 - Joining the Galactic League of States After decades of
cautious diplomacy, the Emirate of Yashuun reluctantly joins the
Galactic League of States. While some Imams decry the League's secular
leanings, others view it as an opportunity to safeguard Froztan
traditions

while participating in galactic politics. The Emirate is often at odds
with the League's more progressive nations but maintains strong trade
networks and alliances where it benefits their sovereignty. Year 1851 -
The Frostbound Ascendancy By 1851, the Emirate of Yashuun stands as a
beacon of discipline, faith, and resilience in the Inner Straits. Their
Frostblade Legions remain unmatched, their Cryomagi Orders revered, and
their cryo-domed cities thriving amidst frozen landscapes. Though wary
of the Rusted Legion's aggression and the Black Cogs Consortium's
manipulations, the Emirate has solidified its borders and influence.
Pilgrimages to the Coldiron Caliphate and Border Systems continue,
ensuring that Froztan worship binds the Chad Orc clans together, even as
the galaxy beyond grows increasingly unstable. In 1851 By 1851, the
Emirate of Yashuun has cemented itself as a beacon of stability and
devotion in the Inner Straits, a stark contrast to its tumultuous
origins. The frost-carved Frostfortresses dotting frozen planets stand
as symbols of Chad Orc resolve and piety to Frozt, their Ice God. Led by
Emir Zulkar ibn Thashuun, a warrior-scholar revered for his strategic
acumen and unshakable faith, the Emirate maintains firm control over its
core systems while expanding cautiously into the unclaimed frozen
reaches. The Cryomagi Orders, mystic-warriors wielding cryomagic and
honed discipline, patrol holy pilgrimage routes and guard the Froztan
faithful as they travel to sacred sites in the Coldiron Caliphate and
the Border Systems. Their greatest rival remains the Rusted Legion, with
whom they share a contested border scarred by centuries of bitter
skirmishes, but diplomatic efforts have kept the warlords at bay for
now. The Emirate's industrial and agricultural policies---solidified
under the Silver Frost Edict--- ensure self-sufficiency despite the
hostile environments they inhabit. However, internal tensions simmer
among disillusioned merchant clans and younger warlords, who chafe under
the strict religious governance and long for expansion into warmer,
resource-rich systems. The Emirate has also reluctantly embraced its
membership in the Galactic League of States, maintaining its
independence while leveraging trade networks to fortify its defenses and
economy. By balancing faith, discipline, and pragmatism, the Emirate of
Yashuun stands as an enduring power--- one defined by its icy resolve
and unwavering devotion to Frozt. Fire Bishoprics of Octoria Nation in
early 3E In the early Third Era, the Fire Bishoprics of Octoria were a
fledgling nation, born from the Exodus of Flame as a splinter of the
Star Tsars of Zandrov. They settled in the galactic south south-east,
forging their identity through devotion to Core and the rejection of
death magic. Their early years were marked by struggles to unify their
people and establish a foothold in a harsh, lawless region of the
galaxy. The construction of antimagic defenses and the training of their
elite Red Shields laid the foundation for their militarized society.
Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Fire Bishoprics had solidified their role
as a disciplined and militarized theocracy, their faith in Core driving
both their spiritual and military endeavors. Their antimagic
technologies and elite power-armored warriors made them a formidable
force, though their focus on defense left them vulnerable to stagnation.
The Bishoprics maintained a cautious presence in galactic politics, wary
of outside influence but committed to defending their faith and
territory. From 851 to 1851 175 - The Exodus of Flame

Splitting from the Star Tsars of Zandrov, a faction of Core-worshippers
embarks on the Exodus of Flame to establish their own nation in the
galactic east. They name their new territory the Fire Bishoprics of
Octoria, emphasizing their spiritual and military independence. 300 -
The First Flame Conclave The Bishoprics convene the First Flame
Conclave, a gathering of High Bishops and military leaders to codify
their laws and rituals. This conclave establishes the dual leadership of
the Bishoprics: spiritual guidance by the Archbishop of Octoria and
military command by the Flameward Council. 450 - The Siege of Emberhold
Necromantic raiders from a neighboring system invade Emberhold, one of
Octoria's frontier colonies. The siege is broken by the Red Shields, an
elite order of power-armored warriors who use antimagic technology to
suppress the undead. This victory cements the Bishoprics' reputation for
resilience against death magic. 750 - The Sanctification of the Ember
Vaults The Bishoprics expand their antimagic research, creating the
Ember Vaults, a network of underground facilities dedicated to
developing antimagic weaponry and defenses. The vaults become critical
to their military strategy and a source of innovation, but their
construction strains resources and sparks internal disputes over
priorities. 900 - Remembrance Day's Adoption Inspired by the Star Tsars'
tradition, the Bishoprics adopt their own version of Remembrance Day,
honoring Core's protection during the Exodus and the sacrifices made in
defense of their faith. The ceremony becomes a cornerstone of national
identity, fostering unity during times of strife. 940 - Remembrance
Day's Adoption Driven by visions of Core, the Fire Bishoprics launch a
holy war into the uncharted regions of the Void. The crusaders encounter
a hostile "Shadowfire Legion," beings that consume heat and light. The
crusade ends in retreat but not without legend of the "Black Sun," a
colossal entity they awaken. 1000 - The Schism of Cinders A theological
dispute arises within the Bishoprics, with a faction of High Bishops
advocating for a more aggressive approach to spreading Core's faith.
Known as the Schism of Cinders, the conflict is resolved through a
combination of diplomacy and exile of dissenting Bishops, leaving scars
on the Bishoprics' unity. \_1155 - The Purge of Ashbound A schismatic
sect called the Ashbound, who claim Core's flame must destroy all
sentient life to purify creation, seize control of several frontier
worlds. The Bishoprics respond with brutal efficiency, but survivors of
the Ashbound go into hiding and sow chaos for decades. 1400 - Festival
of Offerings Each year, beginning in 1400, the Bishopric orders the
ignition of a gas giant located beyond it's borders. The site of this
event becomes a temporary holy site, and draws pilgrims from across core
worshipping societies in the galaxy. 1550 - Proselytization of the Many
Several worlds across several sectors begin converting, with the upper
classes of society being bribed into it. It is seen by the public as a
waste of funding - as these 'believers' were only after the credits -
but in the decades to come it would bolster the nations numbers and
economy. 1700 - The War of Karak Bakhmut Alongside the Star Tsars, the
Fire Bishoprics launch an invasion of Dwarven space in the War of Karak
Bakhmut. Initially confident, they suffer devastating defeats as the
Dwarves deploy their Rune-mechs and Skyforged

Armada. The Bishoprics lose several key sectors, including Ignis Prime,
a major industrial hub. The losses leave the nation reeling and force
them into a prolonged period of recovery and membership in the Galactic
League of States. 1750 - The Renewal of the Flame Emerging from the
aftermath of the war, the Fire Bishoprics launch the Renewal of the
Flame, a nationwide initiative to rebuild their lost sectors and
strengthen their antimagic infrastructure. Led by the Archbishop and the
Flameward Council, the initiative emphasizes resilience and Core's
blessings, though progress is slow and costly. 1851 - A Nation Rekindled
By 1851, the Fire Bishoprics of Octoria have regained much of their lost
strength but remain a shadow of their former glory. Their military is
highly disciplined, and their antimagic technologies are among the best
in the galaxy, but the scars of past conflicts linger. Cultural
divisions and the challenges of rebuilding keep the Bishoprics stable
but stagnant, with their faith in Core and their determination to endure
serving as the bedrock of their identity. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Fire
Bishoprics of Octoria are a nation slowly rebuilding from the
catastrophic losses of the War of Karak Bakhmut. Their antimagic
expertise and disciplined military remain their greatest strengths, but
internal divisions and the lingering costs of war continue to hinder
their progress. Despite these challenges, the Bishoprics endure, their
devotion to Core and their determination to reclaim their lost glory
guiding them through the trials of a galaxy that tests their faith and
resilience at every turn. Froztantinople Rise of a Nation The icy
wastelands of Galacia became a jewel of resilience, discipline, and
survival under the watchful hand of Yusuf Chad. His relocation of Cold
Town's population and strict governance laid the foundations of a
self-sustaining, unrelenting society of Chad Orcs. Froztanbul rose as a
capital of frozen grandeur, guarded by towering ice statues and dragons
of frost. Early trade agreements, harsh justice against lawlessness, and
Yusuf's push for excavation and arcane mastery created a firm bedrock
upon which the Chad Orcs could thrive. By the time Yusuf vanished in
851, Froztantinople had become a fledgling power with unmatched
determination to tame the frozen Border Systems. Founding to 1851 851 -
Yusuf Chad's Founding of Froztantinople In a final act of leadership,
Yusuf Chad establishes Froztantinople on Galacia, transforming the ice
world into the center of Chad Orc civilization. He relocates the
population of Cold Town, cements trade agreements with key neutral
systems, and imposes harsh measures to maintain order. Froztanbul, the
capital city, becomes a symbol of Yusuf's legacy. Within months, Yusuf
vanishes, leaving behind a carefully structured society poised for
growth. 860 - Frostfang Campaigns To secure mineral-rich ice worlds for
colonization, the Chad Orcs launch a series of lightning raids known as
the Frostfang Campaigns, subjugating small outposts across the Border
Systems. Froztanbul's first governors establish a defensive perimeter
and station roaming ice statues and summoned dragons as planetary
guardians. 875 - The Governor's Rebellion

One of Yusuf Chad's appointed governors, Lorrak the White-Tusk, rebels
against Froztanbul, refusing to tithe his region's mineral wealth. The
rebellion is crushed after a brutal winter siege, and Lorrak's family is
exiled to a nearby frozen moon. The Lorrak Oath is later declared,
ensuring that no Chad Orc governor may rule without the sanction of
Froztanbul's Frostblade Council. 900 - The Frozen Concordat
Froztantinople solidifies trade dominance in the Border Systems with a
renewed agreement called the Frozen Concordat, partnering with the Trade
League of Castiel and ColdIron Caliphate. These trade routes bring
immense wealth to Froztanbul, fueling the expansion of Chad Orc colonies
onto newly discovered glacial worlds. 940 - The Ice Barricades Completed
With frequent raids from Carrigore Space threatening border stability,
Froztantinople completes the Ice Barricades, a vast defense network of
anti-magic shields, planetary cannons, and orbital bunkers. This
transforms their holdings into an impenetrable fortress within the
Border Systems. 985 - Rise of the Frostguard Order The Frostguard, an
elite order of arcane warriors and conjurers loyal to Frozt, is formally
established. The Frostguard train alongside roaming ice dragons, serving
as defenders of Froztanbul and spearheading expeditions to further
colonize ice worlds. Their rise cements Froztantinople as both a
militaristic and religious powerhouse. 1020 - The Frozen March Driven by
a need to secure further resources, the Chad Orcs embark on the Frozen
March, expanding their influence deeper into the Border Systems.
Frost-touched Chad Orc colonists settle harsh, isolated glacial worlds,
creating resilient, self-sufficient communities connected to Froztanbul.
1100 - The Crystal Famine A critical mineral shortage, later called the
Crystal Famine, threatens Froztantinople's growing colonies. Harsh
winters and low yields drive the Chad Orcs to impose heavier extraction
labor on enslaved Dwarves and Duergar. Despite the famine, Froztanbul
emerges with economic dominance intact due to strategic trade. 1250 -
The Frost-Scourge Raids Carrigore raiders launch the Frost-Scourge
Raids, targeting isolated Chad Orc colonies. Froztantinople's leadership
retaliates with devastating counterstrikes, freezing entire enemy fleets
in orbit. This event solidifies the Chad Orc reputation as unyielding
defenders of their frozen domain. 1380 - The Rimeforged Fleet To
strengthen its hold on the Border Systems, Froztantinople commissions
the Rimeforged Fleet, a formidable navy capable of traversing the harsh
cosmic winds. Built from arcane ice and reinforced metals, the fleet
ensures the Chad Orcs' dominance over their isolated holdings. 1455 -
The Frostblade Crisis A succession dispute among the Frostblade Council
erupts into a civil war, lasting five brutal years. A warrior-priest
named Orakhan Frostblade emerges victorious, uniting the clans under a
single banner and codifying the Frostblade Laws, a new hierarchy for
Chad Orc leadership. 1600 - Excavation of the Crystal Core Inspired by
Yusuf Chad's ancient ambitions, Froztanbul authorizes deep-core
excavations on Galacia, discovering vast deposits of Froststone
Crystals. These crystals revolutionize arcane weaponry and planetary
defense systems, fueling technological and military advancements. 1700 -
The Frozen Accord

After decades of expansion, Froztantinople negotiates the Frozen Accord,
formalizing borders with neighboring systems and trading partners. This
act secures their independence and stabilizes the Border Systems,
ushering in a golden age of economic and cultural prosperity. 1780 -
Joining the Galactic League of States Froztantinople becomes a full
member of the Galactic League of States after years of political
negotiation. While initially viewed with distrust due to their harsh
traditions, their economic contributions and steadfast resistance
against Carrigore Space earn them reluctant respect. 1825 - The Eternal
Winter Doctrine Froztantinople's leadership institutes the Eternal
Winter Doctrine, mandating that all new settlements must focus on
self-sufficiency and planetary defense. The doctrine strengthens their
grip on the Border Systems while ensuring no colony falls to external
raids. In 1851 Froztantinople, the Shining Jewel of the Border Systems,
stands as a testament to Chad Orc resilience and the vision of Yusuf
Chad. Over the centuries, the Chad Orcs have transformed the ice worlds
into heavily fortified bastions of civilization, brimming with arcane
wonders and unyielding defenses. The Rimeforged Fleet patrols their
frozen realm, ensuring their colonies remain secure amidst the chaos of
the galaxy. Froztanbul itself glows with radiant Froststone Crystals,
its towering spires reaching skyward as a beacon of Frozt-worship and
Chad Orc pride. Though isolated, Froztantinople commands respect within
the Galactic League of States. They are seen as unbreakable defenders of
their territories and valuable trade partners for arcane crystal
technology. Yet beneath their frozen strength lies an eternal
vigilance---a deep-seated knowledge that the Border Systems are ever
under threat. As cosmic winds howl and raids grow bolder, the Chad Orcs
of Froztantinople stand ready, Frostblade banners unfurled, prepared to
endure for another millennium against the terrors of the Border Systems.
Gob-Ogre Trade League Nation in early 3E Following the exodus from the
Titanworld, goblin and ogre survivors establish industrialized colonies
across the stars. The Fifty Trade Princes, ambitious leaders of
sprawling corporations, consolidate control over labor, resources, and
trade routes. Their factories churn with relentless efficiency, powered
by a downtrodden workforce--- child laborers, underpaid goblin citizens,
and conscripted ogres. This Victorian industrial hierarchy becomes the
backbone of the League's economic might. Oddly enough, this is likely
the only government of the second era to survive Orrin's Grip in 0 AG,
having held a significant trade bloc in the second material plane for
the last few centuries of the second era. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the
Gob-Ogre Trade League stood as a powerful industrial nation, driven by
ruthless trade practices and corporate ambition. Their worlds were
dominated by sprawling factory cities, where goblin workers toiled under
harsh conditions and ogre overseers ensured order. The League entered
the Myr Cluster Wars, seeking dominance over the region's resources.
However, their industrial might would soon face its greatest
challenge--- Karim Ibn Al Daveed, a force of entropy that would bring
ruin to their worlds. From 851 to 1851

300 - The Trade Princes Rise The first Trade Princes emerge among the
goblin clans and ogre enforcers, consolidating power over material
extraction and factory production networks. Each Prince establishes
corporations specializing in resources such as raw minerals, industrial
goods, chemical production, and weapon manufacturing. Trade Princes rule
like oligarchs, maintaining their power through a mixture of brutal
business tactics, corporate armies, and strategic alliances. 400 - The
Ogre Bargain The Ogre Bargain is struck between the goblin
industrialists and the ogre warlords. The ogres become both enforcers
and labor overseers, serving as the muscle behind the goblin-led
corporations. In return, the ogres gain lands, titles, and shares in
corporate profits. This partnership cements the League's economic
dominance but fosters underlying tensions. 600 - The Charter of Fifty
The Charter of Fifty is signed, formalizing the power of the Fifty Trade
Princes who each rule over a corporation. The document codifies their
roles and sets rules for inter-corporate disputes, stock investment, and
inheritance. Princes often own shares in each other's corporations,
ensuring that cooperation and competition remain in a tenuous balance.
850 - The Myr Cluster Wars Begin The Gob-Ogre Trade League becomes one
of the four primary factions involved in the Myr Cluster Wars. The
League enters the conflict for resource acquisition and trade dominance,
funneling massive funds into their corporate armies. This industrial
warfare exhausts their production capabilities, but the Trade Princes
see it as a necessary investment to secure their power. 851 - Karim Ibn
Al Daveed Targets the League Karim Ibn Al Daveed, Drinker of Arcanis,
Embodiment of Entropy, backed by his Chad Orc legions, begins a
relentless campaign of destruction against the Gob-Ogre Trade League.
Entire factory worlds are razed, goblin populations are culled, and
corporate infrastructure is annihilated. Karim's strength and arcane
might overwhelm all opposition, leaving the Trade Princes scrambling to
respond. 880 - The Fall of Grand Kiln Grand Kiln, the League's largest
industrial production world, is obliterated by Karim's forces. Its
destruction sends shockwaves through the League, collapsing supply
chains and destabilizing corporate authority. The ogres begin abandoning
their roles as enforcers, retreating to defend their own strongholds or
aligning with Chad Orc factions. 920 - The Corporate Collapse Infighting
among the Fifty Trade Princes erupts as their investments and powerbases
crumble. Stock manipulations, sabotage, and assassinations plague the
League. Several corporations split into independent factions, while
others fall under Chad Orc control. Goblin workers riot in factory
cities, overwhelmed by famine and desperation. 950 - The Exodus of the
Princes A group of surviving Trade Princes flees to neighboring regions,
abandoning their territories. Some seek asylum in the Stellar Compact,
while others align themselves with new criminal enterprises like
Pillwake. Their once-mighty corporations fragment into smaller
warlord-led enclaves. 980 - The Rise of Chad Orc Warlords The regions
once controlled by the Gob-Ogre Trade League are dominated by Chad Orc
warlords, who establish powerful tribal states. These warlords maintain
some of the industrial complexes and trade routes, repurposing them to
fuel their growing strength. Goblin populations remain under harsh rule,
reduced to indentured workers. 1000 - The Trade League's Remnants

What remains of the Gob-Ogre Trade League are scattered trade enclaves,
industrial ruins, and broken corporate infrastructures. A few goblin-led
mercantile groups survive, desperately clinging to their old titles and
territories. These enclaves engage in scavenging, smuggling, and limited
trade, but none wield the influence of the former League. 1300 - The
Shattered Corporate Accord Several trade enclaves attempt to unify under
the Shattered Corporate Accord, hoping to rebuild a semblance of the
League's former power. Their efforts fail as Chad Orc nations refuse
cooperation, seeing the goblins as weak. The League's remaining
territory remains fragmented and lawless. 1400 - The Industrial Ruins
The regions formerly ruled by the Trade League become industrial
wastelands, their once-great factory worlds stripped of resources. The
Scavenger Guilds rise from the ashes, comprised of goblins and ogres who
salvage remnants of advanced technology to sell to other factions. These
guilds become a minor but persistent presence in the region. 1851 - The
Legacy of the Trade League By 1851, the Gob-Ogre Trade League exists
only as a memory, its once-mighty corporations reduced to ruins and
warlord-ruled enclaves. The nations that rose from its ashes dominate
the region, while the goblin scavenger guilds fight for survival. The
surviving Trade Princes and their descendants cling to fading titles,
but the League's collapse serves as a grim reminder of what happens when
unchecked ambition meets overwhelming power. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the
Gob-Ogre Trade League is no more. The League's fall, brought about by
Karim Ibn Al Daveed's relentless campaign, has left a shattered region
dominated by Chad Orc warlords and scavenger guilds. Goblin enclaves
cling to survival, scavenging what remains of their once-great
industrial empire. The League's collapse is a tale of ambition undone by
hubris and destruction, its legacy lingering as rusting factories and
broken corporations scattered across the Myr Cluster. Great City of the
Rift Rise of a Nation The Great City of the Rift was founded in 851 by
Great Sage Chad, who sought to impose order upon the chaos of the Rift.
With a vision to transform the Rift into a resting place for fallen gods
and heroes, Chad used the divine shards to resurrect a million souls,
creating the first Rifters and binding the Rift Reavers into the Rift
Wardens. Though his efforts established a corridor of order, the Rift's
chaos resisted taming. The city grew along this fragile path of
stability, its ghostly white marble buildings standing as a testament to
the Sage's resolve. The Rift Wardens became the city's shield, their
twisted forms hidden behind mortal disguises, embodying both the Rift's
dangers and its hope. Founding to 1851 851 - The binding of the Rift
Reavers The Great Sage of the Rift, Chad, uses the divine essence of the
Rift's shards to bind the Rift Reavers, once chaotic marauders, into the
Rift Wardens. These guardians adopt the task of protecting the city and
maintaining order within the Rift. They disguise themselves in their
former mortal forms but reveal their twisted, Rift-infused appearances
during moments of stress and conflict.

900 - First Contact with the Cabal The Cabal, a shadowy faction
operating within the chaos of the Rift, is discovered attempting to
corrupt fallen divine manifestations. The Rift Wardens repel them after
a harrowing battle in the outskirts of the city. This marks the
beginning of a centuries-long conflict between the two factions. They
were known to the Great Sage of the Rift, and he was to them - but
disagreements still led to conflict. Rift Wardens would fall and be
saved by the Sage, and Cabal members would be slain and revived by their
Cabal Masters. 1000 - The Cosmic Emissaries The Great City of the Rift
announces its presence to the galaxy, sending emissaries to a select few
nations to establish contact. Most remain wary, though the emissaries'
knowledge of divine phenomena intrigues scholars and leaders alike. Few
are granted access to the Rift, and those who leave are forever marked
by their time within. 1100 - The Rise of the Riftbound Council To manage
the growing population and complexity of the city, the Great Sage of the
Rift establishes the Riftbound Council, a governing body composed of
scholars, warriors, and sages. While the Sage retains ultimate
authority, the council oversees day-to-day operations, including trade,
defense, and diplomacy. 1200 - The Riftquake Calamity A surge of chaotic
energy erupts within the Rift, destabilizing sections of the Corridor of
Order. Entire districts of the city are lost to the chaos. The Rift
Wardens and the Sage manage to stabilize the core regions, but the
disaster reinforces the precarious balance of life in the Rift. 1200 -
The Great Sundering of the Rift The Great Sundering of the Rift was a
pivotal and devastating event in galactic history, triggered by the
reckless ambition of a faction of Voidwalkers from the Malvrick
Confederated Orders. In 1220, they attempted to harness forbidden divine
energies from the Rift, destabilizing its balance and unleashing a
catastrophic explosion. This created the Sundered Veil, a chaotic region
of spatial anomalies and divine remnants, consuming entire star systems.
In the aftermath, the legendary Rift Sage rallied a diverse force of
five thousand warriors to contain the Veil's expansion, battling
reality-warping monstrosities and ultimately stabilizing the Rift
through a powerful ritual. This disaster irrevocably marred the
reputation of the Voidwalkers and the Malvrick Orders while elevating
the Rift Sage to mythic status. The Sundering remains a cautionary tale
of the peril of wielding forces beyond mortal comprehension. 1400 - The
Great Rift Purge A coalition of Rift Wardens and sages expels a wave of
fallen divine manifestations that threaten the city. During the
conflict, Rift Wardens reveal their monstrous forms to the city's
citizens, sparking fear and mistrust. The Great Sage issues a
proclamation reaffirming the Wardens' role as protectors, though the
scars of the event linger. 1685 - The Cabal infiltrates the Council The
Cabal succeeds in infiltrating the Riftbound Council, corrupting several
members and nearly inciting civil unrest. The Sage uncovers the plot,
purging the traitors in a dramatic public trial. The event increases
tensions between the city's factions and strengthens the Wardens' role
in internal security. 1851 - The Stabilizing of the Rift's core After
centuries of effort, the Great Sage and the Rift Wardens establish a
semi-stable core within the Rift. The Great City of the Rift becomes the
central bastion of order, though the chaos beyond its borders remains
untamed. The Rift is now both a sanctuary for lost souls and a prison
for cosmic threats, cementing its role as a unique and enigmatic nation.
Although this region was only a small sliver of the endless rift, it was
a beacon of hope the would shine through the prison plane.

In 1851 By 1851, the Great City of the Rift had become a paradox: a
place of order within the galaxy's most chaotic domain. Its population,
now thriving within the Corridor of Order, embraced their role as
Rifters, custodians of the divine dead. The Rift Wardens, their
monstrous forms now an accepted part of life, continued to defend the
city against threats from the Cabal and the Rift itself. Though largely
isolated, the city's reputation as a unique sanctuary had reached far
corners of the galaxy. As chaos churned beyond its borders, the city
endured, a fragile bastion in a sea of disorder. Hoard of Drazmyrr Rise
of a Nation Born from the ashes of the Concrete Cartel, the Hoard of
Drazmyrr emerged as a greedy, ambitious empire under the reign of
Drazmyrr the Covetous. This legendary red dragon, a creature the size of
a small moon, seized power with fire, cunning, and a bottomless thirst
for wealth. Over time, the Hoard evolved into a ruthless plutocracy,
where power is bought with gold, and loyalty is guaranteed through the
promise of endless riches. With Genasi alchemists refining raw resources
into commodities and Goblins running black-market operations, the Hoard
thrives as a nexus for trade---both legal and illicit---while Drazmyrr's
ever-growing treasure hoard becomes the mythic centerpiece of their
nation. Founding to 1851 1480 - The Fall of the Concrete Cartel The
remnants of the Concrete Cartel crumble as warlords and rival factions
carve up its territories. Out of the chaos, whispers spread of a
monstrous red dragon consolidating power on a desolate mining moon.
1505 - The Rise of Drazmyrr Drazmyrr the Covetous, a red dragon the size
of a small moon, unleashes fire and fury upon the fractured goblin
warbands. Through sheer strength and guile, Drazmyrr establishes
dominion over critical resource-rich systems, claiming all wealth for
his expanding hoard. Goblin factions, seeing opportunity, swear fealty
in exchange for positions of privilege under the dragon's rule. 1530 -
The Alchemist Accord Seeking to refine his wealth further, Drazmyrr
invites Genasi alchemists from neighboring systems. They revolutionize
the extraction and refinement of raw materials into rare, valuable
commodities, cementing the Hoard as a leader in resource trade. 1600 -
The Black-Market Concord Under Drazmyrr's watchful eye, the Goblins of
the Hoard formalize a sprawling black-market network. Rare goods,
forbidden technologies, and illegal substances flow freely through Hoard
systems, fueling Drazmyrr's growing treasure trove while drawing
suspicion from neighboring states. 1650 - The Forge of Eternal Greed The
Hoard begins construction of the Forge of Eternal Greed, a massive
orbital complex that extracts and processes wealth directly from
planetary cores. Powered by the alchemical mastery of Genasi engineers,
the Forge becomes a symbol of Drazmyrr's unrelenting hunger for riches.
1690 - Joining the Galactic League of States Despite its reputation as a
haven for illicit trade, the Hoard of Drazmyrr is recognized for its
undeniable economic contributions and resource dominance. The Galactic
League of States was still an informal alliance, but it

begrudgingly admits the Hoard, though many member nations regard
Drazmyrr's empire with distrust. 1755 - The Guildmasters' Rebellion A
coalition of Goblin guild leaders attempts to overthrow Drazmyrr, citing
unbearable taxes and draconian demands. The rebellion ends in disaster;
Drazmyrr incinerates entire guild fleets, melting their ships into
molten slag to add to his hoard. Surviving guildmasters pledge eternal
fealty under new, harsher contracts. 1800 - The Great Counting Drazmyrr
summons every guild and alchemical faction to oversee an empire-wide
audit of his hoard. The Great Counting lasts two decades, involving
thousands of archivists and accountants cataloging every coin, artifact,
and trade good in the Hoard. By its conclusion, Drazmyrr's wealth is
declared immeasurable, further solidifying his mythical status. 1840 -
The Smelter Wars Tensions rise as the Hoard clashes with neighboring
systems over mining rights and extraction operations. Drazmyrr's
mercenaries, supported by Genasi alchemical weapons, quickly crush
opposition, securing monopoly control over the most lucrative star
systems. In 1851 From its founding in 1505, the Hoard of Drazmyrr grew
relentlessly. Through a mix of fear, economic innovation, and ruthless
efficiency, Drazmyrr established a plutocracy that rewarded the
wealthiest and most cunning underlings. The Forge of Eternal Greed,
completed in 1650, became the heart of the Hoard's wealth, while Goblin
guilds spread influence across the galaxy through their black-market
dealings. The Hoard's admission to the Galactic League of States in 1690
was met with resistance, but Drazmyrr's economic dominance made him
impossible to ignore. Over the following centuries, the Hoard crushed
rebellion, expanded trade, and secured a near-monopoly on mineral
extraction in neighboring independent systems. However, this prosperity
came at a cost. Drazmyrr's Great Counting in 1800 revealed the scale of
his empire's wealth but sparked growing resentment among his taxed
guilds and alchemists. The Smelter Wars of 1840 demonstrated the Hoard's
military strength, but tensions remain high, with Drazmyrr's greed
driving ever-increasing demands on his subjects. It's widely believed
that Drazmyrr is the wealthiest being in the galaxy - perhaps the
wealthiest being to have ever exixsted. Hundred Stances Nation in early
3E In the wake of the Titan Exodus in 150, Hobgoblin
Stances---fractured, warlike clans obsessed with martial prowess---found
themselves at a crossroads. Left to die on the Titanworld, they turned
to brutal pragmatism. Warlords like Skazak Chainmaul and Rivethook the
Ironbrand enslaved fleeing engineers, stole voidcraft, and ruthlessly
carved a path into the stars. Many Hobgoblins died in the attempt, their
stolen ships breaking apart in orbit or drifting into oblivion. The
survivors scattered into the Wastes, a voidspace surrounding the
Titancore, where shattered remnants of the Second Material Plane
littered the stars like the bones of dead worlds. Here, Hobgoblins saw
opportunity. The Stances built their first Salvage Tethers, anchoring
themselves to drifting fragments of broken planets. Life was harsh, but
the Hobgoblins thrived---raiding weaker fleets, enslaving survivors, and
forging a new identity from the ruin they now called home.

Nation in 851 By 851, the Hundred Stances have established themselves as
the undisputed masters of the Wastes. Each Stance---named for the tools
and weapons they favor---operates like a small fiefdom. The Chainshroud
Stance patrols salvage routes with brutal efficiency, while the Rustclad
Stance controls the largest mobile forges. Others, like the Wrenchblade
and Bloodthresh, specialize in boarding raids and repurposing enemy
ships. The Hobgoblins navigate the treacherous voidspace of the Wastes
with unmatched skill, salvaging relics of unfathomable power and
repurposing them into hybrid war-machines. Massive Chain
Galleons---hulking voidcraft held together with chains and scavenged
armor---serve as mobile fortresses. Outsiders view the Stances as
barbaric scavengers, but to the Hobgoblins, the Wastes are their empire.
The Forge Accord keeps the Stances from open warfare, but tensions
simmer beneath the surface as ambitious Chainlords vie for dominance.
The Great Salvage Accord of 710 brought a tenuous stability, allowing
the Hundred Stances to trade relics with neutral factions. However,
incursions into the Wastes are met with swift and brutal retaliation. To
outsiders, the Hobgoblins are ruthless scavengers, but their society
thrives on innovation, pragmatism, and a relentless will to survive.
From 851 to 1851 Year 150 to 880 -- The Great Escape The Titan Exodus
shatters the Titanworld's tenuous stability. Hobgoblin warbands like the
Shatterscythe, Ironlash, and Rivetchain seize ships and enslave
engineers to escape into the stars. Many Hobgoblins perish in failed
attempts, but the strongest make their way into the void. They settle in
the Wastes, where fragmented remains of worlds from the Second Era float
in chaotic ruin. The Titan Exodus seems to never end - but mostly due to
the expulsion of Hobgoblins from New Cauldron Year 900 -- The Drift Wars
As Hobgoblin Stances fight over salvage zones in the Wastes, chaos
reigns. The Shatterclad Stance rises to prominence, using brutal tactics
to dominate key debris fields. During this period, the Hobgoblins begin
building Chainships---cobbled-together vessels strung with salvage
tethers, their hulls adorned with hooked chains for boarding raids. Year
1030 -- The Forge Accord The warring Stances realize that unchecked
conflict threatens all survival. The Forge Accord is signed,
establishing the first codes of salvage and combat. Scrapwranglers,
skilled salvagers who locate valuable wreckage, rise to prominence.
Hobgoblin warlords form loose alliances, sharing claims and establishing
salvage rights. Year 1220 -- The Chainforge Revolution The Hammerchain
Stance discovers an ancient forge-world fragment deep in the Wastes.
Using recovered schematics, they invent Salvage-Forges, mobile
refineries that can melt debris and reforge it into weapons, armor, and
ships. The Chainforge technology spreads, and the Stances rapidly expand
their fleets. Year 1300 -- The Riftplague Outbreak A Rift anomaly tears
through a salvage field, releasing a cursed energy that mutates
Hobgoblin crews. The Ashenshard Stance sacrifices itself to contain the
plague, earning them legendary status. The surviving Stances establish
the Waste Wanderers, elite enforcers tasked with managing anomalies and
Rift dangers in the Wastes. Year 1410 -- The Iron-Tether Compact Stances
like Wrenchblade, Bloodthresh, and Gritspike unite under the Iron-Tether
Compact, centralizing authority over major salvage operations.
Chainlords emerge---powerful warlords who oversee salvage routes,

defend against incursions, and arbitrate disputes. The Wastes stabilize
into a loose but functional Hobgoblin dominion. Year 1550 -- The Scrap
Siege Outsiders attempt to plunder the Wastes. Armadas from the Ethereal
Nomads and rogue Blackstar Corp fleets clash with Hobgoblin Chainships.
The Hobgoblins repel the invaders using improvised war-machines like
Chain Galleons---massive ships strung together with wreckage. The
victory cements their reputation as masters of void warfare. Year 1685
-- Joining the Galactic League of States Pressured by the Galactic
League to open the Wastes, the Hobgoblins of the Hundred Stances
reluctantly join. Though they oppose resolutions to curtail war or
salvage rights, their knowledge of Rift anomalies and salvaged relics
makes them a critical member. Year 1740 -- The Graviton Collapse A
gravitic anomaly destabilizes entire salvage zones. The Rustclad and
Grimewhip Stances lead a coordinated effort to save drifting wreckage,
preventing galactic catastrophe. Their success wins begrudging respect
from outsiders. Year 1850 -- The Rise of the Salvage Dynasts The
Ironhook Council, a ruling assembly of the most powerful Chainlords,
solidifies control. They centralize authority under the Salvage Dynasts,
elite families who manage the most profitable salvage zones and Rift
salvage operations. The Hundred Stances are no longer just
scavengers---they are a salvaging empire. In 1851 By 1851, the Hundred
Stances have evolved from fragmented warbands into a formidable
salvaging empire. Under the rule of the Salvage Dynasts, the most
powerful Chainlords of the Ironhook Council, the Hobgoblins have
centralized their operations. Salvage Tethers now link entire debris
fields into sprawling Salvage Hives, where millions of Hobgoblins labor
to strip, forge, and repurpose ancient wreckage. Their hybrid
technology---part relic, part innovation---gives the Stances a unique
edge in void warfare and industry. The Wastes remain perilous, with Rift
anomalies and gravitic collapses threatening entire sectors. The Waste
Wanderers, elite Hobgoblin enforcers, patrol the most dangerous zones,
ensuring that no artifact is lost to chaos. Chainships, Chain Galleons,
and the newly developed Graviton Drifters dominate the void, cementing
Hobgoblin control over the Wastes. As members of the Galactic League of
States, the Hundred Stances oppose efforts to restrict salvage
operations or outlaw interstellar war. They are vocal detractors,
arguing that conflict is the crucible of progress. Though many see them
as scavengers, the Hobgoblins wear the title as a badge of honor. To
them, the Wastes are sacred ground ---an endless forge where the strong
endure, the weak perish, and the ambitious thrive. Their creed remains:
"What is broken will be reforged. What is lost will be claimed. And what
is ours will never be taken." Ikzo Nation in early 3E In the early Third
Era, the Ikzo were a fractured tribal society struggling for survival on
the Titanworld. Their gods' physical manifestation, a rare and
terrifying event, united the tribes in a shared mission to escape
impending doom. This revelation triggered a seismic cultural shift, as
shamans and warriors turned their focus from raiding and survival to the
pursuit of knowledge and technology. The Ikzo, once scattered and
savage, began pooling their resources and skills, creating the
foundations for their eventual exodus into the stars.

Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Ikzo have transformed into a burgeoning
interstellar nation in Savage Space. Known for their discipline and
unity under the Scaled Compact, they have earned a reputation as the
most civilized of the Savage Space nations. Their fleets, equipped with
advanced cloaking technologies, patrol their borders with vigilance. The
Ikzo are recent members of the Galactic League of States, using their
