position to foster trade and cooperation while remaining wary of outside
influence. Though their history is marked by conflict, including the
strange and tumultuous War of the Shifting Scales, the Ikzo have forged
a unique identity that balances their tribal roots with interstellar
ambition. From 851 to 1851 120 - The Great Panic On the Titanworld, the
Ikzo gods manifest in physical form, warning their followers of an
impending cataclysm that will consume their land. The dramatic event
sparks widespread panic and inspires the Ikzo to abandon their tribal
rivalries in pursuit of technological advancement. They begin pooling
their resources to develop a means of escape into the stars. 400 - The
Celestial Exodus After centuries of effort, the Ikzo achieve space
travel and flee the Titanworld in mass migrations. This event, known as
the Celestial Exodus, is guided by the Chameleon Guard, a revered holy
order of Lizardfolk warriors who ensure the safety of the migrating
tribes. The Ikzo establish their first colonies in the untamed regions
of Savage Space. 600 - The War of the Shifting Scales A major conflict
arises between the Ikzo and the Jade Pact over the control of Yuan-Ti
ruins in a remote star system. The war is intense and prolonged, with
the Ikzo's guerilla tactics and the Chameleon Guard's stealth abilities
clashing against the Jade Pact's disciplined forces. The war ends in a
bizarre twist when a Yuan-Ti wraith possesses the Ikzo leader, High
Shaman Kreztek, masquerading as him for five decades before being
exorcised by holy men from across Savage Space. 750 - The Forging of the
Scaled Compact Following the war, the Ikzo establish the Scaled Compact,
a formal council of tribes designed to prevent future conflicts and
promote internal cooperation. This marks the beginning of a more unified
Ikzo identity, blending their tribal roots with a growing sense of
interstellar nationhood. 900 - The Rise of the Star-Fangs The
Star-Fangs, a new military caste within Ikzo society, emerges to protect
their borders and enforce the Scaled Compact's laws. These warriors,
drawn from the Chameleon Guard and other clans, solidify the Ikzo's
reputation as fierce and disciplined fighters. Their ships are equipped
with advanced cloaking technology inspired by their natural camouflage.
1025 - The Pact of Serpents The Ikzo enter a period of cultural
reflection and reform, reintegrating lessons learned from their
encounter with the Jade Pact. The Pact of Serpents is established,
honoring Yuan-Ti ruins as sacred and prohibiting further conflicts over
them. This declaration gains respect from neighboring nations and
strengthens the Ikzo's spiritual identity. 1250 - The Stellar Renewal
Project

The Ikzo undertake a large-scale terraforming initiative known as the
Stellar Renewal Project, transforming barren planets in their domain
into lush worlds suited for their species. This project cements their
reputation as stewards of life and ensures the survival of their people
for generations to come. 1450 - The Covenant of the Elders A grand
council of Ikzo elders convenes to create the Covenant of the Elders, a
set of guiding principles that formalize the transition from tribal
traditions to a unified interstellar government. While some tribes
resist the change, the Covenant ultimately strengthens the Ikzo's
cohesion. 1750 - Membership in the Galactic League, then Expulsion The
Ikzo's growing stability and diplomatic efforts culminate in their
acceptance into the Galactic League of States. As one of the smallest
members, they use this position to secure trade agreements and
technological exchanges, elevating their status among Savage Space
nations. They are later expelled in the same year due to their
involvement in the first tide of savage space. 1851 - The Era of Scaled
Harmony By 1851, the Ikzo have established themselves as a small but
cohesive interstellar power. Their blend of tradition and innovation,
combined with their disciplined military and diplomatic acumen,
positions them as a rising force in Savage Space. The Scaled Compact
continues to guide their development, ensuring that their cultural
identity remains intact while they navigate the challenges of an
interconnected galaxy. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Ikzo are a respected and
stable power in Savage Space. Their worlds are lush and thriving thanks
to the success of the Stellar Renewal Project, and their government
under the Covenant of the Elders ensures cohesion and progress. Their
warriors, including the Star-Fangs and the revered Chameleon Guard,
remain a symbol of their martial prowess, while their spiritual respect
for Yuan-Ti ruins and their diplomatic acumen in the Galactic League
bolster their standing among neighboring nations. The Ikzo have
successfully evolved from a tribal society into a unified interstellar
nation, navigating the challenges of the galaxy with resilience and
purpose. Jade Pact Nation in early 3E In the early years of the Third
Era (3E), the Jade Pact arose as a fledgling civilization amidst the
enduring splendor of the ancient Yuan-Ti ruins. The humans who settled
in these lands, descendants of scattered refugees and explorers from
Orrin's wars, were captivated by the timeless beauty and functionality
of the Yuan-Ti structures. The Skyward Temples and Serpent Spires,
intricately sculpted with magical wards and carvings of coiled serpents,
became the centerpieces of their nascent society. These humans organized
themselves into a feudal system to manage the vast and mystically
preserved ruins, with each Lord Protector ruling from a Yuan-Ti citadel
and swearing loyalty to a central Emperor, a symbolic figure believed to
have the divine mandate of the ancestors who first settled the region.
The Jade Pact's society drew heavily from the remnants of Yuan-Ti magic
and culture, though they reshaped it to align with their human heritage.
Architects of the Pact were tasked with deciphering and maintaining the
ancient enchantments that kept their cities eternal, while the
Greenblades, their warrior class, adopted combat techniques inspired by
carvings of Yuan-Ti warriors depicted in flowing, almost serpentine
motion. At the heart of their spiritual practices was a reverence for
the Yuan-Ti's accomplishments, seen not as gods but as enigmatic
precursors whose mastery of magic and craftsmanship symbolized an ideal
for humanity to strive toward. Yet, the Pact faced challenges: the
labyrinthine ruins often harbored hidden dangers, from slumbering
Yuan-Ti constructs to echoes of Orrin's chaotic magic that occasionally
manifested as living shadows.

Despite these threats, the Jade Pact's unity and resourcefulness allowed
them to carve a niche in the galaxy as keepers of one of the most
enduring legacies of the Titan Wars. Their early years were marked by a
blend of reverence and adaptation, as they sought to rebuild a
civilization worthy of the foundations they had inherited, while slowly
expanding their influence beyond the borders of their inherited Yuan-Ti
ruins. Nation in 851 By the year 851 AG, the Jade Pact had grown into a
powerful and enigmatic civilization, its dominion spanning several
sectors and centered around the enduring ruins of the Yuan-Ti. The
Pact's core systems are a blend of human ingenuity and ancient Yuan-Ti
grandeur, with sprawling cities dominated by towering Serpent Spires and
intricately carved Skyward Temples that still hum with the enchantments
of their original architects. The Jade Pact's society remains rooted in
its quasi-feudal structure, with Lord Protectors overseeing vast
territories under the authority of the Green Emperor, a hereditary ruler
whose lineage is believed to descend from the first settlers of the
Yuan-Ti ruins. The Green Court, housed in the colossal Emerald Citadel,
serves as both the spiritual and political heart of the Pact, a place
where tradition, magic, and diplomacy intertwine. The Pact's economy and
culture revolve around their stewardship of the Yuan-Ti's preserved
wonders, as well as their mastery of Serpentine Craft, a fusion of
Yuan-Ti enchantments and human technological advancements. The
Architects of the Pact are revered as sages, maintaining the delicate
balance between preserving the ancient magic and pushing its boundaries
to suit the needs of a galactic power. Meanwhile, the Greenblades, elite
warriors clad in jade-encrusted armor, serve as both defenders of the
Pact and enforcers of its will, their fluid combat style reflecting the
artistry of their Yuan-Ti predecessors. Politically, the Jade Pact's
dominion is stable but cautious, wary of the ambitions of its
neighboring powers. Their feudal vassals ensure loyalty through a mix of
tradition and fear of the Pact's ability to mobilize devastating Yuan-Ti
war constructs, still operational after millennia. The ruins themselves,
however, remain both a blessing and a curse; while they grant the Jade
Pact prestige and a technological edge, their depths hide forgotten
dangers, including dormant Yuan-Ti guardians and erratic magical surges.
Despite these challenges, the Jade Pact thrives in 851 AG as a
civilization defined by its unique blend of reverence for the past and
ambition for the future, balancing its heritage with the demands of an
ever-expanding galactic presence. From 851 to 1851 860 - The Emerald
Reforms Recognizing the need for modernization, Green Emperor Hanzai III
implements the Emerald Reforms, which streamline the Pact's feudal
governance by requiring standardized taxes and military contributions
from vassals. This increases central authority while preserving the
traditional autonomy of the Lord Protectors. 910 - The Awakening of the
Woven Deep beneath the ruins of the Serpent Spires, a long-dormant
Yuan-Ti construct, known as the Woven Serpent, activates due to an
accidental disturbance by the Pact's Architects of the Pact. The
colossal entity wreaks havoc on several settlements before being
subdued. This event prompts the Pact to establish the Order of the
Coiled Blade, a military branch specializing in neutralizing Yuan-Ti
remnants. 1020 - The Incursion of the Obsidian Tide The Jade Pact faces
a major external threat as the Obsidian Tide, a marauding fleet of
pirate factions, invades its outer sectors. The Greenblades, led by the
legendary commander General Isaru Zhen, mount a successful
counteroffensive, pushing the Tide back after a decade of war. The
campaign secures the Pact's borders and enhances its military
reputation. 1115 - The Verdant Compact

In a rare diplomatic achievement, the Jade Pact forges the Verdant
Compact with the nearby Maru Accord, a coalition of independent systems.
This alliance ensures shared trade routes and mutual defense,
stabilizing the Pact's galactic position. The pact also allows for
cultural exchanges that enrich both civilizations. 1200 - The Shadowed
Purge Dissident factions within the feudal lords, led by Lord Protector
Takomi of the Jade Scales, rise against the central authority of the
Green Emperor, resulting in a brief but bloody civil war known as the
Shadowed Purge. The rebellion is crushed by the Greenblades, and
Takomi's forces are exiled. This event prompts stricter oversight of
vassal territories and the establishment of the Imperial Wardens, a
secretive body tasked with rooting out sedition. 1255 - The Luminal
Expedition The Architects of the Pact lead the Luminal Expedition,
exploring uncharted ruins within the Pact's sectors and uncovering a
trove of Yuan-Ti relics. These discoveries lead to breakthroughs in
Serpentine Craft, further integrating Yuan-Ti enchantments into modern
technology. This golden age of innovation sees the rise of VerdantClass
Warships, designed with advanced magical shields and precision weaponry.
1400 - The Coiled Frontier Campaign As the Jade Pact seeks to expand its
territory, it encounters fierce resistance from the Shattered Clans, a
collection of warlike human and alien factions occupying resource-rich
systems. The Coiled Frontier Campaign stretches over half a century,
ultimately resulting in the annexation of several key systems but at the
cost of significant resources and lives. 1520 - The Green Horizon
Initiative With the Coiled Frontier secured, the Green Emperor launches
the Green Horizon Initiative, a program aimed at colonizing and
terraforming inhospitable worlds using Yuan-Ti-inspired technology. This
ambitious project strengthens the Pact's economy and allows it to become
a key exporter of advanced terraforming techniques. 1650 - The Great
Succession Crisis The death of Green Emperor Kaizan IV without an heir
plunges the Jade Pact into the Great Succession Crisis, a period of
political intrigue and minor skirmishes between rival claimants. The
crisis is resolved after six years when Empress Riku the Wise, a distant
relative of Kaizan, is elected by the feudal lords in a rare display of
unity. Her reign brings stability and prosperity. 1725 - The Awakening
Plague A malfunction in a Yuan-Ti artifact releases a virulent magical
plague that ravages several core worlds. The Order of the Coiled Blade
works alongside the Architects to contain the outbreak, but the disaster
leaves lingering scars on the Pact's infrastructure and population.
1800 - The Emerald Accord To secure its position in the Galactic League
of States, the Jade Pact establishes the Emerald Accord, a declaration
of its commitment to stability, trade, and technological cooperation
with other League members. The accord strengthens the Pact's influence
in galactic politics. 1820 - The Subjugation of the Veiled Blades A
rogue faction of the Order of the Coiled Blade, known as the Veiled
Blades, rebels against the Green Emperor, claiming that reliance on
Yuan-Ti relics will doom the Pact. After a decade-long campaign, the
rebellion is quashed, reaffirming the central authority of the Emperor
and showcasing the might of the Greenblades. 1851 - A Balanced Power By
1851, the Jade Pact stands as a balanced and thriving power in the
galaxy. Its mastery of Yuan-Ti relics and its ability to integrate these
into modern technology have made it a leader in advanced enchantments
and terraforming. While its feudal structure endures, central authority
is stronger than ever, supported by a blend of tradition and

innovation. The Pact's sectors are stable, though the depths of the
Yuan-Ti ruins remain a constant source of both inspiration and danger.
In 1851 In 1851, the Jade Pact is a shining example of balance between
reverence for the past and adaptation to the future. Its feudal
hierarchy operates with unprecedented efficiency under the strong
leadership of the Green Emperor, and its sectors are prosperous thanks
to the enduring value of Yuan-Ti relics and the technological
advancements they inspire. The Pact is deeply respected within the
Galactic League, known for its blend of magical and technological
prowess, as well as its ability to preserve stability despite internal
and external challenges. Though occasional unrest and the unpredictable
nature of the Yuan-Ti ruins pose risks, the Jade Pact is a model of
resilience, ambition, and unity. Karak Ankor'dûm Nation in early 3E The
Dwarven Clansystems emerged in the aftermath of cataclysmic wars on the
Titanworld, as dozens of clans abandoned the scarred continents of their
ancestral home to seek refuge among the stars. Among the most notable
were Clan Ironstout, renowned for their unmatched craftsmanship and
unyielding resilience, Clan Forgebraid, famed for their expertise in
metallurgy, and Clan Anvilthane, whose warriors were said to wield
weapons blessed by the echoes of their ancestors. Bound together by
desperation and determination, these clans pooled their knowledge,
resources, and traditions to survive the perilous journey into the void.
Their exodus was fraught with challenges, from infighting over
leadership to the logistical nightmare of crafting ships strong enough
to carry their people into the great unknown. Despite these struggles,
the Dwarves' steadfast unity prevailed, culminating in the formation of
the first Council of Clans, a rudimentary governing body tasked with
organizing their new society. By embracing their shared history and
adapting their renowned runic magic to the needs of spacefaring life,
the Dwarves transformed their plight into opportunity, laying the
foundation for the industrious and resilient nation that would one day
be known as the Dwarven Clansystems. Nation in 851 In 851 AG, the
Dwarven Clansystems stood as a resolute and industrious federation,
their societies rooted deeply in tradition and innovation. Emerging from
centuries of civil war, the Clansystems had unified under a shared
commitment to rebuilding their shattered worlds and advancing their
mastery of runic magic. This era marked a golden age of cultural and
technological development, with great houses like the Chromazz,
Ironbeard, and Stonefist clans leading the charge in engineering marvels
and the revival of ancestral-binding techniques. The Runeforges of Karak
Thandril, vast complexes where ancestral spirits were imbued into
weapons and tools, had become the centerpiece of Dwarven society,
blending spirituality and practicality in their everyday lives.
Politically, the Dwarves were governed by the Council of Forges, a
coalition of clan representatives who worked to maintain peace and
prosperity. However, tensions simmered beneath the surface as rival
clans vied for influence, and the specter of old grudges occasionally
reared its head in disputes over mining rights and technological
secrets. The Clansystems' focus on industrial growth made them a vital
cog in the galactic economy, supplying advanced machinery and raw
materials to other nations. Yet, their isolationist tendencies kept them
wary of deeper alliances, preferring to focus inward on preserving their
cultural identity. Religious practices were nearly non-existent, with
the Dwarves placing their faith in their ancestors and their own
craftsmanship rather than external deities. By 851, the Dwarven
Clansystems embodied resilience and determination, a nation determined
to forge a future worthy of the echoes of its storied past.

From 851 to 1851 900 - The Ancestral Accord The Chromazz Clan initiates
a cultural renaissance centered on preserving ancestral knowledge. This
leads to the establishment of Runic Halls, where bound ancestral spirits
guide governance and innovation. These halls become critical to both
military and civilian decision-making. 1000 - The Iron Veil Compact The
clans unite to create the Iron Veil Compact, a fortified network of
defensive stations infused with ancestral spirits. These fortresses
serve as both military strongholds and sacred sites, protecting trade
routes and expanding the clans' influence. 1100 - The Forge of Unity
Internal competition gives way to cooperation as the clans form the
Anvil Assembly, a unified industrial council. This standardizes runic
technologies and ensures the efficient distribution of resources across
the Clansystems. 1225 - The Skyforged Armada Advances in ancestral
integration technology enable the creation of the Skyforged Armada, a
fleet of ships bound with the spirits of legendary warriors and
engineers. These vessels become renowned for their tactical precision
and resilience, cementing the Dwarves' naval dominance. 1310 - The Runic
Revolution Breakthroughs in runic magic allow for the binding of
multiple spirits into colossal constructs like Iron Golems and
Rune-mechs. These constructs become the backbone of the Dwarven
military, giving them unmatched strength in both ground and space
combat. \_1500 - The Rise of Thargrim Yurtrak Thargrim Yurtrak, a
charismatic and pragmatic leader, begins consolidating power within the
Clansystems. His reforms centralize authority, militarize society, and
establish a strict, disciplined culture centered on duty, unity, and
reverence for ancestral guidance. 1650 - The Birth of Karak Ankor'dûm
Thargrim declares the formation of Karak Ankor'dûm - Meaning "The Forge
of Eternal Might" in the Dwarven tongue, this name reflects the nation's
industrial and militaristic evolution while honoring its ancestral
traditions - transforming the Clansystems into a unified, militarized
state. Their society becomes tightly controlled, with a strong focus on
military strength and industrial efficiency. Ancestral integration is
expanded to include warships, planetary defenses, and large-scale
constructs. They become members of a martial alliance that would later
form the Galactic League of States, but loosen these relations before
the League if formalised due to an invasion. 1700 - The War of Karak
Bakhmut The Star Tsars of Zandrov and the Fire Bishoprics of Octoria
invade Dwarven space, triggering the War of Karak Bakhmut. Initially
framed as a brief military campaign, the conflict devolves into a
prolonged and brutal war that consumes millions of lives. The Dwarves
hold their ground, demonstrating the might of their Skyforged Armada and
Rune-mechs. 1750 - The Forge Concord The Dwarves join the Galactic
League of States, leveraging their advanced technology and military
strength to secure a prominent position. Their participation marks a
shift toward interstellar diplomacy, though their authoritarian policies
remain intact. 1825 - The War of the Null Sectors

A devastating war erupts between Karak Ankor'dûm and the Calix
Techno-Theocracy, driven by competition over resources and ideological
differences. The conflict leaves entire sectors uninhabitable, resulting
in a stalemate and the establishment of a "no man's land." Both sides
suffer catastrophic losses, but the Dwarves' resilience reinforces their
commitment to militarization. 1850 - The Iron Dominion Initiative Karak
Ankor'dûm launches the Iron Dominion Initiative, focusing on reclaiming
lost territory, rebuilding their fleet, and further integrating
ancestral magic into all aspects of life. The initiative underscores
their ambition to remain a dominant galactic force. 1851 - A Unified
Superpower By 1851, Karak Ankor'dûm has evolved into a disciplined and
heavily militarized interstellar state. Their war fleets patrol the
galaxy, their Rune-mechs dominate battlefields, and their authoritarian
governance ensures internal stability. Their society is a blend of
ancestral tradition and cutting-edge innovation, making them one of the
most formidable powers in the Galactic League of States. In 1851 In 1851
AG, the Dwarven nation of Karak Ankor'dûm stands as a formidable and
militarized powerhouse, embodying the culmination of centuries of
industry, innovation, and ancestral reverence. The once-fractured clans
of the early Clansystems have been forged into a singular, disciplined
entity under an authoritarian regime established a century prior. Their
borders are fortified by the Iron Dominion, a network of heavily armed
citadels and orbital defenses, while their fleets, imbued with the
spirits of legendary engineers and warriors, patrol the stars with
unmatched precision. Central to their strength is the widespread
integration of ancestral binding into every facet of their civilization,
from towering Rune-mechs on the battlefield to Skyforged Dreadnoughts in
space, each vessel carrying the wisdom and might of their forebears.
Their military dominance is matched by their economic prowess, as the
forges of Karak Thandril and Anvilspire Prime churn out advanced
machinery and runic technologies coveted across the galaxy. Despite
their might, Karak Ankor'dûm is still recovering from the devastating
War of the Null Sectors against the Calix Techno-Theocracy, a conflict
that left entire swaths of space as uninhabitable "no man's land."
Relations with neighboring nations remain tense, particularly with the
Galactic League of States, where the Dwarves wield significant influence
but are often criticized for their authoritarian practices and
unyielding policies. Internally, the Dwarves maintain strict social
order through a combination of ancestral reverence and the ever-watchful
Runeguard, their secret police tasked with ensuring loyalty and
eliminating dissent. Yet, even within their rigid society, cracks begin
to form as younger generations question the cost of maintaining their
militarized state. In 1851, Karak Ankor'dûm is a nation defined by its
unity, resilience, and unparalleled technological integration, standing
as both a symbol of Dwarven ingenuity and a stark reminder of the toll
exacted by centuries of conflict and ambition. Keiranoore Nation in
early 3E In the months before Orrin's Grip, a mage known as Keiran
seized an ancient opportunity. Draining the essence of the Giant
King---an extinct god of Orrin's Pantheon---Keiran ascended to godhood,
transforming into the Crystal One and preparing to resume the First War.
When Orrin's happened, he eagerly traveled through the grip to the
Titanworld. Around the year 400, he established Keiranoore on a series
of resource-rich systems, claiming every crystal, star, and fragment of
land in his name. Humans, drawn by Keiran's immense power, flocked to
the nation, alongside Oread who viewed him as the embodiment of
elemental perfection. Early Keiranoore was a theocratic empire, with
Keiran as both a living god and an unparalleled mage, dictating the laws
of magic, trade, and worship.

The Minotaur clans, deeply loyal to Orrin, served as the militant
backbone of Keiranoore. While some remained staunch followers of the
titan, a growing number turned to Keiran, their horns transforming into
dazzling crystalline structures---a mark of divine favor. Keiran's early
rule focused on consolidating power, spreading the gospel of crystalline
magic, and creating a civilization that blended advanced magical
technology with religious fervor. Nation in 851 By 851, Keiranoore is
the second nation to be ruled openly by a god---Keiran the Crystal One.
Its systems gleam with floating crystalline cities, energy conduits, and
artificial moons imbued with Veilstone power. The nation is a hub of
magical innovation, producing crystalline constructs, ships, and weapons
far beyond the technological reach of its neighbors. The population is
split between devoted followers of Keiran and militant worshippers of
Orrin. Despite internal tensions, both factions recognize Keiran's
supremacy and the prosperity his rule provides. Keiranoore's borders are
guarded by fleets of crystalline warships and magic-wielding Acolytes
who can channel devastating spells through crystal-bound conduits.
Neighboring nations regard Keiranoore with a mix of awe and suspicion,
particularly for its Orrin-aligned origins. Diplomatically isolated,
Keiranoore remains an enigmatic presence in the galaxy. The Galactic
League of States views the Crystal One as a volatile force, while
Keiranoore itself thrives under his unchallenged rule. From 851 to 1851
851 - The Crystal Throne Ascendant Keiranoore stands as a nation of
immense magical power and reverence for Keiran the Crystal One, with
crystalline magic dominating its technology, architecture, and warfare.
Humanity and Oread thrive under Keiran's watch, while Minotaur
worshippers of Orrin serve as enforcers of war doctrine. The Crystal One
personally oversees magical advancements, ensuring his dominion remains
unmatched in the Border Systems. Neighboring nations fear Keiranoore's
growing influence, as rumors circulate of crystalline weapons capable of
shattering entire fleets. 960 - The Crystal Expansion Keiran's
followers, empowered by crystalline-infused ships and magic, expand into
neighboring systems, claiming resource-rich sectors in the Border
Systems. These "Crystal Crusades" are justified as reclaiming what
rightfully belongs to the Crystal One. The expansion raises tensions
with smaller neighbors and sparks minor skirmishes with independent
systems. 1020 - The Fracture Rebellion A group of Minotaur clans loyal
to Orrin rebel against Keiran's rule, seeking to eliminate what they
call the "Blasphemy of Crystal." Led by Ghornak of the Red Horn, the
rebellion spreads across three systems, causing significant damage to
key crystalline infrastructure. Keiran intervenes personally,
obliterating the rebellion with immense magical force and crystallizing
Ghornak's army into statues. These statues remain on the war-torn planet
of Akzorath as a grim warning. 1150 - The Age of Translucence Keiranoore
enters a golden age of magical and technological development.
Crystalline generators revolutionize energy production, and floating
crystal citadels become the seats of local governance. This era sees the
creation of Crystal Golems, constructs infused with Keiran's essence,
used to guard borders and sacred sites. Trade with neighbors grows
cautiously, though cultural distrust remains due to Keiranoore's Orrin
alignment. 1280 - The Shattering Plague

A devastating magical anomaly sweeps through Keiranoore, known as the
Shattering Plague, causing crystalbased technology to spontaneously
crack, explode, or fail. Entire cities reliant on crystalline generators
suffer catastrophic collapses. Followers of Orrin claim this is a
punishment for over-reliance on Keiran's gifts. The Crystal One retreats
to his sanctum for decades to resolve the crisis, while regional
governors enforce martial law to maintain order. 1350 - The Crystal
Reforging Keiran re-emerges, having discovered a way to stabilize
crystalline magic by fusing it with a new energy source extracted from
the Border Systems' latent magical anomalies. Known as Keirastone, these
minerals replace fragile crystalline cores, reinvigorating Keiranoore's
infrastructure and military might. The event is marked by celebrations
across the nation, solidifying Keiran's divine status further. 1450 -
War of Crystalline Aegis A coalition of smaller nations attempts to
challenge Keiranoore's control over resource rich stars within the
Border Systems, claiming its magical dominance is a destabilizing force.
Keiran's forces respond decisively, deploying crystalline fleets and
magical storms to annihilate enemy incursions. The war ends with
Keiranoore reinforcing its borders and establishing crystal outposts in
contested systems. 1715 - Joining the Galactic League of States After
decades of diplomatic tension, Keiranoore is granted membership in the
Galactic League of States. However, their insistence on sending holy men
in place of secular diplomats sparks further distrust among League
members. Keiranoore remains a controversial presence, viewed as an
extension of Orrin's warlike ambitions despite Keiran's insistence on
peaceful study and trade. 1800 - The Crystal Concordance Keiran declares
a new edict called the Crystal Concordance, mandating greater autonomy
for local governors to reduce inefficiencies caused by his centralized
rule. This reform results in rapid economic and military growth, as
local systems compete to prove their loyalty through achievements. In
1851 By 1851, Keiranoore has become a paragon of magical and
technological might. Keiran the Crystal One remains its unshaken ruler,
though his direct involvement in governance has lessened due to the
Crystal Concordance, which granted more power to regional governors. The
Keirastone revolution of the 14th century stabilized Keiranoore's
infrastructure and strengthened its fleets, ensuring its presence across
the Border Systems. Crystalline warships, powered by Keirastone cores,
patrol the nation's borders, while towering golems safeguard cities and
holy sites. The nation thrives economically, exporting crystalline
energy and unique magical technology to trusted trade partners, though
its Orrin worship continues to cause friction with the Galactic League.
Culturally, Keiranoore is a realm of contrasts. Crystalline magic is
used to craft unparalleled beauty and art, yet it is also the source of
devastating weapons. Orrin's militant followers remain ever vigilant,
eager for conflict, while Keiran's more scholarly devotees focus on
unlocking deeper secrets of the cosmos. The Minotaur population, now
fully integrated and accepting the Crystal One as their patron, stands
as Keiranoore's most feared enforcers - their crystalline horns
shimmering with magic as they march in Keiran's name. Keiranoore stands
as an empire of divine ambition and arcane supremacy, its shimmering
cities a testament to the Crystal One's enduring legacy. Yet its
alignment with Orrin and refusal to conform fully to galactic diplomacy
keeps it teetering between respected power and feared isolationist
force. Kwellick Nation in early 3E

The Kwellick were scrapper clans on the Titanworld, living among the
ruins of ancient civilizations. Their survival depended on scavenging
and repurposing materials left behind by greater empires. Each clan
operated independently, valuing ingenuity and resourcefulness above all
else. When the Titan Exodus began, the Kwellick clans salvaged abandoned
ships, retrofitting them with scavenged engines and weapons to escape
into the stars. Settling in unclaimed systems rich in debris fields, the
Kwellick quickly adapted to their new environment. By the end of the
early Third Era, they had established themselves as industrious
salvagers, specializing in extracting and refining rare materials.
Despite their crude methods, their craftsmanship was unmatched, earning
them a reputation as the galaxy's premier scrappers. Nation in 851 By
851, the Kwellick Nation was a burgeoning power centered around
Rustspire Station, their sprawling orbital scrapyard capital. The
Kwellick thrived on salvaging Second Era ruins, refining rare materials,
and creating hybrid technologies. Their society, governed by the Council
of Scrappers, emphasized practicality and efficiency, valuing gold,
silver, and precious resources over galactic credits. The Scrap Guard,
their defensive fleet, patrolled the Kwellick systems, fending off
frequent raids by the Olneans. Though their fleets were cobbled together
from salvaged parts, their ingenuity and resourcefulness made them
formidable defenders. The Kwellick remained neutral in galactic
conflicts, focusing solely on trade and material acquisition, earning
them a reputation as reliable but aloof partners. From 851 to 1851 Year
851 - the Ascendance of Scrappers' Clans The Kwellick, scattered among
various industrial systems, consolidate into a unified nation. They
establish Rustspire Station as their capital, a massive orbital
scrapyard-turned-metropolis where their obsession with salvaging and
refining rare materials shapes their cultural identity. Year 920 - the
First Olnean Raids The Olneans, nomadic space Yetis, begin targeting
Kwellick systems, seeing their rich stockpiles of salvaged resources as
prime plunder. Though initially overwhelmed, the Kwellick form the Scrap
Guard, a fleet of repurposed salvaged ships outfitted with crude but
effective defenses. Year 1030 - the Rustfire Renaissance The Kwellick
innovate in metallurgy and engineering, developing Rustfire Alloy, a
unique material forged from scavenged metals infused with arcane energy.
This breakthrough bolsters their economy and strengthens their ships and
infrastructure. Year 1100 - the Compact Accord The Stellar Compact signs
a limited trade agreement with Kwellick, exchanging surplus dieselpunk
tech for refined materials. The accord improves Kwellick's industrial
capabilities and reinforces their neutral reputation in galactic
affairs. Year 1200 - the Siege of Rustspire A massive Olnean fleet
launches an assault on Rustspire Station, testing the Kwellick's
defenses. The Scrap Guard, led by Commander Zraksh Ironcog, uses
innovative ambush tactics in the dense debris fields surrounding the
station to repel the invaders. The victory cements Kwellick's reputation
as resourceful defenders. Year 1400 - the Ironhold Controversy

Leaked documents reveal that several Kwellick clans secretly stockpiled
forbidden relics from the Second Era, triggering widespread distrust
among their Galactic League peers. The Council of Scrappers launches
investigations, ultimately exiling the offending clans to maintain their
reputation. Year 1785 - Joining the Galactic League of States The
Kwellick officially join the Galactic League of States, presenting
themselves as a neutral and industrious member. Their focus on rare
materials and salvaging expertise makes them valuable trade partners.
Year 1800 - the Great Materials Rush A surge in demand for rare
materials leads to the Great Materials Rush, with Kwellick systems
becoming galactic hubs for trade and manufacturing. This economic boom
solidifies their importance as industrial and technological pioneers. In
1851 By 1851, the Kwellick Nation had cemented itself as a vital
industrial power in the galaxy. Their systems were hubs of production
and refinement, with Rustspire Station at the heart of their operations.
The Great Materials Rush of the late 1700s had transformed their
economy, making them indispensable to nations seeking advanced alloys
and rare resources. The Scrap Guard, now a highly organized defensive
force, maintained order in Kwellick space and deterred Olnean
incursions. Their hybrid ships, built from salvaged and refined
materials, were both efficient and deadly. As a member of the Galactic
League of States, the Kwellick were respected for their neutrality and
industriousness. Their singular focus on material wealth and pragmatic
governance ensured their continued prosperity in an increasingly complex
galaxy. Though they eschewed politics and ideology, the Kwellick's
mastery of salvage and resource management made them a cornerstone of
galactic trade, proving that even the humblest beginnings could forge a
lasting legacy among the stars. League of Ki Nation in early 3E In the
early Third Era, the League of Ki began as a fractured collection of
human settlements fleeing the chaos of the Titanworld. United under the
First Edicts, a series of laws designed to preserve balance and
stability, they formed the foundations of their Tribunal system. Local
councils of judges and sages began to resolve disputes, laying the
groundwork for a lawful and impartial society. Nation in 851 By 851 AG,
the League of Ki had emerged as a structured and stable interstellar
nation governed by a vast network of Tribunals. Their annual gatherings
at the Halls of Accord allowed representatives to resolve disputes and
update the Edicts of Ki, their foundational legal framework. Known for
their efficiency, impartiality, and disciplined judicial process, the
League had become a respected member of the Galactic League of States,
offering mediation services to rival factions. From 851 to 1851 300 -
The Edicts of Foundation

After fleeing the Titanworld, the human survivors of Ki establish their
first settlements in isolated systems. Fragmented and divided, the
survivors are united under the First Edicts, a series of laws drafted by
a council of sages that later becomes the foundation of the Ki Tribunal
Collective. The Edicts emphasize impartial arbitration, meritocratic
leadership, and balance. 500 - The Rise of the Tribunals As the League
of Ki expands across star systems, local Tribunals---councils of judges
and sages---are established to mediate disputes and maintain law. The
Halls of Accord, a massive meeting place located on the capital world of
Ki's Hold, are constructed to host annual gatherings of the Tribunals.
This structure formalizes their method of collective decision-making.
700 - The Accord Reforms A crisis caused by corruption within several
Tribunals leads to the Accord Reforms. New systems of oversight are put
in place, requiring Tribunals to report their rulings to the Halls of
Accord for review. A rotating council known as the Grand Tribunal is
established to oversee major disputes and uphold the impartiality of the
Edicts. 900 - The Codification of the Edicts The League of Ki undertakes
the Codification of the Edicts, a monumental effort to unify their laws
across all systems. The Great Codex of Ki is completed, a massive
archive that houses every ruling, precedent, and interpretation of the
Edicts. Copies of the Codex are maintained in the Halls of Accord and
every major Tribunal. This effort solidifies the League's reputation as
a center of law and order. 1100 - The Border Conflicts Clashes erupt
between the League of Ki and the neighboring Shatter Stance, as the
Hobgoblin nation encroaches on Ki-controlled systems. The Tribunals
resolve to defend their borders while maintaining their commitment to
impartiality. The conflict ends with the Treaty of Red Accord, a
diplomatic agreement negotiated at the Halls of Accord. 1300 - The
Shadow Tribunals A hidden faction of rogue judges, calling themselves
the Shadow Tribunals, is discovered attempting to manipulate the Edicts
for their gain. The Grand Tribunal launches a massive purge of corrupted
officials, reinforcing their commitment to the League's impartiality.
Though the rebellion is crushed, the scars linger as a reminder of the
danger of unchecked power. 1500 - The Mechanization of Justice The
League of Ki integrates advanced Judicial Automata into its Tribunal
system. These sentient constructs assist judges by cataloging laws,
analyzing disputes, and enforcing the Edicts without bias. The Judicial
Mechanism Accord ensures the automata's strict adherence to the Codex,
further solidifying the League's reputation for efficiency. 1685 - The
Galactic League of States The League of Ki becomes a ket member of a
grand martial alliance, and pushes for the founding of the Galactic
League of States for decades, offering their Tribunal system as a model
of impartial arbitration. While they refrain from taking a special role,
they are respected for their neutrality and legal expertise. The Halls
of Accord host intergalactic mediations, elevating the League's
influence. 1750 - The Rebellion of the Unruled Several outlying systems,
frustrated by the slow pace of Tribunal rulings, rebel against the
League. Known as the Rebellion of the Unruled, the conflict tests the
League's ability to maintain balance. The Grand Tribunal intervenes,
negotiating a peaceful reintegration while implementing reforms to
streamline legal processes. 1800 - The Concordance Expansion

The League of Ki launches the Concordance Expansion, extending the
influence of the Tribunals into unclaimed systems. Neutral arbiters and
Judicial Automata are dispatched to mediate disputes and enforce order
in lawless regions. This expansion is met with both praise and suspicion
from neighboring nations. 1851 - The Keepers of Balance By 1851, the
League of Ki stands as a beacon of order and stability in the galaxy.
The Grand Tribunal continues to oversee the Edicts of Ki, ensuring
impartial rulings and justice. The Halls of Accord have become a symbol
of peace and diplomacy, hosting interstellar disputes between rival
nations. While some criticize the League's rigid neutrality, its
commitment to balance and fairness ensures its continued survival. In
1851 By 1851 AG, the League of Ki stands as a bastion of law and balance
in a chaotic galaxy. The Grand Tribunal oversees the implementation of
the Edicts across their systems, with Judicial Automata assisting in
cataloging and enforcing laws. The Halls of Accord serve as a hub for
interstellar mediation, ensuring that the League remains a trusted
neutral arbiter. While their expansion into unclaimed systems has drawn
suspicion, their reputation as the Keepers of Balance remains unshaken,
a testament to their unwavering commitment to stability, fairness, and
impartiality. Lord Protector's Court Nation in early 3E In the early
Third Era, the Lord Protector's Court was born from conflict and divine
retribution. The Lord Protector, an elven ruler who had forsaken
Gro'May, pledged loyalty to Yu, the titan patron of chaos and
transformation. Empowered by Yu, the Lord Protector ruled over a
militant faction of elves, his power symbolized by the Ivory Snake, a
colossal serpent gifted by Yu. However, the Court's ambitions were
shattered when Caledor Sin'Dal and his companions confronted the Lord
Protector in a titanic battle. The Ivory Snake fell, the land was
scarred, and the Lord Protector was defeated. In the aftermath, Gro'May,
newly freed by Caledor, returned to elven space and demanded submission
from the Lord Protector. As penance for his betrayal, the Lord Protector
was sentenced to rule over a buffer zone of systems at the edge of elven
space, a frontier plagued by raids and incursions from barbaric forces,
pirates, and beasts. Thus, the Lord Protector's Court became a
militarized government, eternally tasked with guarding elven space
against external threats. The Court's society was shaped by its duty:
disciplined, hierarchical, and bound to Gro'May's will. Nation in 851 By
851, the Lord Protector's Court had evolved into a formidable military
state. Its systems were fortified bastions, filled with orbital defense
platforms and heavily armed fleets patrolling the frontier. The Ivory
Throne, the seat of the Lord Protector, rested on Courthaven, a world
encased in layers of defensive shields and weaponry. The Court's
governance was rigidly hierarchical, with the Lord Protector ruling as
an autocrat beneath Gro'May's oversight. While the Court was prosperous,
its position as a buffer zone meant it bore the brunt of constant
incursions. Beastlike raiders, pirate armadas, and other barbaric forces
frequently tested its defenses. Despite these challenges, the Court
maintained its vigilance, viewing its duty as sacred penance for the
Lord Protector's ancient betrayal. Diplomatically isolated, forbidden
from engaging with nations beyond elven space, the Court relied solely
on Gro'May's protection and limited trade with other elven factions.
Life within the Court was highly regimented, with citizens trained in
military service from a young age. Elven culture here was austere,
focused on martial prowess and loyalty to the Eternal King. Despite
this, resentment simmered

among some, who saw their isolation as a punishment they no longer
deserved. From 851 to 1851 Year 890 - The Beast Armada Strikes A massive
coalition of beast-like raiders breaches the Court's defenses, ravaging
several outer systems. The Crimson Concord, an elite task force, pushes
the invaders back after a year-long campaign. The Court rebuilds but at
great economic cost. Year 950 - The Corsair Insurgency Pirates from the
Red Maw Syndicate establish strongholds in the Court's outer systems,
disrupting trade routes and supply lines. A brutal crackdown led by
Marshal Veltharion eliminates the threat, but the insurgency highlights
weaknesses in the Court's naval strategies. Year 1010 - The Silent
Betrayal A rogue general within the Court conspires with a non-elven
nation to weaken defenses in exchange for power. The plot is discovered,
and the general is executed. Gro'May imposes stricter oversight, further
curtailing the Court's autonomy. Year 1100 - The Courthaven Accords To
stabilize the frontier, the Lord Protector enacts the Courthaven
Accords, mandating mandatory service for all citizens and restructuring
the military into smaller, more agile units. These reforms improve the
Court's ability to respond to incursions. Year 1230 - The Incursion of
the Black Wyrms A series of attacks by mutated, dragon-like creatures
devastate outer colonies. The creatures are eventually driven back by
Celestial Lances, weapons developed from salvaged ancient technology.
The event becomes a source of national pride. Year 1310 - The Return of
the Exiled A faction of elves exiled centuries earlier seeks refuge in
the Court, offering their allegiance in exchange for sanctuary. Though
initially distrusted, the exiles bring valuable knowledge and manpower,
revitalizing the Court's defenses. Year 1400 - The Great Rift Crisis A
series of rift storms wreak havoc on the Court's outer systems, leaving
entire worlds uninhabitable. The Court pleads for aid from other elven
nations, but their requests are denied, forcing them to handle the
crisis alone. Year 1450 - The Glimmer Pact The Court establishes the
Glimmer Pact, a formal alliance with several elven merchant houses.
While still forbidden from interacting with non-elven nations, this
trade arrangement brings much-needed resources to the Court. Year 1550 -
The Warden Reforms The Warden Reforms further militarize the Court's so
ciety, creating specialized units known as Wardens of the Void to guard
key systems. These reforms solidify the Court's reputation as an
impenetrable bulwark. Year 1700 - The Skirmish of Redfang Reach

A beast-like raiding fleet launches a surprise attack on the Court's
borders. Though the Wardens of the Void repel the invaders, the costly
victory reignites debate over the Court's isolation and the lack of
support from elven allies. Year 1780 - The Ivory Serpent Cult A secret
cult venerating the slain Ivory Snake emerges within the Court, claiming
that Yu will return to restore their former glory. The cult is crushed
after an investigation reveals connections to external raiders, but the
incident causes unrest. Year 1850 - The Sapphire Bastion Completed The
Court unveils the Sapphire Bastion, a massive orbital fortress designed
to guard Courthaven. The Bastion becomes a symbol of the Court's
enduring strength and dedication to its duty, even in the face of
isolation. In 1851 By 1851, the Lord Protector's Court stood as the
first line of defense for elven space, its systems fortified with
cutting-edge defenses and the battle-hardened Wardens of the Void. The
Sapphire Bastion, completed in 1850, served as both a military hub and a
monument to the Court's resilience. Isolated from galactic diplomacy by
Gro'May's decree, the Court remained reliant on the Eternal King and
trade with elven merchant houses to sustain its economy. While the Court
had successfully repelled countless incursions over the centuries, its
isolation continued to breed frustration among its citizens. The growing
resentment, coupled with whispers of secret cults and political
intrigue, hinted at a society grappling with its burdens. Yet, despite
these challenges, the Court's people remained steadfast in their duty,
bound by their oath to Gro'May and their belief in their sacred role as
guardians of elven space. As the galaxy continued to evolve, the Lord
Protector's Court clung to its traditions and its martial legacy,
standing as a symbol of sacrifice, discipline, and the price of
redemption. Malvrick Confederated Orders In 851 In the year 851 AG, the
Malvrick Confederated Orders were a union of many holy orders that
worshipped Malvrick of the The Pantheons of Orrin and Yu. Malvrick had
spent the second era bidding his time and growing resentful, angry, and
even bitter about the situation he and his brothers had placed
themselves in during the Battle of a Dozen Millennia, leading to open
disputes, interplanetary, and even interdimensional conflicts with those
that followed the other sons of Yu the Stained. Unfortunately during
this time, many of Malvrick's followers had fallen from grace during the
second era. In the dawn of the third era though, Yu had blessed Malvrick
and offered a path to redemption - healing his wounds and reversing the
damage that Yu had once dealt to him during the final days of the first
era. Malvrick had aligned with Carrigore, a dark visage of Yu's
fractured mind that had grown in power over the eons - until Malvrick
was freed at the start of the third era. Since then, Malvrick
followers - especially those that are in the holy orders that make up
the Malvrick Confederated Orders - do what they can to redeem
themselves, their ancestors, and their God in the eyes of Yu. From 851
to 1851 Year 900 - The Carrigore Cataclysm: A devastating battle occurs
near the border of Carrigore Space, where the Malvrick fleets suffer
massive losses. This defeat triggers widespread questioning of the
current military strategy, leading to the first signs of internal
dissent within the Orders.

Year 950 - The Schism of the War Priests: A significant group of War
Priests, frustrated with the continuous losses against Samulo, breaks
away from the central command. They establish a splinter faction called
the Crimson Vow, seeking more radical methods to combat the Carrigore
threat. This marks the beginning of internal fragmentation. Year 1020 -
The Reformist Revolt: In one of the outer systems, a charismatic leader
named Cardinal Athrun rises to power, advocating for significant reforms
in the Orders' structure, particularly regarding the centralization of
power and the distribution of resources. His movement gains traction,
leading to a brief but intense civil conflict known as the Reformist
Revolt. Though the revolt is quelled, the tensions leave lasting scars
on the confederation. Year 1100 - The Founding of the Steel
Enlightenment: Following the technological advancements made during the
last century, a new Order called the Steel Enlightenment is founded.
They are devoted to the integration of machine and man, believing that
this is the path Malvrick has ordained for victory. However, their focus
on technology causes friction with more traditionalist Orders,
exacerbating internal divisions. Year 1250 - The Silent Expanse Crisis:
A series of unexplained disappearances of entire star systems within a
region known as the Silent Expanse leads to panic. The Orders send
multiple fleets to investigate, but none return. This mysterious event
further shakes the confidence of the leadership and heightens the sense
of vulnerability within the confederation. Year 1330 - The
Ecclesiastical Tribunal: The central leadership, in an attempt to regain
control, establishes the Ecclesiastical Tribunal, a body designed to
enforce orthodoxy and maintain unity. However, the Tribunal's
heavy-handed methods, including the execution of several high-ranking
dissenters, only deepen the divisions and spark further resentment among
the Orders. Year 1415 - The Great Famine of Aragon Prime: A catastrophic
famine strikes Aragon Prime, one of the key agricultural worlds in the
confederation. The crisis is mishandled, with food aid being diverted to
more politically loyal Orders. This leads to riots and the eventual
secession of several systems, which declare independence under the
banner of the Malvrick True Path. Year 1500 - The Covenant of the Four:
In response to the growing instability, four of the most powerful Orders
form a secret pact known as the Covenant of the Four. They agree to
cooperate more closely in military and economic matters, effectively
creating a power bloc within the confederation. This further weakens the
central authority and sets the stage for future power struggles. Year
1600 - The Return of the Crimson Vow: The Crimson Vow re-emerges as a
major power after decades of operating in the shadows. They begin a
campaign of targeted strikes against Carrigore Space, claiming several
key victories. Their resurgence is met with both admiration and fear, as
their methods are ruthless and often border on heretical. Year 1705 -
The Second Schism: The long-simmering tensions between the Steel
Enlightenment and the traditionalist Orders explode into open conflict.
The Second Schism lasts for nearly a century, resulting in the
devastation of several systems and the weakening of the confederation's
overall power. Year 1800 - The Treaty of Anvil's End: Exhausted by
centuries of conflict, the various Orders finally agree to a ceasefire
and sign the Treaty of Anvil's End. The treaty restructures the
confederation, granting greater autonomy to the individual Orders while
maintaining a loose central command for mutual defense. While this
brings a temporary peace, the confederation is now more

fragmented than ever. Joining the Galactic League of States becomes a
top priority, and happens by the end of the year. Year 1850 - The Dawn
of the Phoenix Crusade: In the final year of the time skip, a new
charismatic leader named High Marshal Xeros rises within the ranks of
the Orders. He declares a new crusade against Carrigore Space, promising
to unite the fractured confederation through a holy war. Whether this
will succeed or lead to further fragmentation remains uncertain as the
new era begins. In 1851 In the year 1851 AG, the Malvrick Confederated
Orders stand fractured yet unbroken, their unity held together by
fragile treaties and a shared devotion to Malvrick. Over the past
millennium, the Orders have endured internal strife, external defeats,
and growing ideological rifts, yet they remain a force to be reckoned
with in the galaxy. Their relentless war against Samulo and Carrigore
Space has drained resources and lives, leaving many questioning the
viability of their holy crusade. The once-centralized command of the
confederation has splintered into semiautonomous factions, with powerful
blocs such as the Covenant of the Four and radical groups like the
Crimson Vow pursuing their own agendas. Despite this fragmentation,
Malvrick's followers continue to seek redemption in the eyes of Yu,
driven by a deep sense of purpose and divine mission. Technological
advancements spearheaded by Orders like the Steel Enlightenment have
bolstered their military capabilities, but these gains are undermined by
the persistent cultural and political divisions that plague the
confederation. The recent rise of High Marshal Xeros and his call for a
Phoenix Crusade has rekindled hope among some and fear among others, as
the Orders teeter on the edge of either reunification or complete
disintegration. In 1851, the Malvrick Confederated Orders are a shadow
of their former glory, yet they remain resolute in their devotion,
holding onto the belief that their sacrifices will one day restore both
their honor and Malvrick's place in the pantheon. New Black Republic
Nation in early 3E In the chaotic aftermath of the Compact's
near-collapse in 131, the Benthic Tieflings, imprisoned in a walled-off
village within a sprawling desert prison, seized their chance for
freedom. Led by charismatic rebels who still revered their hero Benthi,
they launched a bloody revolt as the Compact's forces scrambled to deal
with the upheaval caused by Caledor, whose sorcery lifted their capital
into the sky. Armed with little more than cunning, scavenged weapons,
and the latent chrono-magics that lingered within their bloodline, the
Tieflings overwhelmed their captors and claimed the prison as their
first bastion of freedom. They renamed it Shadowhold, a fortress-symbol
of their defiance and the birthplace of their burgeoning nation. During
these early days, the Benthic Tieflings were a people driven by survival
and vengeance, conning and bartering with nearby warlords while sending
raiding parties to the Compact's remaining strongholds. Benthi himself,
still a mysterious and revered figure among his kin, was said to appear
sporadically, guiding key leaders with cryptic advice and bolstering
their morale. Though fractured and lacking true unity, the Tieflings'
shared hatred of the Compact and their unwavering determination to carve
out a future cemented their transformation from prisoners into
revolutionaries. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the New Black Republic has
evolved into a fearsome spacefaring nation, their fleets of sleek,
blackhulled raiding ships striking terror into neighboring systems. The
Benthic Tieflings are no longer scattered survivors but a formidable
society built on cunning, guile, and the calculated application of
force. Their government operates as a loose confederation of powerful
crime lords, with a figurehead leader known as the Dusk Regent, rumored
to be a descendant of Benthi himself or perhaps the immortal hero in
disguise. Their fleets patrol the lawless stretches

of space between their domain and the fragmented Shattered Daimyos,
whose recurrent attempts to unify into a Celestial Empire are often
sabotaged by the Tieflings' subterfuge. The New Black Republic thrives
on piracy, raiding, and the exploitation of fractured alliances in the
regions they target. Yet their society is not without its peculiar
order: a strict code of conduct governs internal affairs, with harsh
punishments for treachery within their ranks. The Chronarchs, a
secretive council of mystics who wield the timealtering magics inherited
from Benthi, oversee strategic planning and keep the Republic ahead of
their foes. Meanwhile, the Republic's infamous Star Cutters, elite
raiding squadrons, are renowned for their ability to infiltrate and
dismantle even the most fortified trade convoys. While the New Black
Republic revels in its reputation as a galactic menace, their growing
power has made them a target of numerous coalitions. Despite this, their
mastery of deception and the lingering effects of Benthi's chronomagic
keep them one step ahead, ensuring their continued dominance in the
region. By 851 AG, the Benthic Tieflings stand as a testament to
resilience and ambition, their rise from captivity to interstellar
infamy echoing across the stars. From 851 to 1851 870 - The Splintering
of Shadowhold A power struggle erupts among the Republic's crime lords
over control of Shadowhold, the Republic's symbolic first stronghold.
The conflict escalates into a civil war that fractures the Republic's
unity for decades. Benthi appears at the height of the chaos, using his
time-altering abilities to unravel key battles before they occur,
forcing a truce and reestablishing his authority as leader. 890 - The
Timewind Massacre A catastrophic misuse of time artifacts by rogue
factions causes the Timewind Massacre, freezing entire cities in stasis.
Benthi intervenes to reverse the damage, but his absence destabilizes
the Republic, allowing warlords to seize power over the frozen zones.
920 - The Treaty of Stolen Stars To replenish resources and maintain
control over their fleets, the Republic brokers a secret agreement with
Carrigorian warlords to sell intelligence and captured goods. While
profitable, the deal angers many Tieflings who view it as a betrayal of
their self-reliant ethos, leading to the first whispers of dissent
against the Republic's leadership. 1010 - The Rise of the Black Masques
A rogue faction of elite raiders, known as the Black Masques, emerges
within the Republic, refusing to follow central leadership and instead
forming their own semi-autonomous enclaves. Their cunning tactics and
brutal efficiency make them a threat even to the Republic's own fleets.
Benthi briefly reappears, using his knowledge of future events to
orchestrate a daring ambush that forces the Black Masques back under
Republic control. 1050 - The Ivory Theft -A legendary heist by the Ghost
Blades steals the Republic's most sacred artifact, the Chrono-Tome of
Benthi, leading to years of internal purges. The chaos enables several
mercenary companies to rise as pseudogovernments, fragmenting the
Republic. 1115 - The Conquest of the Glass Nebula The Republic expands
its influence into the Glass Nebula, a resource-rich region previously
controlled by independent traders. The campaign is a costly victory, as
the Republic suffers heavy losses defending their newly acquired
territories from retaliatory raids. The Mist-Weavers, a secretive group
of time-magic practitioners, emerge during this period, using their
powers to ensure victory in critical battles.

1200 - The Collapse of the Third Celestial Empire The Republic
orchestrates a series of devastating raids and political subterfuge that
dismantle the third attempt by the Shattered Daimyos to unify into a
Celestial Empire. Using a combination of propaganda and time-altering
magics, the Republic engineers key defections within the Daimyo
leadership, ensuring the fractured region remains ripe for exploitation.
1350 - The Oath of Benthi's Return In a rare appearance, Benthi swears
to return at the Republic's darkest hour. His words spark the formation
of the Twilight Courts, a coalition of mystics who wield artifacts to
stabilize the Republic's fractured regions. 1350 - The Great Rift Crisis
A sudden spatial anomaly disrupts trade and travel in the Republic's
core territories. Benthi reappears to stabilize the region, using time
magic to coordinate evacuations and reestablish routes through the
affected systems. His intervention is heralded as divine, but the crisis
leaves the Republic's infrastructure weakened for decades. 1450 - The
Zenith Rebellion Dissatisfied with the increasing centralization of
power, a coalition of Tiefling clans declares independence from the
Republic, igniting the Zenith Rebellion. The conflict stretches the
Republic's forces thin as they attempt to suppress the uprising while
defending their borders from opportunistic raiders. Benthi's absence
during the rebellion leads to whispers of doubt about his omniscience.
1600 - The Founding of the Scarlet Haven The Republic establishes
Scarlet Haven, a fortified space station and sanctuary for Tieflings
fleeing persecution in neighboring sectors. Scarlet Haven becomes a
symbol of Republic resilience and unity, helping to restore faith in the
government after the rebellion's suppression. 1751 - The Grand Jubilee
of Benthi On the millennium anniversary of Benthi's transformation of
the Tieflings, the Republic holds a grand celebration. During the event,
a shadowy conspiracy attempts to assassinate Benthi, believing his death
would destabilize the Republic. Benthi foresees the attack and
manipulates the timestream to thwart the assassins, exposing them and
reaffirming his position as an immortal protector. 1820 - The Twilight
Concord The Republic signs the Twilight Concord, a tenuous
non-aggression pact with several factions of the Shattered Daimyos. The
agreement is purely pragmatic, allowing the Republic to consolidate
power in their own regions while the Daimyos deal with their own
internal conflicts. 1851 - A Fractured Legacy By 1851, the New Black
Republic remains a dominant but fractured force in the galaxy. Its
economy thrives on piracy and trade, but internal struggles continue to
plague its unity. Benthi's rare but timely appearances maintain a
semblance of cohesion, while the Republic's mystics and raiders ensure
their external dominance. However, whispers of rebellion and
overextension linger, casting doubt on the nation's long-term stability.
In 1851 In 1851 AG, the New Black Republic is a complex tapestry of
ambition and chaos. Their fleets remain a terror to neighboring sectors,
their time magic ensuring they stay one step ahead of their enemies.
Yet, their reliance on piracy and manipulation leaves them vulnerable to
internal dissent and external coalitions. Benthi's sporadic guidance
keeps the Republic from splintering entirely, but even his influence
cannot mask the cracks forming in their

foundation. The Republic's future hinges on whether it can balance its
anarchic nature with its growing need for stability and direction. New
Cauldron Cauldron in early 3E The Origins of Cauldron New Cauldron's
roots trace back to the destruction of Orrin's Grip, a world obliterated
when it faced its creator. As the planet fractured, a chunk of its
capital city survived the cataclysm, hurtling through the void to
crash-land in a mountainous region on a distant world. The protective
peaks encircled the urban remnants like a cauldron, giving the
settlement its name: The Cauldron. In the ruins, survivors rebuilt their
lives, salvaging technology and resources to establish a fledgling
society. For years, the Cauldron prospered in relative peace until word
of its riches spread, attracting raiders and opportunists. Chief among
these threats were the Hobgoblin Stances, militarized tribes that
launched relentless assaults, seeking to claim the city's treasures. The
Rise of the Warlord and the Formation of the Vault Stance To protect
their newfound haven, the survivors banded together to resist the
Hobgoblins, but their efforts were ultimately crushed when a Hobgoblin
Warlord united the Stances and seized the city. Under his iron rule, the
Cauldron became a Hobgoblin stronghold, its streets patrolled by the
Warlord's enforcers. From among his most elite warriors, the Warlord
formed the Vault Stance---a new faction tasked with guarding the city's
wealth and treasuries. The other Stances, such as the Hammer and Axe
Stances, were used for conquest and occupation. The Warlord's reign
brought stability, but at the cost of the citizens' freedom and
resources. His rule lasted for decades, until his sudden death from a
mysterious disease left a power vacuum that his successors failed to
fill. The Adventurers' Revolt and John Skyrim's Rise Sensing the
Hobgoblins' weakness, a group of adventurers led a revolt, rallying the
oppressed citizens of the Cauldron to overthrow their overlords. Among
these heroes was John Skyrim, who, following their victory, seized
control of the city. Recognizing the potential of the Vault Stance, he
restructured it, turning it into his personal guard and enforcers. John
Skyrim ruled the Cauldron with charisma and ambition, restoring order
and beginning the city's recovery. However, his reign ended abruptly
when he faked his own death, vanishing into obscurity with a significant
portion of the city's wealth. His betrayal left the Cauldron in turmoil,
with no clear successor. Mikey the Great and Galactic Expansion In the
chaos that followed, Mikey the Great (formerly Mikey Smalls) emerged as
a stabilizing force. Charismatic and visionary, Mikey took control of
the city, steering it toward a new future. He began the early stages of
galactic colonization, using Cauldron's remaining resources to establish
trade routes and outposts in nearby systems. Under his leadership, the
seeds of New Cauldron were planted. As the galaxy opened its arms to
colonization, Mikey spearheaded the transformation of Cauldron from a
fractured city-state into a thriving interstellar nation. While most of
the Hobgoblin Stances were dissolved or exiled, the Vault Stance
remained loyal to the aristocracy, serving as mercenaries and elite
soldiers in New Cauldron's expansionist campaigns. The Legacy of
Cauldron From its humble beginnings as a fragment of a shattered world
to its rise as a galactic power, Cauldron's history is one of resilience
and reinvention. The betrayal of John Skyrim and the visionary
leadership of Mikey the Great are key chapters in its saga, marking the
transition from a ruined city to the founding of New Cauldron. Today,
the Vault Stance stands as a reminder of the city's turbulent past, a
symbol of both its trials and triumphs. New Cauldron in 851: A Galactic
Power

By the year 851, New Cauldron has ascended to the ranks of a formidable
galactic power, its influence spanning multiple star systems. Renowned
for its highly disciplined legions of mercenaries, New Cauldron's
military might is a cornerstone of its identity, with these forces hired
by nations across the galaxy for their unparalleled efficiency and
loyalty to contract. The nation is equally famous for its vibrant and
diverse culture, where numerous cults thrive alongside its secular
institutions, reflecting its staunch belief in freedom, autonomy, and
self-determination. Its markets bustle with the energy and richness of
trade reminiscent of the great galactic powers of the Second Era,
offering everything from exotic goods to cutting-edge technology.
Politically, New Cauldron prides itself on its democratic foundations,
holding elections every five years since its formal establishment in the
year 131. The first president, Mikey the Great, led with members of his
infamous mercenary guild, the Band of Cock, setting a precedent of
blending military strength with civic governance. In 851, the current
administration is focused on numerous goals, most notably its
involvement in the Myr Cluster Wars---a series of escalating conflicts
between multiple nations vying for dominance over the strategically
vital Myr Cluster. These wars present both a challenge and an
opportunity for New Cauldron to expand its influence, offering its
mercenaries for hire while navigating the complex web of alliances and
rivalries. Amidst this turbulence, New Cauldron remains a beacon of
resilience and ambition, embodying the unyielding spirit of its founders
and charting its course as a dominant force in the galaxy. From 851 to
1851 Year 885 - The Battle of Lumina Verge New Cauldron's Mercenary
Legions, under the command of Legate Darius Varron, win a decisive
battle against the Coldiron Caliphate at Lumina Verge in the Myr
Cluster. The battle solidifies New Cauldron's reputation for efficient
military strategy and establishes Legate Varron as a legendary figure.
However, the heavy cost of victory highlights the strains of prolonged
conflict. Year 950 - The Formation of the Iron Pact To consolidate its
position in the Myr Clusters, New Cauldron brokers the Iron Pact with
smaller independent systems, offering them protection in exchange for
military and economic support. This alliance strengthens New Cauldron's
foothold in the region but creates resentment among its larger rivals,
particularly the Second Elrossi Imperium. Year 1020 - The Vault Stance
Diversification The Vault Stance undergoes a major restructuring,
opening its elite ranks to non-human races, including Elves, Dwarves,
and even Orcs. This transformation turns the Vault Stance into a symbol
of unity within the diverse population of New Cauldron and expands its
role from mere city defenders to elite strike forces across the galaxy.
Despite this, tensions arise as some human veterans resent the changes.
Year 1130 - The Siege of Nebra's Hollow During the final phase of the
Myr Cluster Wars, New Cauldron leads a coalition force to break the
Gob-Ogre Trade Federation's control over the vital trade hub of Nebra's
Hollow. The siege lasts nearly a decade, draining resources but
ultimately resulting in victory. The war begins to wind down shortly
thereafter. Year 1300 - The Treaty of Solace The Myr Cluster Wars
officially end with the signing of the Treaty of Solace, mediated by the
neutral Stars of the Triprophets. As part of the treaty, New Cauldron
establishes a client state---the Freehold of Myr---to act as a buffer
zone between it and the Gob-Ogre Trade Federation. This marks a
strategic victory for New Cauldron and ensures relative peace in the
region for centuries. Year 1370 - The Rise of the Brass Concord A major
shift occurs within the Mercenary Legions, as several prominent guilds
merge into a dominant faction called the Brass Concord. Led by General
Andros Hale, the Concord becomes a powerful entity within New Cauldron,

controlling nearly half of the Legions. While this consolidation
increases efficiency, it also sparks debates about the autonomy of
smaller guilds. Year 1410 - The Second Founding of New Cauldron The
capital planet undergoes significant urban renewal under the leadership
of President Alyssa Markov, including the construction of the Grand
Cauldron Spire, a colossal skyscraper symbolizing the nation's
resilience and unity. This era also sees the introduction of advanced
infrastructure projects across the core systems, stabilizing trade and
communication. Year 1500 - The Silent Storm Rebellion A sudden rebellion
breaks out in the outer colonies, led by Commander Torik Raith, a former
Mercenary Legionnaire disillusioned with New Cauldron's central
government. The rebellion is crushed after three years, but it exposes
cracks in the confederation's governance over distant territories. This
prompts reforms to delegate greater autonomy to outer systems. Year
1610 - The Cybernetics Accord New Cauldron pioneers advancements in
cybernetics, introducing the Cybernetics Accord, which provides
subsidies for augmentations among military and civilian populations.
This leads to a significant boost in productivity and military
efficiency, although it sparks ethical debates about the over-reliance
on cybernetic enhancements. Year 1685 - The First League Conference New
Cauldron hosts the first meeting among it's grand alliance. It's during
this meeting that ideas of the Galactic League of States, a coalition of
interstellar nations aimed at fostering peace and cooperation, are
vocalised by several members. The event marks New Cauldron as a leading
diplomatic power and further elevates its standing in galactic politics.
Year 1705 - Annexation and Establishment of the State of Myr Declaring
the Freehold of Myr to be a state in New Cauldron - the puppet nation
had finally been annexed after being formed four centuries prior in the
Treaty of Solace. It's looked upon with pride from citizens across both
New Cauldron and the new state. Year 1750 - Founding Member of the
Galactic League The Galactic League of States is officially established,
with New Cauldron playing a prominent role in drafting its charter. As a
founding member, New Cauldron wields significant influence within the
League, promoting its principles of autonomy and mutual defense. Year
1800 - The Helix Project New Cauldron launches the Helix Project, a
monumental initiative to terraform barren worlds using advanced
technologies developed during the Cybernetics Accord and reverse
engineered from remnents of projects from the once great Helix Corp of
Xerxes. This project revitalizes several underdeveloped systems and
cements New Cauldron's reputation as a technological innovator. Year
1851 - A Beacon of Stability By 1851, New Cauldron stands as a
cornerstone of the Galactic League, its Mercenary Legions and Vault
Stance elite forces renowned across the stars. The nation maintains a
careful balance of power, leveraging its irreligious governance,
technological prowess, and diplomatic influence to remain a dominant
force in the galaxy. In 1851

In 1851 AG, New Cauldron is a thriving, stable interstellar power deeply
integrated into the fabric of galactic politics. The nation's
irreligious stance has allowed it to remain pragmatic and adaptive,
focusing on trade, military efficiency, and technological progress.
While internal rebellions and cultural tensions have occurred, they have
been effectively managed through reforms and military action. The
Mercenary Legions, now dominated by the Brass Concord, continue to act
as a powerful and profitable arm of New Cauldron's influence, while the
Vault Stance has evolved into a prestigious multi-racial elite force
symbolizing the nation's diversity and resilience. As a leading member
of the Galactic League of States, New Cauldron's voice carries weight in
interstellar diplomacy, and its investments in projects like the Helix
Project showcase its commitment to innovation and expansion. Though
challenges remain, New Cauldron's trajectory is one of measured growth
and enduring influence. New Xerxes Rise of a Nation The origins of New
Xerxes are steeped in tragedy and reinvention. In 850, the legendary
city of Xerxes drowned beneath the tides conjured by a powerful Water
Oni, an event that claimed millions of lives and erased the city's
once-great influence in the Myr Cluster. Survivors, desperate to
preserve their heritage, were evacuated to Dome 2 aboard the Nimbus Ark,
a colossal ship carrying cities in isolated domes. In 851, tensions
aboard the Ark reached a breaking point. Corbin David, a charismatic
leader and advocate for independence, led the people of Dome 2 in a
rebellion against Calix Corp's authoritarian policies. Dome 2 was
detached and sent hurtling to an uninhabited world, where the survivors
rebuilt as the newly independent city-state of New Xerxes. In its early
years, the city was fragile, its resources scarce, and its leadership
determined to secure survival through innovation and vice. With Corbin
David's declaration that "Excess is our export," New Xerxes forged a new
identity, building its economy on gambling, pleasure, and black-market
