competition between the two vassals. Year 930 - The Starborne Symposium
A summit hosted by Caledor gathers the brightest elven minds from the
Sovranty and beyond to develop a codified system of mana redistribution.
This innovation becomes the backbone of the Sovranty's burgeoning
magical infrastructure. Year 960 - Rise of the Stellar Guilds The
creation of guilds specializing in forging celestial tools and
enchantments marks a new era of economic growth. The guilds become both
a source of innovation and a hotbed of political intrigue as rivalries
emerge over access to resources. Year 1000 - The Forging of Stars
Caledor Sin'dal unveils his ability to forge stars, an achievement few
thought possible. This process, achieved through an intricate blend of
magic and ritual, elevates the Sovranty's prestige and strengthens its
position within the Cohorts of Thae'Viriel. Year 1060 - The Silverleaf
Accords To mitigate growing tension among guilds, Caledor implements the
Silverleaf Accords, a series of laws standardizing resource allocation
and guild privileges. The accords restore order but also give Caledor's
homunculi greater control over trade. 1150 AG - The Harmonium Enclave
Caledor forms the Harmonium Enclave, a council of advisors chosen from
his descendants and prominent guild leaders. Though ostensibly
collaborative, the enclave allows Caledor to maintain indirect control
over the Sovranty's key industries. Year 1250 - The Starlit Concord
Expansion The Sovranty extends its influence within the Starlit Concord,
leading efforts to unify elven vassals under Gro'May's vision. This
diplomatic victory bolsters the Sovranty's prestige within the Cohorts.
Year 1300 - The Titan Sabotage

The Titan Sabotage marks a pivotal moment for Ado'Quor, as the
once-hidden demiplane becomes a focal point of divine intrigue and
conflict. The revelation of its existence and the subsequent
interference of the titans disrupt its secluded harmony, introducing
instability that ripples through both Ado'Quor and the Sovranty of
Sin'dal. Though Caledor Sin'dal retains his dominion over Ado'Quor, the
emergence of rival deities and growing tensions set the stage for future
challenges that will test the stability of his rule and the unity of the
elven realms. Year 1380 - The Veilwoven Summit A gathering of the
Cohorts' vassals sees the Sovranty introducing innovations in arcane
security, including veils of magical concealment. These advancements
earn respect but also deepen rivalries with other elven states. Year
1450 - The Luminary Ascension Caledor designates a council of his
homunculi to oversee colonial development. Their effectiveness in
streamlining operations strengthens the Sovranty's infrastructure but
sparks whispers of overreach. Year 1600 - The Midnight Accord with
Thae'Viriel Caledor and Gro'May broker an agreement granting the
Sovranty limited autonomy in magical governance. This ensures stability
within the vassal state while fostering greater collaboration. Year
1750 - The Celestial Reclamation The Sovranty pioneers techniques to
reclaim lost elven artifacts scattered during earlier conflicts. These
efforts strengthen ties with other vassals and demonstrate Caledor's
commitment to elven heritage. Year 1851 - The Eternal Vigil Caledor
fortifies the Sovranty's borders with enchanted defenses, preparing for
potential incursions. The Sovranty emerges as a bastion of stability,
its governance blending innovation with unwavering loyalty to the
Cohorts - in the public eye. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Sovranty of Sin'dal
stands as a paragon of elven resilience and innovation. Governed by
Caledor Sin'dal and his homunculi, it is a realm of meticulous order and
arcane prowess. Though outwardly loyal to Gro'May, the Sovranty remains
a subtle power in the elven realms, its destiny intertwined with the
ambitions of its enigmatic ruler. Spellslate Nation in early 3E The
origins of Spellslate lie in the Titan Exodus, when desperate mage
conclaves and scholarly guilds fled the crumbling Titanworld. Unlike
many who escaped blindly into the void, the magisters pooled their
collective arcane knowledge to carve out a haven of order and
innovation. Settling at the edge of the Border Systems, they built
austere cities powered by early Riftforges---reactors harnessing
volatile planar energy. From these foundations emerged The Arcane
Consensus, a magocratic meritocracy where arcane mastery dictated one's
social standing. While mages enjoyed a life of scholarship and ethereal
luxury, Non-Magical Personnel (NMP) were relegated to manual labor and
menial tasks. This divide sowed the seeds of resentment, even as
Spellslate flourished into a bastion of magical progress and discovery.
Nation in 851

By 851, Spellslate stands as a gleaming monument to arcane achievement,
its ethereal spires and radiant cities symbols of the magisters' power.
It is a golden age of innovation: Riftforges provide near-limitless
energy, while planar treaties fuel extraordinary advancements in
spellcraft and technology. Spellslate has become a hub of scholarship,
attracting brilliant minds from across the galaxy to study at its
academies, such as the renowned Radiant Echelon. However, the divide
between mages and the NMP has deepened, with entire worlds of laborers
toiling to support the magocratic elite. NMP unrest bubbles beneath the
surface, whispered dissent spreading in factory cities and orbital
platforms. Though outwardly stable, Spellslate's gilded façade conceals
the fractures of a society straining under its own weight. From 851 to
1851 Year 150 - Founding of the Arcane Consensus The mage conclaves and
scholarly guilds fleeing the Titan Exodus band together, settling in
resource-rich systems at the edge of the Border Systems. Their
collective magical prowess allows them to construct the first ethereal
cities and floating spires. They establish The Arcane Consensus, a
meritocratic government where arcane mastery determines one's place in
society. Year 400 - The Ascension Codex The Arcane Consensus finalizes
the Ascension Codex, a foundational text detailing laws of magical
governance, ethical limits on arcane experimentation, and procedures for
societal advancement. However, this codex solidifies the rift between
mages and Non-Magical Personnel (NMP), codifying their subordinate role
in labor and society. Year 720 - The Riftforges Unleashed Seeking to
harness the energy of the Second Era's remnants, Spellslate magisters
discover the Riftforges---arcane reactors capable of transforming
chaotic planar energies into fuel for their cities. This sparks an era
of unparalleled innovation, but also instability. Mishandled Riftforges
lead to arcane anomalies that ravage entire colonies, resulting in the
Fivefold Cataclysm. Year 850 - Spellslate's Arcane Golden Age Spellslate
enters a golden age, becoming a hub for arcane research, technological
innovation, and spellcraft unparalleled in the galaxy. Scholars from
other nations secretly flock to its ethereal academies, while the NMP
underclass grows increasingly resentful of their societal exclusion.
Magical cities such as The Radiant Echelon and Concordia Obelisk
symbolize Spellslate's mastery over magic and matter. Year 1000 - The
Spark of Dissent Tensions between mages and the NMP erupt when an
influential revolutionary, Sorin Talebryn, leads a rebellion in the
Vyrana System. Though swiftly crushed by the magocratic council, the
Rift Insurrection exposes the fault lines in Spellslate society. Sorin's
manifesto, The Shackled Hand, continues to circulate underground. Year
1170 - The Planar Concords Spellslate magisters broker an unprecedented
treaty with planar entities, creating Planar Concords that allow stable,
long-term manipulation of extraplanar energies. Riftforges become safer,
and Spellslate's ethereal spires grow even larger, reaching into the
upper atmosphere of resource-rich worlds. Neighboring nations accuse
Spellslate of exploiting planar resources irresponsibly. Year 1400 - The
Silent Echelon Crisis A Riftforge in the capital of Auroryx Prime
destabilizes catastrophically, unleashing planar rifts that consume half
the city. Thousands perish in an instant, and those who survive are
horribly altered, becoming Echoborn---beings half-attuned to the planar
chaos. The Silent Echelon Crisis shakes the nation's confidence, forcing
magisters to scale back experimentation.

Year 1600 - The Riftbound Manifesto In response to growing concerns
about planar overreach, radical mages known as the Riftbound push for
total abandonment of planar magic. While dismissed at first, their
movement gains traction among NMP communities and anti-magocratic
dissidents. Year 1770 - Joining the Galactic League of States Spellslate
joins the Galactic League of States as a neutral center for research and
diplomacy, though it faces ongoing scrutiny for its arcane practices.
Despite this, their Riftforges and magical technologies provide critical
support to interstellar infrastructure. Year 1820 - The Second Rift
Insurrection NMP revolutionaries, secretly bolstered by neighboring
factions wary of Spellslate's dominance, launch a coordinated uprising
across several systems. The rebellion is suppressed with brutal
efficiency by the Etherguard, but the magisters are forced to enact
reforms, offering limited political rights to the NMP underclass. In
1851 In 1851, Spellslate remains a beacon of arcane power and
technological mastery, but its society is more fragile than ever. The
ethereal cities of the magisters float serenely above industrialized
worlds, where Riftforges hum with planar energy. Spellslate's mage elite
continue to dominate the Arcane Consensus, but cracks have formed in
their once-absolute rule. The Second Rift Insurrection has granted the
NMP underclass a measure of representation, though many still labor in
resentment under the magisters' shadow. The Echoborn, survivors of
planar anomalies, have emerged as a new social class---half-mage,
half-chaos---demanding recognition and rights. Despite its internal
struggles, Spellslate remains a technological and magical powerhouse,
supplying Riftforge technology and ethereal infrastructure to the wider
galaxy. However, many wonder how long the Arcane Consensus can hold, as
whispers of revolution and planar instability haunt the future of this
shining, fragile empire. Star Tsars of Zandrov Nation in early 3E In the
early Third Era, the Star Tsars were a fractured people fleeing the
chaos of the Titanworld. Their faith in Core's purifying flames united
them as they navigated the perils of space, settling in the Zandrov
Cluster and establishing a feudal society under Tsarist rule. The
Catastrophe of Esnen, fresh in their memory, shaped their traditions of
antimagic and heavy militarization, with power armor and Core's clergy
at the heart of their new nation. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Star
Tsars had become a stable but insular member of the galactic community,
their antimagic technologies and Inferno Guards securing their borders.
Annual Remembrance Day ceremonies reinforced national pride, while their
alliances, like the Steel Forged Compact with the Fire Bishoprics,
ensured their influence in galactic politics. However, tensions simmered
between religious and secular factions, threatening to disrupt their
unity. From 851 to 1851 900 - The First Remembrance Day

The first Remembrance Day is held, with Tsar Alexei recounting the story
of his ancestor who defended Esnen during the Catastrophe of 132 3E. The
event becomes a national tradition, fostering unity and reverence for
the sacrifices of the past. 1000 - The Steel Forged Compact The Star
Tsars formalize alliances with the Fire Bishoprics of Octoria, a fellow
Core-worshipping nation, establishing the Steel Forged Compact. This
pact guarantees mutual defense and trade between the two nations, though
it also entangles the Star Tsars in future conflicts. 1080 - The Titan's
Eye Massacre When a malfunctioning antimagic device activates in New
Esnen, a secondary capital of the nation, it wipes out the city's elite
guard and devastates its infrastructure. Saboteurs linked to Orrin
worshippers infiltrate the system, spreading chaos. This event ignites a
purge of "death sympathizers" and solidifies the Star Tsars' antimagic
focus. 1150 - The Forge Vanguard Initiative A significant leap in
technology occurs as Zandrov scientists develop the Forge Vanguard, a
class of power armor equipped with integrated antimagic emitters. These
suits, deployed among the Inferno Guards, enhance the military's ability
to counter death magic and cement their reputation as elite warriors.
Reinforcement of antimagic zones in key cities and development of
portable antimagic field generators becomes a common focus in society.
\_1203 - The Emberlight Accord To stabilize their economy after decades
of endless conflict, the Star Tsars initiate a trade pact with the Fire
Bishoprics of Octoria. While initially successful, cultural disputes and
differing interpretations of Core's doctrines cause the Accord to
dissolve violently, leading to border skirmishes. As a result public
discontent rises; antimagic policies intensify to suppress dissent.
1400 - The Cleansing of Hollowspire A rogue necromantic sect takes
control of the Hollowspire System, threatening the Zandrov Cluster. The
Inferno Guards lead a brutal campaign to reclaim the system, using
antimagic zones to neutralize the undead. Though successful, the
campaign deepens the cultural divide between religious zealots and
secular factions within Zandrov. 1400 - The Cleansing of Hollowspire An
experimental antimagic dreadnought detonates near the frontline in the
war with Karak Ankor'dûm. The explosion consumes a star system, leaving
behind "Vyrnsk's Hollow," an antimagic void. Rumors spread that Core's
fire priests were involved in sabotaging the ship to weaken Zandrov's
war machine. This results in the founding of the Hollow Fleet, an elite
battalion that operates in antimagic voids of society. \_1670 -
Membership before the League Influenced by external forces, the Tsars
agree to join the martial alliance that was accepting nations from
across the galaxy. Many citizens question this decision, as it featured
disbelievers and those that may have ill intent. 1700 - The War of Karak
Bakhmut Framed as a mission to liberate Core-worshipping minorities, the
Star Tsars and the Fire Bishoprics of Octoria invade Dwarven space. The
war, intended as a swift campaign, devolves into a brutal conflict
against the Dwarven Skyforged Armada and Rune-mechs. The war's high
casualty rate and lack of progress strain the Star Tsars' resources and
deepen internal dissent. 1750 - The Fractured Concord The prolonged war
with Karak Ankor'dûm leads to the Fractured Concord, a period of
political upheaval as rival Tsars accuse the central government of
mismanagement. While the Head of State retains power, the cracks in
Zandrov's unity widen, and calls for reform grow louder.

1851 - The Embers of Resistance By 1851, the Star Tsars of Zandrov
remain a formidable but troubled nation. Their power armor and antimagic
technologies are unmatched, and their soldiers are revered across the
galaxy. However, the strain of prolonged conflict, combined with
cultural and political divisions, threatens to destabilize their
society. The nation stands at a crossroads, torn between its legacy of
fire and its need for renewal. In 1851 By 1851, the Star Tsars of
Zandrov are a nation under strain. Decades of war with the Dwarven Karak
Ankor'dûm have drained resources and exposed cracks in their feudal
hierarchy. Their heavy reliance on antimagic technologies and
power-armored warriors maintains their strength, but internal divisions
grow as reformists challenge the old Tsarist order. Despite these
challenges, the Star Tsars remain a potent force in the galaxy, defined
by their fiery faith and unyielding resolve. Stars of the Triprophets
Nation in early 3E At the dawn of the Third Era, the orc worlds that
would form the Stars of the Triprophets were chaotic and fragmented. The
teachings of the Triprophets, three orc champions who had fought
alongside holy orders in the Second Era, brought order and unity. Their
scriptures emphasized discipline, honor, and divine wisdom, leading to a
cultural transformation that elevated the orcs into a faith-driven and
educated society. With this civilized way of life, they managed to
acquire passage to the galaxy by defending the weak and bartering with
those they would save. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Stars of the
Triprophets had become a thriving interstellar nation, known for their
unique blend of faith, reason, and martial discipline. The Golden Star
Paladins patrolled their borders, acting as both defenders and holy
warriors, while the Scholarly Orders of Elross fostered one of the
highest education rates in the galaxy. Their paladins, adorned with
golden tattoos of holy scriptures and ancestral lines, were symbols of
devotion and strength. As a respected member of the Galactic League of
States, the Stars often served as mediators and voices of reason in
galactic affairs. From 851 to 1851 851 - The Teachings of the
Triprophets Spread The three Triprophets---mighty orc champions who had
fought alongside holy orders in the Second Era---are canonized as divine
heralds of Elross. Their teachings, focusing on discipline, honor, and
divine learning, spread across orc-dominated worlds. A holy council,
known as the Starborn Synod, codifies their scriptures, establishing a
united religious framework. 950 - The Golden Lineage of the Paladins The
practice of tattooing holy scriptures and ancestral lines with gold ink
becomes a sacred ritual for orcish paladins of the Elrossi faith, citing
the practice existing in the second era among the orcs that would stand
against the Malvrick aligned orcs. It was a practice that was still
widespread before this, but now it had become almost a standard.
Warriors spend decades mastering both martial and theological knowledge,
earning the honor of Golden Markings that reflect their life's devotion.
This unique practice becomes a defining cultural tradition, admired
across the galaxy.

1000 - The Scholarly Orders of Elross The Stars of the Triprophets
establish the Scholarly Orders of Elross, a network of universities and
seminaries dedicated to preserving knowledge, scripture, and
advancements in science and theology. Education becomes a cornerstone of
orcish society, fostering a new generation of scholar-warriors and
theologians. The Stars gain recognition as one of the most literate and
educated orc nations. 1100 - The Cleansing of the Iron Reavers A rogue
orc faction, known as the Iron Reavers, rejects the teachings of the
Triprophets and begins raiding neighboring systems. The Golden Star
Crusade is declared, as holy paladins and fleets descend upon the Iron
Reavers, annihilating their forces and reclaiming their worlds. The
event reinforces the Stars' reputation as holy protectors of their faith
and allies. 1250 - The Astral Relic Dispute A minor conflict arises
between the Stars of the Triprophets and the Jade Pact over a disputed
Yuan-Ti relic discovered on a distant world. The relic, believed to hold
immense religious significance, leads to tense negotiations. The Stars
ultimately withdraw from conflict, opting to preserve their honor and
reputation as voices of reason within the galactic community. 1300 - The
Founding of the Starbound Paladins The Stars of the Triprophets
formalize the creation of the Starbound Paladins, a fleet-bound holy
order tasked with defending the galaxy against rising evils. Equipped
with blessed ships inscribed with holy markings, they patrol dangerous
regions, often intervening in conflicts to preserve peace and stability.
Their presence further elevates the Stars as protectors of the faithful.
1450 - The Burning Rift Conflict A tear in space known as the Burning
Rift opens near the Stars' borders, releasing waves of infernal
creatures tied to Carrigore's influence. The Starbound Paladins lead a
grueling 50-year campaign to cleanse the Rift, sealing it with divine
magic and sacrificial heroism. The Martyrs of the Rift are honored in
scripture, their names added to the holy texts and their sacrifices
marked in gold ink. 1725 - Joining the Galactic League of States The
Stars of the Triprophets join the Galactic League of States as a
welcomed and respected member. Their reputation for wisdom, reason, and
impartiality in disputes earns them seats on several councils. Their
leaders often act as mediators in galactic conflicts, offering guidance
grounded in faith and discipline. 1750 - The Pilgrimage Wars A series of
religious uprisings occur within fringe colonies as heretical prophets
claim to reinterpret the teachings of the Triprophets. Known as the
Pilgrimage Wars, these conflicts threaten to destabilize the Stars. The
Starborn Synod responds decisively, quelling the uprisings while
reaffirming the original teachings. The Doctrine of the Eternal Stars is
issued, declaring the Triprophets' wisdom eternal and immutable. 1800 -
The Silver Zenith Doctrine The Silver Zenith Doctrine is adopted,
encouraging further advancements in education, technology, and space
travel while maintaining strict adherence to the Triprophets' teachings.
This balance of progress and faith solidifies the Stars as an
influential and stable cultural power. 1851 - A Nation of Faith, Reason,
and Strength By 1851, the Stars of the Triprophets stand as a beacon of
orcish achievement. Their educated and unified society thrives, with the
Golden Star Paladins defending their borders and patrolling the galaxy
as protectors of the faithful. Their influence in the Galactic League
remains strong, with their leaders often sought after as voices of
wisdom and

impartiality. While external pressures persist, the Stars' unwavering
devotion to their faith and traditions ensures their resilience and
continued growth. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Stars of the Triprophets
remain a shining example of orcish civilization, combining religious
devotion, intellectual achievement, and military strength. Their society
thrives under the teachings of the Triprophets, with the Starborn Synod
overseeing the spiritual and political direction of the nation. The
Golden Star Paladins continue to defend their borders and uphold the
faith, while the Silver Zenith Doctrine ensures technological and
educational progress. The Stars' influence within the Galactic League of
States remains significant, as their leaders offer guidance and balance
in an ever-chaotic galaxy. Stellar Compact Nation in early 3E The East
Moldic Town Compact was founded as a coalition of small industrial towns
in the Moldic Marshlands of Varrak, where harsh conditions demanded
ingenuity and cooperation. Relying on their mastery of dieselpunk
technology, the Compact constructed mechanized defenses and waterborne
transports to navigate the treacherous marshes. In 130, the
Rot-Rising---a sudden and cataclysmic undead incursion---forced the
Compact to flee their homeland. Crossing the seas to Harroland, they
renamed themselves The Compact, establishing a new industrial base.
However, the hostility of neighboring nations turned survival into a
brutal struggle. Though their dieselpunk war machines gave them an edge,
the Compact's population dwindled under constant attack. By the late
early Third Era, their survival seemed uncertain, but their
determination to adapt and endure set the stage for their future in the
stars. Nation in 851 By 851, the Stellar Compact had transformed into a
spacefaring nation, leveraging salvaged Second Era technology to build a
fleet of dieselpunk-inspired starships. Their systems, scattered across
the edges of the Titancore, were industrial powerhouses producing
weapons, fuel, and hybridized technologies. The Compact's society
revolved around salvage expeditions, with Salvage Commanders leading
dangerous missions into ancient ruins to recover vital resources.
However, the Compact's resilience masked underlying fragility. Their
population remained small, their governance fractured between industrial
magnates and military leaders, and their territory vulnerable to
external threats. Their decision to expand into contested regions drew
the ire of powerful neighbors. Yet, the Compact's reputation for
ingenuity and survival ensured they remained a force to be reckoned
with, even in the face of inevitable conflict. From 1 to 1851 Year 1 -
the Founding of the East Moldic Town Compact Formed in the isolated
Moldic Marshlands of Varrak, the East Moldic Town Compact was a
coalition of small industrial towns bound by mutual defense pacts and a
shared reliance on dieselpunk technology. Despite a lack of natural
resources, the Compact thrived on mechanical ingenuity and salvage
engineering, developing crude but effective weaponry and transport
systems. Year 130 - the Rot-Rising

The Rot-Rising begins as legions of the dead claw their way out of the
marshlands, wreaking havoc across the continent of Varrak. The East
Moldic Town Compact's defenses, bolstered by mechanized walkers and
rudimentary flamethrowers, slow the undead hordes but cannot stop them.
Facing annihilation, the Compact abandons their homeland and flees
across the seas to Harroland. Year 131 - the Migration to Harroland
Arriving in Harroland, the Compact renames itself simply The Compact and
establishes a new industrial heartland in the fertile river valleys.
However, neighboring nations view the Compact as interlopers and attack
them relentlessly. While the Compact's dieselpunk technology gives them
an edge, they lack the manpower to hold their gains and are forced into
a slow retreat. Year 350 - the Siege of Karvich The Compact's capital of
Karvich endures a year-long siege by a coalition of hostile neighbors.
The Compact's Steelheart Automata, diesel-powered war machines, hold the
line long enough for the population to evacuate. Though the city falls,
the event cements the Compact's reputation for ingenuity and resilience.
Year 550 - the Great Salvage Era Desperate for survival, the Compact
focuses its efforts on salvaging Second Era technology buried in the
Titancore. Their engineers develop techniques to adapt ancient relics
into their existing dieselpunk framework. This era sees the rise of
Salvage Commanders, engineers-turned-military leaders who lead
expeditions into dangerous ruins to recover vital resources. Year 700 -
the Titan Exodus As the Titanworld destabilizes, the Compact
consolidates all their efforts into a mass exodus. Using salvaged Second
Era starships patched together with diesel technology, they construct a
fleet capable of escaping into the stars. By 710, they officially rename
themselves the Stellar Compact, becoming a spacefaring nation. Year
885 - the Arrival of Karim Ibn Al Daveed The Compact's survival is
tested when Karim Ibn Al Daveed, the Stardrinker, invades their borders.
Over five years, Karim wages a brutal campaign, culminating in the
destruction of the Compact's defensive fleet and the consumption of a
star---a catastrophic act that sears his name into galactic infamy. A
third of the Stellar Compact's territory is lost, and the surviving
regions are flooded with Chad Orcs, who establish their own dominions.
Year 1000 - the Compact Reformation Reeling from Karim's invasion, the
Compact restructures under the Iron Pact, a centralized system of
governance that combines corporate hierarchy with military efficiency.
Their new capital, Forgehaven, becomes a hub of innovation, blending
salvaged technology with their dieselpunk roots to rebuild. Year 1500 -
the Era of Scattered Stars The Compact enters a period of quiet
recovery, expanding cautiously into unclaimed systems and solidifying
its borders. The Chad Orc dominions within their former territories
remain hostile neighbors, forcing the Compact to invest heavily in
automated defenses and mining colonies in safer regions. Year 1850 - the
Compact Resilient By 1850, the Stellar Compact has reemerged as a
resilient yet fractured power. Their territory is small but secure,
centered around thriving industrial worlds. While they are no longer a
major player in galactic politics, their engineering marvels and
reputation for survival ensure their continued relevance. In 1851

By 1851, the Stellar Compact had weathered centuries of hardship but
remained a shadow of its former self. The invasion of Karim Ibn Al
Daveed in 885 had devastated their fleet, reduced their territory, and
fractured their society. The Chad Orc dominions that rose from the ruins
of Compact worlds served as a bitter reminder of their losses. Despite
these setbacks, the Compact endured. Their remaining systems, centered
around the industrial capital of Forgehaven, thrived as centers of
engineering innovation. Salvaged relics from the Second Era, combined
with their dieselpunk ingenuity, gave the Compact a niche as producers
of unique hybrid technologies. Though politically marginalized, they
maintained relevance through trade and diplomacy, leveraging their
technological expertise to secure alliances and protect their borders.
The Compact's legacy is one of survival against impossible odds. Though
diminished, they remain a symbol of resilience, proving that even in the
darkest times, ingenuity and determination can forge a path forward.
Sultanate of El'Rûs Rise of a Nation The war-weary systems of Elross
were never prepared for the relentless waves of Chad Orc zealots under
Karim Ibn Al'Daveed. Where diplomacy failed and fleets burned, Karim
imposed a brutal order founded on religious fervor, military discipline,
and pragmatic governance. His imposition of the Dhimmi system created a
layered society where the conquered paid for their survival, while their
children became tools of the Sultanate's strength. The Janissary Corps,
forged in this era, embodied Karim's ethos---unyielding loyalty, brutal
efficiency, and spiritual devotion to Frozt. The Sultanate of El'Rûs
emerged not as a kingdom of Chad Orcs alone but as a fusion of conquered
Elrossi nobility, Dhimmi laborers, and Frozt-worshipping warriors.
Founding to 1851 930 - The Chad Orc Crusade Begins Karim Ibn Al'Daveed
launches his war against the Second Elrossi Imperium, a mighty
interstellar bastion. Waves of Chad Orc hordes and Frozt-worshiping
zealots crash against Elrossi defenses, starting what would become a
centuries-long war. 960 - The Blooded Expanse Karim's forces break
through key Elrossi sectors, capturing vital star systems. Entire
planetary populations are subjugated, with the Dhimmi system imposed,
offering heathens protection in exchange for submission, taxes, and the
surrender of their children for service in the Sultanate's growing
Janissary Corps. 1040 - The Siege of Thalivor The longest siege in the
war lasts fifty years, as Chad Orc forces encircle the Elrossi fortress
world of Thalivor. Starving populations surrender, and Karim converts
Thalivor into a military production hub, training Janissaries and
extracting tribute. 1100 - The Founding of the Sultanate of El'Rûs After
more than two centuries of war, Karim declares victory over a portion of
Elrossi space. The Sultanate of El'Rûs is officially founded,
encompassing star systems stretching across the eastern interstellar
borders of the Second Elrossi Imperium. Karim assumes the title of
Sultan, solidifying Chad Orc dominance and integrating conquered
populations. 1150 - Karim Abdicates the Throne

On the second anniversary of the Sultanate's founding, Karim announces
his abdication, passing the Sultanate to his chosen heir while retaining
direct control over the Janissary Corps. From this point forward, Karim
operates as a shadow defender of the Sultanate, wielding influence
through the military elite. 1200 - The Janissary Uprising A faction of
Janissaries rebels against Sultan Karim's successors, citing corruption
and favoritism. Karim reemerges from his self-imposed isolation,
personally purging the insurrection and restoring discipline. This event
reinforces the primacy of Frozt in the Janissaries' creed. 1330 - The
Deep One Ascendancy The first Deep One Janissary emerges, a descendant
of Karim's founding warriors. These "Blessed Ones" are heralded as
Frozt's chosen, gifted with immense strength and vitality. Over time,
Deep Ones become an elite subsect within the Janissaries, revered as
symbols of Karim's enduring legacy. 1470 - War with the Second Elrossi
Imperium Resumes The Second Elrossi Imperium launches a renewed campaign
to reclaim their lost systems, but the Sultanate of El'Rûs, now heavily
fortified and militarized, holds firm. The Fortress Worlds of El'Rûs,
ringed by orbital defenses and Janissary garrisons, prove impenetrable.
1530 - The Silver Tribute A cultural compromise emerges wherein noble
Elrossi houses pay annual tributes of wealth, firstborn sons, and
daughters to remain autonomous under the Sultanate's rule. Elrossi
daughters in the royal harem begin wielding significant influence,
maneuvering politically to elevate their families' fortunes. 1690 -
Joining the Galactic League of States The Sultanate of El'Rûs officially
joins the Galactic League of States, leveraging its religious and
military dominance to secure recognition. While distrusted by some
nations, its stability and military might ensure it is taken seriously
on the galactic stage. 1745 - The Frost-Sheikh Schism A theological
dispute arises within Frozt-worshiping sects. A radical movement, the
Frozen Sha'ik, calls for purging Dhimmi classes and abandoning cultural
leniency. Though suppressed by the Janissaries, the movement seeds
internal tension within the Sultanate. 1851 - The Eternal Guard By 1851,
the Sultanate of El'Rûs is defined by the strength of its Janissary
Corps and the growing influence of Deep One descendants. The Sultanate
remains a stable power but faces rising internal pressures from
religious schisms and demands for reform among its Dhimmi classes. In
1851 By 1851, the Sultanate of El'Rûs is a powerful and stable
theocratic state, defined by its military tradition and religious
authority. The Janissary Corps remains the cornerstone of its might,
with elite Deep One warriors leading campaigns and guarding the
Sultanate's borders. The Dhimmi classes, though oppressed, form the
backbone of the Sultanate's economy, while noble Elrossi houses maintain
tenuous alliances with the Sultan's court. Internal tensions simmer
beneath the surface. The Frost-Sheikh factions continue to challenge the
Sultanate's cultural leniency, while Dhimmi populations grow restless
under heavy taxation. The Sultanate's Fortress Worlds along the Elrossi
border stand as enduring monuments to Karim Ibn Al'Daveed's legacy---a
warrior-king who forged a nation from blood, faith, and discipline.

Though Karim himself remains unseen, his legend persists. To this day,
it is said that the Janissary Corps marches not only in service to the
Sultan but also under the eternal watch of their founder---the shadow
Sultan who sacrificed conquest for the dream of his children - those of
his blood and not. The Broken Daimyos Nation in early 3E The ancestors
of the Broken Daimyos flee the Titanworld as the Titan Exodus begins in
150 AG. They scatter across star systems, settling a cluster of colonies
where fertile worlds and isolated asteroid belts offer refuge.
Initially, their society flourishes as independent city-states and
agricultural settlements, but a growing need for resources and power
divides them. By 500 AG, the first unification under the Celestial
Empire of Nippon occurs, marked by shared culture, militarized
governance, and reverence for ancestors. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the
Broken Daimyos remain fragmented. The Eighth Sengoku Jidai continues as
dozens of rival daimyos fight over control of systems and orbital
stations. No single leader holds enough power to unite the nation.
Mercenaries from the Shatter Stance and tiefling warlords exploit the
chaos, selling their swords to the highest bidder. Trade with
neighboring nations is limited, as constant skirmishes disrupt supply
lines and infrastructure. Shogun Billiam Infusive Nintendo, a
controversial warlord, is one of many vying for supremacy but holds only
a tenuous grasp on power. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - The Eighth Sengoku
Jidai The Broken Daimyos remain in a state of shattered governance.
Multiple clans vie for control of their holdings across the star
systems, but no single power emerges. Skirmishes flare as factions seek
dominance, but the Eighth Sengoku Jidai drags on without resolution.
Year 920 - Unification of the Eighth Celestial Empire Daimyo Tokugari
Yatsume unites the clans through brilliant diplomacy and brutal warfare,
declaring the reformation of the Celestial Empire of Nippon. Yatsume's
"Celestial Accord" demands loyalty through oaths sworn under bloodmoon
rituals. The Galactic League of States cautiously acknowledges this new
empire, though diplomatic envoys are recalled after infighting resumes.
Year 970 - The Ninth Sengoku Jidai Following Tokugari Yatsume's
assassination under mysterious circumstances, the Celestial Empire
shatters once more. Powerful daimyos begin a period of interstellar
warfare. This Sengoku Jidai is marked by widespread use of mercenary
bands, including hobgoblin stances and rogue tiefling warlords. Year
1025 - Rise of the Silver Blossoms A faction of warrior-monks known as
the Silver Blossoms emerges during the Ninth Sengoku Jidai, promoting
unity through spirituality. Their leader, Dai-Arahime, briefly rallies
dozens of minor clans but is slain in battle. Her death cements the
Silver Blossoms as legendary but ultimately powerless in preventing
further conflict. Year 1100 - Rebirth of the Ninth Celestial Empire The
Ninth Celestial Empire reforms under Shogun Kagehisa Tsubaru, a ruthless
warlord who employs assassins and tiefling mercenaries to conquer rival
daimyos. Though seen as a tyrant, Tsubaru enforces stability for nearly
seventy years.

Year 1170 - First Great Tiefling Purge Growing resentment toward
tiefling mercenaries leads to mass purges within the Celestial Empire.
The Broken Daimyos collectively blame the New Black Republic for
economic instability and rising dissent. Shogun Tsubaru dies
mysteriously, triggering widespread rebellion and the fall of the Ninth
Empire. Year 1240 - The Tenth Sengoku Jidai The longest Sengoku Jidai
begins. Feuding daimyos clash across entire systems, deploying new
technologies such as mech-samurai and voidwalkers---heavily armored
space knights. The Shattered Spires campaign sees orbital stations torn
apart and scorched to ruin. Year 1320 - Brief Unity Under the Council of
Five Clans Five of the most powerful clans form a temporary ruling
council. For forty years, the council prevents open war, but political
assassinations and infighting gradually erode its foundation. The
Galactic League of States denies the Celestial Empire's request for
membership, citing instability. Year 1400 - The Shogunate of Kurozane
Daimyo Kurozane claims the title of Celestial Shogun, defeating dozens
of rivals. His reign is marked by economic reform and a focus on
rebuilding devastated systems. The Celestial Empire experiences relative
peace for the next century. Year 1515 - War with the New Black Republic
The Benthic Tieflings of the New Black Republic raid border systems of
the Broken Daimyos, seizing trade convoys and colonies. Propaganda
paints the Tieflings as the ultimate cause of all misfortune. The
conflict unites the clans temporarily, but Kurozane's lineage ends in
infighting after his heir is assassinated. Year 1620 - The Eleventh
Celestial Empire Forms The Eleventh Celestial Empire arises under Shogun
Takeda Hoshimura, a respected strategist. He reintroduces cultural
reforms, emphasizing art, poetry, and mech-samurai warfare as symbols of
the empire's glory. Hoshimura opens negotiations to join the Galactic
League of States but recalls representatives when hostilities resume
among his generals. Year 1839 - The Fall of the Eleventh Empire The
Eleventh Empire collapses in an event known as the Night of the Thousand
Knives, where political assassinations decimate leadership across the
star systems. The perpetrators remain unknown. The death of Shogun
Hoshimura splinters the Celestial Empire once more. Year 1851 - The
Eleventh Sengoku Jidai The Broken Daimyos descend into civil war once
again. The surviving remnants of the Eleventh Celestial Empire cling to
a fragile claim of legitimacy under Shogun John Redcorn. Warlords scour
systems with mech-armored armies and mercenaries, while millions perish
in the crossfire. In 1851 In 1851 AG, the Broken Daimyos enter the
Eleventh Sengoku Jidai following the collapse of the Eleventh Celestial
Empire. The death of Shogun Takeda Hoshimura during the Night of the
Thousand Knives leaves a power vacuum that spirals into war. Shogun John
Redcorn, leads a desperate campaign to restore unity, but rival daimyos
challenge his authority at every turn. Advanced mech-samurai patrol
shattered orbital stations, while entire fleets clash among the stars.

Many blame the Benthic Tieflings of the New Black Republic for inciting
this collapse, further fueling hostility toward external forces.
Neighboring nations, such as Pillwake and the Stellar Compact, remain
wary of the Broken Daimyos' instability. Still, amidst the chaos,
whispers circulate of several potential unifiers--- unknown figures
rising from the shadows to reclaim the Celestial Throne. The
Excommunicated Houses Rise of a Nation The Excommunicated Houses were
born out of rebellion, deceit, and an open rejection of the Second
Elrossi Imperium's faith and hierarchy. These rebellious noble families,
many of whom had secretly aligned with Vy'esh, the god of death, had
long cultivated vampiric bloodlines in the shadows. As their influence
grew, they established covens that spanned generations, embedding
themselves in the political and cultural structures of the Imperium. By
the year 1300, these houses openly declared their independence, rallying
under the banner of death worship and their claim to lineage from
Vy'esh's divine family. Their rebellion plunged the Second Elrossi
Imperium into chaos, but it ended in a stalemate. The Imperium lacked
the strength to crush the Excommunicated Houses outright, and the
rebellious nobles lacked the unity to deal a decisive blow. By 1315, the
Imperium chose isolation as its strategy, severing all ties with the
rebelling sectors and formally excommunicating the houses. Left to their
own devices, the Excommunicated Houses forged a nation in the fragmented
remains of their rebellion, carving out territories in the galactic
northwest, southwest, and east of the Imperium. Their society is steeped
in death worship and ruled by vampiric covens that hide their true
nature behind a thin veneer of aristocracy. While vampirism is an open
secret, few dare to question the rule of their undead lords. The nation
thrives on intrigue, theocratic devotion to Vy'esh, and an economy
driven by blood tithes, necromantic labor, and dark sorcery. Despite
their fractured geography, the Excommunicated Houses have built a web of
alliances and intrigue that holds their nation together. Founding to
1851 Year 1300 - Declaration of Independence A coalition of noble houses
across the Imperium simultaneously declares their independence,
denouncing the Elrossi faith as a false doctrine. They openly proclaim
allegiance to Vy'esh and reveal their vampiric heritage. Year 1301 - The
Bloody Veil Uprising The Excommunicated Houses rise in open rebellion,
but their forces are poorly coordinated. The Imperium launches brutal
counterattacks, resulting in catastrophic losses for both sides. Year
1315 - The Pact of Shadows After years of fighting, the rebellion ends
in a stalemate. The Second Elrossi Imperium isolates the rebelling
sectors, severing all official ties. The excommunicated territories
unite under the Pact of Shadows, formalizing their allegiance to Vy'esh
and their vampiric governance. Year 1340 - The Silent Court Formed The
Excommunicated Houses establish the Silent Court, a ruling council of
vampiric lords who arbitrate disputes and enforce allegiance among the
covens. This stabilizes the fractured governance of their isolated
regions. Year 1400 - Rise of the Blood Tithes Necromantic labor replaces
traditional agriculture and industry in the Excommunicated Houses. The
population is required to pay blood tithes, fueling the vampiric
aristocracy's power and securing their rule.

Year 1500 - The Shadow Plague A vampiric disease spreads across one of
thenorthern sectors, mutating undead servants into ravenous creatures.
The Silent Court declares martial law, and the plague is eventually
contained, but the region remains scarred and underdeveloped. Year
1600 - The Reclamation of Blood Facing an internal crisis as fragmented
noble houses grow too independent, the Silent Court demands absolute
loyalty from the aristocracy. A series of bloody purges eliminates
dissenting covens, uniting the fractured territories under tighter
control. Year 1680 - Necromantic Expansion The Excommunicated Houses
expand their necromantic capabilities, raising vast armies of undead
servants and soldiers. Their reliance on death magic makes them a
feared, if loathed, force among neighboring nations. Year 1700 - Trade
Wars with the Imperium Illicit trade routes between the Excommunicated
Houses and discontented factions within the Imperium spark a series of
trade wars. While the Imperium disrupts some supply lines, the vampiric
nation's reliance on necromantic labor cushions the economic impact.
Year 1750 - The Blood Accord The Excommunicated Houses negotiate a
tenuous Blood Accord with Carrigore Space, exchanging necromantic
expertise and cursed relics for military aid and support against Elrossi
incursions. This marks the first major external alliance for the
rebellious houses. Year 1820 - The Southern Revolt The southern sector
experiences a populist uprising as mortal citizens, tired of blood
tithes and necromantic servitude, rebel against their vampiric
overlords. The rebellion is crushed, but unrest spreads to the eastern
and northern sectors. Year 1850 - Shadows of Stability The Silent Court
enacts reforms to quell growing discontent, granting limited autonomy to
mortal governors while preserving vampiric dominance. Though their
borders remain secure, internal divisions threaten their long-term
stability. In 1851 By 1851, the Excommunicated Houses remain a fractured
yet enduring nation defined by intrigue, necromancy, and their
allegiance to Vy'esh. Spread across three isolated territories in the
northwest, southwest, and eastern edges of the Second Elrossi Imperium,
the Houses maintain their rule through a combination of fear, dark
magic, and carefully managed alliances. The Silent Court, composed of
the most powerful vampiric lords, holds the nation together, arbitrating
disputes and ensuring loyalty among the covens. The blood tithe system
remains the foundation of their economy, sustaining their undead
aristocracy and fueling necromantic armies. Necromantic labor ensures
production continues efficiently, but growing discontent among mortal
citizens poses a constant threat. The populist uprising of 1820 left
scars, and the Silent Court has since granted limited power to mortal
governors in an attempt to pacify the masses. Externally, the
Excommunicated Houses are viewed with suspicion and fear. While they
have established a tenuous alliance with Carrigore Space, their trade
wars with the Imperium continue to define their external relations.
Their necromantic expertise and vampiric rulers make them a formidable,
if controversial, player on the galactic stage.

The Excommunicated Houses endure, balancing their dark heritage with the
need for stability. While their fragmented geography poses challenges,
the vampiric aristocracy remains committed to preserving their
sovereignty and ensuring the legacy of Vy'esh in a galaxy that views
them as pariahs. The Old Horde Nation in early 3E The Old Horde begins
as a brutal and disjointed collection of barbaric races---Minotaurs,
Orcs, Lizardfolk, and others---who worship the titan Orrin. Their
society values strength, dominance, and conquest above all else. On the
Titanworld, the Horde wages constant war against their neighbors,
expanding their influence through sheer ferocity. United only by the
promise of plunder and Orrin's divine favor, the Horde's leaders rise
and fall in bloody succession. When the Titan Exodus begins, the Horde
scatters across the stars, stealing ships and enslaving engineers to
escape into the galaxy. Many settle in the Border Systems, drawn by
Orrin's whispers to seek relics and resources in the stars. Nation in
851 By 851, the Old Horde is at the height of its power, led by Gorak
the Unrelenting, a Minotaur warlord who unites the clans under a vision
of conquest. Gorak declares war on the Silkstrider Realm, promising his
followers wealth, slaves, and glory. The Horde's brutal raids devastate
the Silkstrider borderlands, but as the war drags on, cracks begin to
form. Gorak's reliance on mercenary forces and tribute payments from
vassal states sustains the war effort, but resentment festers among the
clans. While the Silkstrider Realm's time magic and archery exact a
heavy toll, Gorak's charisma and unyielding will keep the Horde from
fracturing---at least for now. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - The
Unrelenting War Under the iron-fisted leadership of Gorak the
Unrelenting, the Old Horde wages a brutal war against the Silkstrider
Realm. Gorak, a towering Minotaur warlord, promises his followers untold
riches and legions of slaves to fuel their empire. While early victories
see the Silkstrider borderlands ravaged, the war soon falters. The
Horde's lack of technological advancement and organized logistics forces
Gorak to rely on mercenary armies, funded by an expanding network of
tributary states. Gorak's reputation as a ruthless but charismatic
leader maintains the Horde's cohesion despite mounting losses. Year
880 - The Tribute Wars Facing economic strain from the prolonged
conflict, Gorak institutes the Tribute Wars, campaigns to subjugate
neighboring states for resources and tribute. Minor nations along the
Silkstrider Realm's periphery fall to the Horde's relentless raids,
while internal dissent grows among the Lizardfolk tribes and Orc clans,
many of whom resent Gorak's mercenary reliance. Year 920 - Battle of the
Wyrmscar Peaks A turning point comes when the Silkstrider Realm ambushes
a major Horde force in the Wyrmscar Peaks. Despite their superior
numbers, the Horde suffers catastrophic losses due to the Drider's time
magic and precision archery. Gorak himself narrowly escapes, but his
aura of invincibility begins to crack. In the aftermath, several Orc
clans splinter off, refusing to pay further tribute. Year 960 - The
Mercenary Fracture With Gorak aging and weakened, discontent reaches a
boiling point among the mercenary companies and vassal states that
sustain the war effort. A notable rebellion led by the Skullsplitter
Clan erupts, refusing to fight further for

a losing cause. While Gorak crushes the rebellion, the internal
fractures deepen. More vassal states quietly cease their payments, and
mercenary leaders abandon the field, leaving the Old Horde increasingly
desperate. Year 1000 - The Collapse of the Old Horde The death of
Gorak's last grandson, Karnak the Red, marks the final unraveling of the
Old Horde. With no clear successor to unite the clans, the once-mighty
nation splinters into warring factions. Chief among these are the
Ironfang Dominion, a militant Minotaur-led faction, and the Bloodscale
Kin, a loose coalition of Lizardfolk warbands. The Silkstrider Realm
consecrates their victory with the founding of the Eternal Loom, and the
war officially ends. Across the Old Horde's former territory, chaos
reigns as clans, tribes, and warlords carve out their domains. Year
1100 - Rise of the Ironfang Dominion The Ironfang Dominion emerges as a
dominant force in the aftermath of the Old Horde's collapse. Led by the
warlord Tharnak the Ironhide, the Minotaur faction reclaims swathes of
former Horde territory, subjugating rival clans under a brutal regime.
The Bloodscale Kin and other splinter factions engage in constant border
skirmishes. Year 1400 - The Ashen Treaties After centuries of
intermittent warfare, the fractured remnants of the Old Horde sign the
Ashen Treaties, a loose agreement to establish borders and trade routes.
While the Ironfang Dominion maintains dominance, it faces growing
pressure from neighboring nations and Silkstrider-backed rebellions.
Year 1851 - Forgotten Fury By 1851, the Old Horde's legacy is one of a
scattered and weakened people. Though their descendants still roam the
galaxy in warbands, they lack the unity that once made the Horde a
feared power. Relics of their conquests remain scattered across the
Border Systems and Silkstrider territory, coveted by historians and
adventurers. The Ironfang Dominion and Bloodscale Kin remain locked in
petty rivalries, their glory days little more than myth. In 1851 By
1851, the Old Horde exists only as a fractured legacy. The Ironfang
Dominion rules over a portion of the former Horde's territory with an
iron fist, their Minotaur warlords imposing harsh order through might
and fear. The Bloodscale Kin, a loose confederation of Lizardfolk
tribes, cling to survival through raiding and guerrilla warfare. Smaller
factions, including rogue Orc warbands and nomadic tribes, wander the
galaxy as mercenaries and scavengers. Relics of the Old Horde's
conquests---ancient weapons, banners, and Orrinic idols---are sought
after as treasures of a bygone era. The Silkstrider Realm's consecrated
victory stands as a grim reminder of the Horde's fall, while their
former territory remains a patchwork of warlord states and lawless
regions. Though Orrin's worship persists among the clans, the Old
Horde's glory is now little more than a tale told around warcamp fires.
Of the Old Horde, all that was left by this year were the Stars of
Barbarism. The Order Chronothalius Nation in early 3E The Order
Chronothalius emerges from the devastation of the First Era as the most
enigmatic survivors of Orrin's Grip. While others battle for land,
power, and escape, the Order withdraws entirely, founding their
sanctuaries in regions untouched by war - a large ship carrying a colony
of it's practitioners ventured out into the universe. Guided by their
ageless founder, they establish the Time Vaults of Chronothalius,
beginning their eternal mission: to preserve time and protect the
universe from the Titans' grasp. The Order builds a society of paladins,
mages, monks, and rangers dedicated to time's flow---uninterested in
conquest but obsessed with safeguarding the galaxy's future.

Nation in 851 By 851, the Order Chronothalius is both feared and revered
as a silent guardian of history. Their enclaves hover in the depths of
space, surrounded by shimmering Temporal Wards that distort reality for
anyone attempting entry. Their presence in the galaxy is felt but rarely
seen---small, masked envoys negotiate with the Galactic League of
States, refusing membership but treating the League as a single entity
for diplomacy. The Time Vaults of Chronothalius are the stuff of legend:
rumored to imprison horrors, rogue gods, and relics too dangerous for
mortal hands. Many wonder what the Order knows of the Titans and why
they dedicate themselves to keeping time's flow safe. Their
interventions are subtle but undeniable, nudging destiny ever away from
cataclysm. From 851 to 1851 Year 851 - The Veil of Time The Order
Chronothalius emerges in whispered tales as the most enigmatic force in
the galaxy. Remaining isolated in their gilded temporal bastions, they
neither conquer nor expand, their focus locked on the tides of time
itself. Hidden deep within their secluded systems, the Time Vaults of
Chronothalius become legend---said to house relics, entities, and
atrocities too dangerous for the universe to bear. Diplomats from the
Galactic League of States travel under strict agreements to petition for
favors or counsel, but are rarely granted entrance beyond the surface of
their outer sanctums. The Order, led by its ageless founder, intervenes
subtly in galactic events, nudging flows of destiny away from the
Titans' grasp. Year 950 - The Riftbound Purge Reports surface of
fissures in time erupting across a barren system on the galactic fringe.
The Order responds swiftly, their agents appearing in ships that phase
in and out of existence. Observers describe "crystalline stasis fields"
that consume entire rift anomalies and lock them in place. The Order
declares the Riftbound Purge complete, warning that continued tampering
with reality may force their hand against unnamed factions experimenting
with time. Whispers abound that the anomalies were fragments of Titan
influence, and that the Order sealed the breaches before the Titans
could gaze outward. Year 1075 - The Shifting Edict The Galactic League
of States accuses the Order Chronothalius of temporal interference after
seventeen failed treaties mysteriously collapse over decades. The
Order's response, delivered through masked envoys, is a single message:
"The threads of fate frayed too dangerously." While suspicions linger,
no League power dares antagonize the Order directly. Many believe this
moment marks the beginning of the League viewing the Order as an
untouchable, unknowable entity beyond conventional politics. Year 1250 -
The Time Vault Crisis An anomaly breaches containment within the Time
Vaults, unleashing horrors from stasis into the Order's core sanctum.
Temporal quakes ripple through nearby systems, collapsing local
timelines into overlapping loops. Entire days repeat across border
colonies until the Order restores balance. To observers, the event lasts
seconds, but for the Order, it is said to have taken centuries to
resolve. Afterward, the Order strengthens the Vaults' wards and
establishes new layers of stasis protocols, ensuring no entity---no
matter how powerful---can escape its confinement again. Year 1400 - The
Displacement Treaty For reasons known only to the Order, entire
populations of endangered systems are discreetly transported across
sectors. Galactic League records reveal no evidence of ships or fleets,
only that the populations vanish overnight, relocated to thriving colony
worlds. When questioned, the Order's emissaries state: "This was their
appointed hour of survival." Some leaders call this "playing god," but
no one can deny the displaced populations prosper in their new homes.

Year 1600 - The Eternity Accord The Order Chronothalius intervenes in
the collapse of several minor nations near the Devoid Systems,
preventing their fall into chaotic anarchy. While never revealing their
methods, observers claim time itself was rewritten in these systems,
staving off collapse for decades. The League demands explanations but
receives none. The event cements the Order's mythic status, as some
suspect they now guide the broader flow of history on an imperceptible
scale. Year 1700 - The Anachron Battle A rogue experiment in time magic
by a fringe faction causes a temporal cascade that spreads into the
Border Systems. For the first time, the Order Chronothalius deploys its
military might: The Stasis Guard, a force of knights clad in shimmering
armor that bends light and time around them, descend upon the region.
The cascade is halted, but a significant portion of the affected star
system is sealed permanently in Temporal Null, where no time flows. The
Order allows no one near this dead zone, fueling speculation that a
Titan's influence was involved. After several decades, the Stasis Guard
vanishes and the Star System is nowhere to be found on any galactic
charts. Year 1850 - The Veiled Warning The Order Chronothalius sends
envoys to the Galactic League of States with a simple, cryptic warning:
"The hourglass wanes. Be vigilant." The meaning of this message is
unclear, but the League calls for unified readiness against threats both
known and unknown. In the shadows, powerful factions debate whether the
Order senses a coming catastrophe or merely seeks to frighten the galaxy
into submission. In 1851 In 1851, the Order Chronothalius stands as a
galactic paradox: a nation without borders, a force without armies, and
a power beyond comprehension. Their isolation remains unbroken, their
floating sanctuaries cloaked in shimmering temporal fields. The galaxy
regards them with a mixture of awe and suspicion---stories of the Stasis
Guard and the Time Vaults inspire both dread and reverence. The Order's
founder, whose identity has been erased from history, is still believed
to lead them, an immortal figure guiding their inscrutable mission.
While others war and expand, the Order watches, nudging time away from
cataclysm. Their message to the galaxy remains cryptic: "Preserve the
flow, or lose everything." For many, the question lingers---what future
do they see, and what price will the galaxy pay to avoid it? The
Q'Xorlarrin Domain Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, the
Q'Xorlarrin Domain remained a scattered collection of Shadar'Kai and
Drow enclaves seeking survival amid the chaos of the Titan Exodus. Their
faith in Maev'Rey, the Raven Queen, guided them through the turmoil, as
the priestly caste wielded divine power to unify the drow under a rigid
caste system. Their settlements on the Titanworld were shadowy,
void-saturated cities, where the Talon Guard enforced order and
Maev'Rey's will. From these enclaves, the Q'Xorlarrin focused on
creating stability amid the chaos of Orrin's Grip, though the seeds of
internal dissent were already present. Nation in 851 By 851, the
Q'Xorlarrin Domain had established itself as a dominant power within the
elven realms, enforcing strict isolation from non-elven nations under
Gro'May's vassalage rules. Their void-enhanced espionage and mastery of
shadowy underworld tactics made them both feared and respected. The
Talon Guard, enigmatic and zealous enforcers of the faith, acted as both
protectors and secret police, ensuring the caste system remained
unchallenged.

The Domains' rigid caste structure created a society of hierarchy and
oppression, though cracks began to form as commoners murmured of reform.
Their borders were constantly tested by the Broken Daimyos and raids
from the New Black Republic, forcing the Q'Xorlarrin to maintain a
relentless focus on defense and covert operations. From 851 to 1851 Year
851 - The Ascendant Balance Under the authority of Maev'Rey, the Raven
Queen, the Q'Xorlarrin Domain consolidates its position within the elven
realms. The caste system remains rigid, with the priestly caste wielding
immense influence under the guidance of the Talon Guard, enigmatic
protectors rumored to be void-imbued entities. The Domains maintain
strict adherence to the Eternal King Gro'May's vassalage rules, banning
diplomacy with non-elven powers and focusing their efforts on espionage
and religious expansion. Year 950 - The Shadow Concord The Q'Xorlarrin
Domain and the Cohorts of Thae'Viriel sign an agreement solidifying a
mutual understanding regarding espionage activities within elven
territories. This agreement is designed to prevent espionage conflicts
between the two nations while targeting external threats like the New
Black Republic and the Broken Daimyos. Year 1020 - Uprising of the
Commoner Sect A brief but bloody rebellion occurs as commoners, tired of
their immovable status in the caste system, rise up against the noble
caste. The Talon Guard brutally suppresses the rebellion, leaving
thousands dead. This event sparks the first whispers of reform within
the priestly caste, though change remains slow and met with resistance.
Year 1100 - The Silent Tithe Facing increasing raids by the Broken
Daimyos, the Domain establishes an annual tithe paid in rare void-forged
artifacts to ensure minimal interference in their operations. This
temporary solution weakens their influence over several border systems.
Year 1200 - Caledor's Subversion Plan The enigmatic elf Caledor begins a
subtle campaign to destabilize the Q'Xorlarrin caste system from within.
Through propaganda and covert aid to reformist elements, he plants seeds
of discord among the lower castes, particularly in key priestly
enclaves. The Domains, suspicious of external manipulation, increase
internal security and purge suspected conspirators. Year 1400 - The
Fractured Conviction A high-ranking noble within the priestly caste,
Llarinth Var'Qor, attempts to reform the caste system by introducing
limited mobility between castes for extraordinary merit. His proposals
are deemed heretical, and he is executed by the Talon Guard. This
triggers internal schisms, with many questioning the absolute power of
the noble caste. Year 1500 - Espionage against the Sovranty of Sin'dal
The Q'Xorlarrin are implicated in a massive espionage operation
targeting the Sovranty of Sin'dal. The operation is exposed, leading to
strained relations and retaliatory actions by Sin'dal's forces. Despite
the controversy, Maev'Rey worshippers frame the operation as a divine
mission, further consolidating control over dissent within the Domain.
Year 1600 - The Daimyos Warfront The Broken Daimyos briefly unite under
a powerful warlord and launch an assault on Q'Xorlarrin space. The Talon
Guard spearhead the defense, repelling the invasion but at great cost.
This war cements the Domains' reputation as stalwart defenders of elven
space and reinforces their reliance on Maev'Rey's divine guidance. Year
1650 - The Shroud Accord

A secret agreement between the Lord Protector's Court and Q'Xorlarrin
formalizes cooperation in rooting out rogue elements within their shared
borders. While the accord bolsters security, it also creates tension
among other elven states wary of centralized power. Year 1700 -
Assassination of High Priest Var'Duun High Priest Var'Duun, the
spiritual leader of Maev'Rey's faith, is assassinated in a coup led by
reformist nobles. The coup fails as the Talon Guard crushes the
insurrection, but the incident weakens the priestly caste's absolute
authority and forces minor reforms in governance. Year 1800 - The Void
Proclamation The Raven Queen's high priest declares the Void
Proclamation, reaffirming the Domain's focus on spiritual supremacy and
its commitment to remaining outside the Galactic League of States. This
proclamation solidifies their position as an enigmatic, isolationist
power. Year 1850 - The Ascendant Schism Debate over the caste system
ignites a major internal schism. Reformist elements propose dismantling
the caste system entirely, while traditionalists cling to Maev'Rey's
sacred structure. Though the Talon Guard prevents outright civil war,
tension threatens to destabilize the Domain in the near future. In 1851
By 1851, the Q'Xorlarrin Domain stands as an isolated but formidable
power within the elven realms. Their mastery of void magic and espionage
ensures their dominance in covert operations, though their internal
struggles over caste reforms continue to simmer. The Talon Guard remains
a symbol of Maev'Rey's will, suppressing dissent and enforcing divine
order. While their inability to join the Galactic League of States
isolates them diplomatically, it also shields them from foreign
influence. The Domain's future hangs in the balance, as reformist voices
grow louder and external threats from the New Black Republic and Broken
Daimyos test their strength. The Q'Xorlarrin remain steadfast in their
worship of the Raven Queen, determined to uphold her will, even as the
shadows within their society threaten to consume them. The Rusted Legion
Rise of a Nation From the ashes of the Trade League's fallen factory
worlds, General Irgash the Ironbound---a colossal ogre warlord---rose as
a warlord of unmatched brutality and pragmatism. Aided by goblin
machinists and scavengers, Irgash converted derelict industrial zones
into War-Forges, vast factories that churned out crude but deadly
machines of war. By 1085, the Rusted Legion had solidified its power,
rallying smaller ogre warbands and goblin engineers beneath Irgash's
iron fist. The Rusted Legion earned its name from their monstrous
Ironclad Ogres, armored giants wielding salvaged weapons, and their
legions of retrofitted Mech-Breakers, war machines built from industrial
scrap. Karim Ibn Al Daveed's conquest had left the Trade League's
resources ripe for exploitation, and Irgash seized this opportunity to
create a regime where might ruled absolutely. The Rusted Legion thrives
on conquest, raiding neighboring territories for materials, enslaving
populations for labor, and striking fear into nearby Chad Orc warlords
who refuse to bow to their might. Founding to 1851 Year 1085 - The March
of Irgash the Ironbound

From the shattered remains of the Gob-Ogre Trade League's industrial
ruins, General Irgash the Ironbound unites scattered ogre warlords and
goblin machinists under his rule. Declaring strength and conquest as
their salvation, Irgash establishes the Rusted Legion, converting
derelict industrial complexes into massive War-Forges. These factories
churn out crude but effective war machines and retrofitted armor for his
Ironclad Ogres, monstrous warriors clad in salvaged power suits. The
Legion's first conquest begins as they seize neighboring systems,
crushing smaller Chad Orc clans and forcing them into servitude. Year
1100 - The Foundry Wars The Rusted Legion turns its attention to the
outer systems of the Black Cogs Consortium, seeking control of abandoned
mines and war-torn foundries. A brutal war erupts as the Legion's
Mech-Breakers---scrap-constructed war engines---clash with the
Consortium's mercenary Cogblade Enforcers. Despite heavy casualties,
Irgash's forces claim several key industrial zones, though they lack the
expertise to maintain them fully. The Foundry Wars solidify the Legion's
reputation as relentless but resource-poor conquerors. Year 1135 - The
Great Collapse of Khul Tharak Overextension strains the Legion's war
economy when an ambitious goblin warlord, Tarkrim the Gearbane, revolts
in the vital industrial hub of Khul Tharak. Sabotage cripples the
War-Forges, and Tarkrim's forces destroy key infrastructure before being
hunted down. The rebellion marks a turning point, revealing cracks in
the Legion's governance. Irgash's successors struggle to balance control
over the ogre warlords and goblin machinists, who grow resentful under
increasingly tyrannical oversight. Year 1180 - The Chains of Service To
address growing unrest, Warlord Varuk the Binder enacts the Chains of
Service, a brutal system that binds goblin engineers and defeated enemy
populations into indentured servitude. While this restores the
War-Forges' production, it fuels a cycle of cruelty and rebellion.
Entire generations of goblins are born into forced labor, creating
resentment that simmers beneath the Legion's surface. The Chains of
Service become synonymous with the Legion's rule---necessary for
survival, yet a festering wound in their society. Year 1230 - The Siege
of Ashbrun Seeking to expand further into the Inner Straits, the Legion
launches an invasion against a fortified Chad Orc colony allied with the
Emirate of Yashuun. The siege lasts for five brutal years as Yashuun's
Frostblade Legions counterattack with disciplined strikes. The Legion's
forces eventually withdraw after catastrophic losses, marking their
first major defeat. The Siege of Ashbrun becomes a cautionary tale,
teaching future warlords the cost of reckless expansion and the
Emirate's unyielding resolve. Year 1305 - The Forgemaster's Revolt
Discontent among goblin machinists reaches a boiling point when
Forgemaster Grixal the Shatterhand leads a workers' revolt across
several War-Forges. The revolt spreads quickly, crippling production and
throwing the Legion into chaos. Warlord Grugnak Ironjaw suppresses the
rebellion with horrific brutality, executing thousands and instituting
even harsher labor controls. While Grugnak restores order, the
Forgemaster's Revolt permanently fractures the relationship between
goblin workers and ogre warlords. Year 1380 - The Red Foundry Accord
Recognizing the Legion's decline, a coalition of ogre warlords and
goblin overseers convene to reorganize their governance under the Red
Foundry Accord. This treaty establishes a council of Forge-Lords, who
oversee production and resource allocation, while the warlords retain
military authority. Though the Accord stabilizes the Legion's economy,
internal rivalries persist as Forge-Lords and warlords compete for
influence. The Legion's expansion halts, with its leadership focused on
consolidating power. Year 1445 - The Breaking of Khural's Gate The
Rusted Legion faces a catastrophic invasion from an unknown Chad Orc
warband attempting to claim their weakened outer systems. The conflict
culminates in the Breaking of Khural's Gate, a major industrial world
and

defensive bastion. The warbands devastate Legion defenses, leaving
entire regions of space lawless and in ruin. While the invaders
eventually retreat, the Legion's inability to defend its own holdings
further weakens its reputation, sowing distrust among its subject
populations. Year 1510 - The Great Scourge A plague known as the
Ironblight spreads through the Legion's industrial hubs, corroding
machinery and weakening the Ironclad Ogres' salvaged armor. Unable to
contain the outbreak, entire War-Forges are shut down to halt the
contagion. Warlord Tarmog the Cold-Fist declares a ruthless Scourge,
purging infected workers and entire populations to save the Legion.
Though the Scourge halts the plague, it leaves the Legion depleted and
despised by its own subjects. Year 1600 - The Reforging of Irgash's
Chain Desperate to restore the Legion's power, Warlord Thruk the
Ironsinger enacts sweeping reforms known as the Reforging of Irgash's
Chain. Thruk revives the ancient war chants of General Irgash, rallying
the warlords and machinists under a renewed sense of pride and purpose.
New War-Forges are built, and the Ironclad Ogres are rearmed with
salvaged antimagic technology purchased from fringe traders. The reforms
stabilize the Legion, but their recovery remains fragile. Year 1750 -
Reluctant Joining of the Galactic League Under pressure from economic
collapse and external threats, the Rusted Legion begrudgingly joins the
Galactic League of States in 1750. The Legion views the League as a
necessary evil, using their membership to gain access to trade and
technological advancements while opposing resolutions that curtail war
and expansion. The ForgeLords maintain tight control over their systems,
resisting efforts to reform their brutal governance. Year 1851 - A
Hollow Empire of Rust By 1851, the Rusted Legion stands as a battered
but enduring power in the Inner Straits. Its War-Forges continue to
produce weapons of war, though at a reduced capacity, while the Ironclad
Ogres remain fearsome symbols of strength. The Forge-Lords preside over
a fractured society, where labor unrest and warlord rivalries threaten
to tear the Legion apart. Though weakened, the Legion's brutality and
sheer resolve ensure its survival---its worlds smogchoked, its engines
grinding ceaselessly, and its ambitions still burning beneath layers of
rust and ash. In 1851 The Rusted Legion limps into 1851 as a fractured
yet persistent power, its industrial worlds cloaked in choking smog and
its warlords ruling over crumbling fortresses of steel and rust. Once a
brutal rising force under General Irgash the Ironbound, the Legion has
descended into a decentralized collection of feuding warlords and
crumbling War-Forges. Their war economy churns on sheer momentum,
producing crude war machines and salvage-based ships that remain
effective despite their ramshackle design. The Ironclad Ogres, monstrous
warriors clad in patchwork power armor, serve as both enforcers and
symbols of the Legion's enduring might. At the heart of the Legion lies
the ever-feuding Forge-Lords, ruthless leaders who care more for
hoarding resources and power than uniting their people. Constant labor
revolts plague the War-Forges, as generations of indentured goblins and
captured populations suffer under oppressive regimes, kept in line by
brutal Mercenary Enforcers. The Legion's relationship with the Black
Cogs Consortium remains one of wary trade and mutual disdain---while the
Consortium provides the technology the Legion cannot produce itself,
they mock the Legion's reliance on raw brutality. Border conflicts with
the Emirate of Yashuun rage on, their contested zones marred by ruined
colonies and scavenged battlefields. Yet, despite their instability, the
Legion endures. The name "Rusted Legion" strikes fear into the hearts of
weaker factions, and the chaotic ambition of the Forge-Lords ensures
that while the Legion may be rusted, it will never truly break. In a
galaxy filled with progress and diplomacy, the Rusted Legion remains an
anachronism of brutality---living proof that conquest and survival are
their own forms of power. The Shatter Stance

Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, the Shatter Stance emerged as
a loose alliance of exiled Hobgoblin stances who rejected submission
under the founder of New Cauldron. Forged in the chaos of the
Titanworld's outer reaches, their identity became one of survival,
defiance, and bitterness. Known for their brutal methods and siegecraft
expertise, the Ironstone Stance and others like the Ash Marchers laid
the foundation for a society that would endure through strength and
conflict. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Shatter Stance had established
itself as a formidable military power on the edge of the Devoid Systems.
Their society, united under the Ironshard Concord, balanced
technological innovation with relentless warfare. The Warforges, massive
siege walkers, were a symbol of their dominance, while their engineers
and tacticians were renowned for their pragmatic brutality. As a member
of the Galactic League of States, the Shatter Stance served as a vocal
detractor, opposing efforts to curb war and conflict. Their deep
resentment toward New Cauldron's legacy remained a central tenet of
their cultural identity. From 851 to 1851 300 to 900 - The Exile of the
First Stances Hobgoblin stances that had once dominated Cauldron are
shattered and driven into exile by the founder of New Cauldron, seeking
to consolidate his rule. Among the exiled, the Ironstone Stance, the
Broken Glaive, and the Ash Marchers unite under a single banner, forging
a new identity on the Titanworld's inhospitable borders. Their leaders
vow to reclaim what was lost, coining the term "Shatter Stance" to
symbolize their resilience and defiance. 950 - The Exodus of Shards With
the Titanworld's escalating titan-born conflicts rendering survival
impossible, the Shatter Stance flees into the stars. Unlike the
organized exoduses of other nations, theirs is fragmented, with various
stances settling in isolated systems scattered across the Devoid
Systems. It is an age of conflict and brutal adaptation, as the stances
clash with local warlords, alien species, and remnants of other exiled
peoples. 1025 - The Shard Wars The Shard Wars erupt among the various
stances, as warbands seek to dominate resources and establish
leadership. The Ironstone Stance, known for their brutal siege warfare
and fortress-building skills, eventually prevails, creating the first
unified council of warlords: the Ironshard Concord. While this council
