brings temporary peace, its rule remains tenuous, with dissent festering
in the shadows. 1100 - The Siege of Drot's Bastion A massive force of
exiled Hobgoblins defeats an Orog raiding clan at Drot's Bastion. The
victory solidifies the Shatter Stance's military doctrine of fortified
cities, but at the cost of strained relations with neighboring powers.
1200 - The Cauldron Decree To assert independence from New Cauldron's
influence, the Shatter Stance declares the Cauldron Decree, outlawing
all hobgoblins from being members of New Cauldron mercenary companies,
with several groups listed by name - including the Vault Stance. This
ignites cultural-based persecution for hobgoblins fleeing New Cauldron.
1250 - The Rise of the Warforges Shatter Stance engineers develop
Warforges, massive siege walkers powered by salvaged and stolen
technology. These mechanical titans become a symbol of Shatter Stance's
power, enabling them to expand aggressively into

neighboring systems. Their technological innovations earn them a
reputation as masters of brutal, methodical warfare. \_1350 - The
Ironfront Accord Under external pressure from the League of Ki, the
Shatter Stance signs the Ironfront Accord, securing economic stability
but locking the nation into unfavorable trade obligations. This leads to
deep resentment among the working class. 1460 - The Iron Edict Facing
repeated rebellions from newly absorbed systems, the council enforces
the Iron Edict, a brutal decree that mandates the suppression of local
cultures and the assimilation of conquered peoples. While effective in
maintaining control, this policy deepens resentment and exacerbates
internal divisions among the stances. 1550 - The Shattermarch Crusades
The Shatter Stance launches the Shattermarch Crusades, a series of
military campaigns against nearby factions, including the Old Horde and
the League of Ki, to secure resources and bolster their influence.
Though initially successful, the campaigns overextend their forces,
leading to a costly stalemate. 1715 - Reluctant Entry into the Galactic
League The Shatter Stance reluctantly joins the Galactic League of
States after intense pressure from neighboring nations. Many see this as
a betrayal of their warrior culture, fueling resentment among the
stances. The Shatter Stance quickly establishes itself as a detractor
within the League, opposing resolutions that limit war, raiding, and
conquest or slavery. 1750 - The Chains of Exile A resurgence of
nationalist sentiment sweeps through the Shatter Stance as historians
uncover more records of the original Hobgoblin exiles from Cauldron.
Known as the Chains of Exile Movement, this cultural revival inspires
renewed militarism and disdain for galactic norms. The Ironstone Stance
declares the need for a "Reckoning" with New Cauldron, though direct
conflict is avoided due to the Galactic League's influence. 1800 - The
Forgeclad Uprising Discontented laborers and oppressed peoples within
Shatter Stance systems rise up in the Forgeclad Uprising, using stolen
Warforge technology against their Oligarchic rulers. The uprising is
crushed after years of brutal conflict, but the scars remain, deepening
divisions between the ruling warlords and their subjects. 1851 - A
Fractured but Unyielding Nation By 1851, the Shatter Stance remains a
paradox: a technologically advanced, militaristic nation fueled by
cultural bitterness and relentless ambition. Their Warforges and brutal
combat doctrines make them a force to be reckoned with, but internal
divisions and resentment toward the Galactic League limit their
potential. The Shatter Stance remains defiant, a fractured nation that
thrives on war and conflict. In 1851 By 1851 AG, the Shatter Stance
stands as a fractured yet unyielding nation. Their relentless
militarism, advanced siege technologies, and Warforges keep them
dominant on the galactic fringes, but their internal divisions and
cultural bitterness limit their growth. The scars of the Forgeclad
Uprising and the nationalist Chains of Exile Movement have left the
ruling council wary and authoritarian, suppressing dissent while
maintaining their warrior traditions. Though they are a member of the
Galactic League, their opposition to galactic norms ensures they remain
isolated, a stubborn bastion of Hobgoblin resilience in a galaxy that
increasingly seeks peace. The Stars of Barbarism

Rise of a Nation By 851, the Old Horde had grown unstable, plagued by
internal strife and challenges to its Warchief's authority. Splinter
factions emerged, leading to widespread distrust among the barbarian
clans. Though still a formidable force, cracks had formed in the Horde's
unity, foreshadowing its eventual collapse. Neighboring nations like the
Shatter Stance and the Orog Clans watched eagerly, waiting for the
inevitable chaos to spill forth. In the year 1000, it had finally
happened - and the Stars of Barbarism were born. Founding to 1851 Year
1000 - The Collapse of the Old Horde The death of the last Warchief's
grandson shatters the Old Horde's fragile unity. Clans of orcs, ogres,
trolls, and beastfolk splinter into hundreds of warbands, each led by
chieftains or warlords vying for dominance. The oncegreat Horde
dissolves into The Stars of Barbarism, a lawless expanse rife with
bloodshed and chaos. Neighboring nations brace for the resulting
anarchy, as marauders pour out of the region in search of plunder. Year
1030 - The Great Bloodhunt The Aurath Ascendancy, viewing the barbarian
splintering as an opportunity, launches a crusade into the Stars of
Barbarism. Their aim is to enslave the warbands and conquer key systems.
However, the fractured clans respond viciously, banding together in
temporary alliances. Known as the Great Bloodhunt, this decade-long war
forces the Ascendancy to retreat after suffering catastrophic losses,
leaving behind ruined fleets and gutted colonies. The warlords loot the
remains, arming themselves with captured ships and weapons. Year 1080 -
The Warlord's Gambit A cunning ogre warlord named Gargaz the Starbreaker
unites several tribes under his banner, establishing a fleeting empire
in the eastern Stars. Gargaz's forces sack several outposts of the Orog
Clans of Xonoth, leading to retaliatory strikes from the Orogs. By 1090,
internal betrayal among Gargaz's lieutenants causes his empire to
crumble, leaving his legacy as another tale of ambition thwarted by
barbaric greed. Year 1130 - The Shatter Stance Raids The Shatter Stance
exploits the lawlessness of the Stars of Barbarism, launching raids to
capture resources, slaves, and starships. These raids escalate into a
long cycle of violence as warbands retaliate, attacking Shatter Stance
outposts and convoys. While the Hobgoblins initially see success, they
learn the perils of waging war against scattered foes---defeating one
warband simply creates another. Year 1190 - The Rise of the Rime-King
From the frozen voids of the western Stars emerges Tharnak the
Rime-King, a troll chieftain who uses scavenged cryogenic technological
relics of the second era to empower his followers with frost-forged
weapons. Tharnak leads brutal campaigns, freezing entire starships and
worlds in his wake. By 1205, Tharnak meets his end when his fleet is
ambushed by combined warbands desperate to end his tyranny. His
frost-forged technology becomes a highly sought-after treasure, but is
never found among the wreckages of his fleets in the following years.
Year 1250 - The Maw of the Old Horde Barbarian warbands stumble upon The
Maw, a region deep in the Stars littered with ancient wrecks and
dangerous anomalies. Believed to be the remnants of titanic battles from
the Second Material Plane, the Maw becomes a deadly proving ground for
warlords. Those who return wield powerful weapons and salvaged
artifacts, earning respect among the warbands. However, most who enter
are never seen again, spawning legends of a lurking terror within the
Maw. Year 1315 - The Iron Teeth Pact

In response to growing incursions by the Orog Clans of Xonoth, several
beastfolk tribes unite under the Iron Teeth Pact. While the pact lasts
only a few decades, it successfully repels the Orogs, demonstrating that
the scattered clans can occasionally achieve brief unity when pushed to
the brink. Year 1380 - The Starfire Plunder The Shatter Stance escalates
operations within the Stars, establishing a trade network to buy and
sell salvaged goods looted by warbands. Known as the Starfire Plunder,
this arrangement briefly stabilizes the region economically, with
warlords profiting from selling slaves and relics. However, in 1400, the
Shatter Stance is betrayed when several warbands raid their largest
trade station, plunging the region back into chaos. Year 1500 - The
Endless War of Karnath Two massive warbands---The Karnath Claw and The
Gorthak Maw---wage a century-long war over control of the largest
salvage site in the Stars. The conflict becomes so destructive that
nearby systems are rendered uninhabitable. The war finally ends when
both warbands destroy each other in a climactic battle, leaving the site
a desolate graveyard of ships and bodies. Year 1700 - Skirmish with
Karak Ankor'dûm Forces Dwarven forces from Karak Ankor'dûm launch an
exploratory fleet into the Stars of Barbarism, seeking to claim valuable
salvage and establish footholds. The barbarians respond with
overwhelming ferocity, inflicting heavy casualties on the Dwarves.
Despite winning the battle, the warbands suffer severe losses, and Karak
Ankor'dûm chooses to withdraw. The failure sparks controversy within the
Galactic League of States, as neighboring nations question the Dwarves'
intentions. The victory isn't worth the cost - toppling many warlord
states and slaying billions as the Karak Ankor'dûm death squads sweep
planets and remnant space stations of life. Year 1750 - The Rime-King's
Legacy Fragments of Tharnak the Rime-King's frost-forged relics are
rediscovered in the Maw. Several warlords claim to wield his legacy,
leading to renewed conflicts as rival warbands vie for control of the
relics. The frosted technology spreads across the Stars, becoming
symbols of strength and power. Year 1851 - The Eternal Strife By 1851,
the Stars of Barbarism remain a fragmented realm of warbands,
scavengers, and marauders. The cycle of violence, brief unity, and
collapse continues. The region is a constant threat to its
neighbors---The Orog Clans of Xonoth, the Aurath Ascendancy, and the
Shatter Stance---as warbands raid outposts and plunder trade routes.
Attempts to bring order to the Stars are met with hostility, as the
warlords cling to their anarchic traditions. In 1851 By 1851, the Stars
of Barbarism are a brutal and fractured region where no single warlord
or faction holds lasting control. Orcs, ogres, trolls, and beastfolk
perpetuate cycles of raiding, scavenging, and bloodshed, clinging to the
chaotic traditions of the Old Horde. The Maw remains a deadly lure for
ambitious warlords, promising power at great risk. Relics of the
Rime-King and ruins of ancient wars litter the Stars, serving as both
prizes and battlegrounds. To the neighboring Orog Clans, Aurath
Ascendancy, and Shatter Stance, the Stars are both a threat and a
resource. Efforts to tame the region have failed, as the warbands
fiercely resist any semblance of order. The Stars of Barbarism endure as
a lawless, untamed frontier where might makes right and survival is the
only constant. The Warrens Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era,
the ratfolk of the Myr Clusters were a fractured collection of
decentralized worlds, each identifying more with their local warren than
with a collective identity. These sprawling urban-industrial centers
were

choked by smog and powered by the volatile, mutagenic properties of
Skarlmagicka. While the Greater Warrens controlled vast resources and
influence, the Minor Warrens toiled in poverty and subjugation. The
seeds of rebellion were planted as dissatisfaction grew among laboring
ratfolk, setting the stage for Skrit's revolution. Nation in 851 By 851,
The Warrens were embroiled in chaos. Skrit, a charismatic and cunning
leader, had launched his revolution against the ruling clans of Txic'Er,
using a combination of guerrilla warfare, mass mobilization, and
Skarlmagickafueled propaganda. The ratfolk population was divided, with
Greater Warrens supporting the status quo while Lesser and Minor Warrens
rallied behind Skrit. The skies of the Myr Clusters burned as industrial
centers became battlegrounds, and the fragmented nature of ratfolk
society left their enemies baffled by the war's unpredictability. From
851 to 1851 Year 851 - The Revolution of Txic'Er Begins Skrit, a former
miner and mercenary, declares independence from the ruling ratfolk clans
of Txic'Er, a Lesser Warren, with the backing of his private military
company, LFTx Systems. From a moon-based headquarters, he orchestrates
the first major victories of the revolution, using Skarlmagicka as both
a weapon and a symbol of divine favor. Year 860 - The Fall of Greater
Warren Klawhaven Revolutionary forces, armed with unconventional tactics
and poisoned reserves of Skarlmagicka, overrun Klawhaven, one of the
largest Greater Warrens. The fall of Klawhaven signals the revolution's
legitimacy and inspires widespread uprisings across the Myr Clusters.
Year 881 - The Blooded Frontier Revolutionary factions clash with
loyalist forces along the Warrens' borders. Skrit implements a brutal
scorchedearth policy, sacrificing entire Minor Warrens to stall loyalist
advances. The mutagenic effects of Skarlmagicka poison these worlds,
rendering them uninhabitable but securing revolutionary control of
critical routes. Year 950 - The Myr Cluster Ceasefire After decades of
conflict, loyalist factions splinter, with many ratfolk worlds pledging
allegiance to Skrit's revolution. A formal ceasefire is declared in most
regions, though isolated conflicts persist. Skrit's faction consolidates
power, marking the revolution's de facto victory. Year 981 - The
Unification of The Warrens Skrit's forces achieve total dominance over
the Myr Clusters. Skrit declares himself the First Burrowlord,
establishing a centralized yet tenuous government for the first time in
the Warrens' history. Skrit's vision of a unified ratfolk nation becomes
reality, albeit at great cost. Year 1000 - The Death of Skrit Skrit dies
suddenly, with scientists attributing his long life and rapid death to
heavy Skarlmagicka exposure. His death sparks political instability as
competing factions within the Warrens vie for control. Year 1050 - The
Era of Silent Claws The Silent Claws, an enigmatic faction of
revolutionary veterans, rise to power under the leadership of Scryn
Nexas. This era is marked by political assassinations, internal purges,
and consolidation of Skrit's reforms. Year 1150 - Industrial Overdrive

The Warrens enter a period of unchecked industrialization, driven by the
extraction and refinement of Skarlmagicka. Toxic smog and rampant
mutation plague the population, while the Warrens' technological
capabilities expand dramatically. Year 1300 - The Border Conflicts Begin
Facing incursions from the Coldiron Caliphate and New Cauldron, The
Warrens leverage their massive population and unconventional tactics to
repel their enemies. Despite heavy losses, they maintain territorial
integrity, cementing their reputation as a fierce and unpredictable
power. Year 1400 - The Plague of Shifting Forms A mutation-driven
epidemic spreads through The Warrens, mutating thousands of ratfolk into
grotesque forms. The disease is eventually contained by the Plagueguard,
a specialized unit of bio-engineered soldiers, but the event deepens
fears of Skarlmagicka's dangers. Year 1500 - The Treaty of Scars The
Warrens and New Cauldron broker an uneasy truce, establishing neutral
trade zones to reduce the frequency of border skirmishes. Though
fragile, the treaty stabilizes the region and allows the Warrens to
refocus on internal development. Year 1685 - Joining the Galactic League
of States The Warrens join the Galactic League of States, reluctantly
presenting themselves as a stabilizing force in the Myr Clusters. Their
membership is controversial, as neighboring nations fear their chaotic
nature and reliance on Skarlmagicka. Year 1750 - The Ascension of the
Scarl Lords A new faction, the Scarl Lords, rises to power, advocating
for increased refinement and militarization of Skarlmagicka. Under their
rule, the Warrens develop advanced hybrid technologies but risk
alienating the Galactic League with their volatile practices. Year
1850 - The Smogged Future By 1851, The Warrens stand as a grim yet
powerful force in the Myr Clusters. Their worlds are heavily polluted
but teeming with life, their military vast and unpredictable. The
reliance on Skarlmagicka defines their technological and social
identity, for better or worse. In 1851 By 1851, The Warrens were a
grimly industrialized, heavily populated nation. Their worlds, shrouded
in smog and glowing with the unstable light of Skarlmagicka, were a
testament to their ingenuity and recklessness. The Scarl Lords, a ruling
faction of militaristic and industrial leaders, had brought
technological advancements but at great environmental and social cost.
Their military, while vast and unpredictable, remained a key deterrent
against incursions from New Cauldron and the Coldiron Caliphate. Despite
their chaotic reputation, The Warrens were recognized as a vital power
in the Myr Clusters and a key, if uneasy, player in galactic politics.
Trade League of Castiel Nation in early 3E In the early Third Era, the
remnants of Baphomet's followers clung to survival in the ruined
underwater city of Kael'tharion, one of the last domed bastions of their
civilization on the Titanworld. Surrounded by the crushing depths of the
ocean and the remnants of their once-glorious faith, they mourned the
loss of Baphomet, their dragongod of balance, whose death at the hands
of Yu had left their people adrift. It was in this desperate moment that

Samulo, the proclaimed son of Carrigore and herald of the Cosmo Cult,
descended upon Kael'tharion with legions of fanatical warriors. He
offered Lord Castiel, the ruler of the House of Castiel, a grim
ultimatum: embrace Carrigore as their titan patron or face annihilation
as the legions of tieflings poured through portals in the domed city.
Faced with the complete destruction of his people, Lord Castiel, whose
nanite-enhanced body carried the weight of centuries, made the fateful
decision to accept Samulo's pact. In a somber ceremony beneath the
shimmering waters above Kael'tharion, the survivors renounced Yu's name,
swearing loyalty to Carrigore and joining the Carrigorian Pantheon.
Their devotion was rewarded with stability, as Samulo spared the city
and uplifted the Trade League of Castiel as a vital part of the
pantheon's ambitions. However, the League chose a path distinct from
Carrigore Space's raiding and pillaging, instead harnessing their skills
in trade, diplomacy, and martial discipline. Kael'tharion became the
name of the first world they claimed as their own, and became the hub of
a burgeoning trade network that funneled wealth and resources to support
Carrigorian wars while preserving the remnants of Baphomet's philosophy
of balance within their new pantheon. This pact marked the beginning of
a new chapter for the Trade League, transforming them from a shattered
remnant into a key economic and spiritual force for the Carrigorian
Pantheon and a deeper connection to Baphomet as the pantheons newest
member. Nation in 851 By 851 AG, the Trade League of Castiel has evolved
into a prosperous and tightly controlled interstellar nation, with its
influence radiating outward from its capital world of Kael'tharion. The
domed city, now transformed into a sprawling megapolis beneath the sea
and beyond, stands as both a marvel of resilience and a symbol of their
commitment to Carrigore. Towering spires of black and white stone, laced
with glowing runes of balance, reflect the League's devotion to
Baphomet's ideals even under their new allegiance to the Carrigorian
Pantheon. At the heart of their culture lies the Castielian Order, a
disciplined martial and spiritual tradition composed of monks, paladins,
and knights who channel the twin flames of black and white fire in
battle, embodying the League's pursuit of balance in both faith and
combat. The Trade League's economy thrives on its expansive Galactic
Trade Concourse, a network of trade stations and shipping routes
connecting their worlds to allied Carrigorian strongholds and neutral
systems. They are a key player in funneling wealth and resources to the
Carrigorian war machine, a role that has garnered them suspicion and
resentment within the Galactic League of States, where they are often
derided as a Carrigorian proxy. Nevertheless, their strategic trade
alliances and the diplomatic acumen of Lord Castiel---whose
nanite-enhanced longevity has made him a living legend---ensure the
League's place as a vital economic hub. Despite their prosperity,
tensions simmer beneath the surface. Many within the League struggle to
reconcile their devotion to Baphomet's philosophy of balance with their
role as financiers of Carrigorian conquest. Meanwhile, the influence of
Samulo and the Cosmo Cult creates a delicate tightrope for Lord Castiel,
who must maintain the League's distinct identity while fulfilling their
obligations to Carrigore. In 851 AG, the Trade League of Castiel is a
nation walking the line between independence and servitude, balance and
submission, striving to preserve its legacy amid the shifting tides of
galactic power. From 851 to 1851 870 - The Call of the Border Wars
Carrigore Space demands an unprecedented tribute from the Trade League
to support their wars along the Border Systems. Thousands of knights,
monks, and laborers are conscripted, leaving Kael'tharion and its
surrounding worlds strained for manpower. This begins a century of heavy
burdens that push the League's resources to the brink. 905 - Divergence
of the League

After centuries of serving as a Carrigorian proxy, internal dissent
arises within the Trade League. A merchant-prince known as Kael'Zar the
Steadfast declares the Divergence, a movement to restore Baphomet's
teachings in secret. This schism destabilizes key trade hubs and pits
merchant factions against each other in economic warfare. 910 - The
Blackfire Rebellion A faction of dissidents within the League, calling
themselves the Blackfire Rebellion, rises in opposition to the
unyielding demands of Carrigore Space. The rebellion is crushed by Lord
Castiel's Paladin Guard, but it leaves scars on the League's internal
unity, prompting stricter control measures and the establishment of the
Order of the Eternal Balance, tasked with suppressing future
insurrections. 1020 - The Bloodtithe Edict Carrigore mandates an
increase in both manpower and wealth to fund their expanding wars. Known
as the Bloodtithe Edict, this decree forces the League to expand its
trade operations at the cost of overburdening its workers. Strikes and
small-scale riots break out in several key trade hubs, but Lord
Castiel's diplomatic and military intervention prevents widespread
unrest. 1022 - The Red Silk Crisis Smugglers begin distributing illegal
Baphomet relics disguised as ceremonial silks. The League enacts harsh
sanctions, leading to the Red Silk Crisis---a bloody crackdown on
smugglers that escalates into a three-year rebellion led by the
Sisterhood of the Silken Blade, a cult of rogue mercenaries. 1125 - The
Siege of Blackwater Spire A rogue faction of the Cosmo Cult,
dissatisfied with the League's refusal to fully integrate into
Carrigorian raiding practices, attempts to seize Blackwater Spire, a
vital trade hub. The siege is repelled by the League's Knights of Dual
Flame, who use their mastery of black and white fire to overwhelm the
invaders. The victory strengthens the League's position as a distinct
but loyal ally of Carrigore Space. 1200 - The Kael'tharion Compact To
quell rebellions and reestablish dominance, Lord Castiel decrees the
Kael'tharion Compact, granting limited autonomy to rebellious trade
guilds. Though peace is restored, distrust festers, as Castiel's heavy
taxation continues to fund Carrigorian wars. 1250 - The Gilded Trade
Compact To recover from economic stagnation caused by centuries of
tribute, the League enacts the Gilded Trade Compact, reforming its trade
routes to increase efficiency and profits. Though successful in
stabilizing the economy, the Compact further widens the gap between the
wealthy merchant elite and the struggling lower classes, sowing the
seeds of future unrest. 1500 - The Pact Reaffirmed In a grand ceremony
attended by emissaries from Carrigore Space, Lord Castiel publicly
reaffirms the League's loyalty to Carrigore. This event, known as the
Pact Reaffirmed, is met with mixed reactions; while it cements the
League's position within the Carrigorian Pantheon, it also reignites
dissent among those who feel the League has lost its independence.
1651 - The Verdant Flame Revolt A coalition of trade workers and minor
nobles, inspired by Baphomet's philosophy of balance, stages the Verdant
Flame Revolt, demanding reduced tribute and greater autonomy. The
rebellion is brutally suppressed by Lord Castiel's forces, but not
before it disrupts trade routes and destabilizes several outer colonies.
1751 - The 3000th Birthday of Lord Castiel The League celebrates Lord
Castiel's 3000th birthday with galaxy-wide ceremonies, underscoring his
role as the League's eternal leader. During the event, rebels disguised
as emissaries attempt to assassinate him, but the plot is

foiled by the Order of the Eternal Balance. The incident serves as a
stark reminder of the internal tensions that continue to plague the
League. 1825 - The Rift Crisis A Carrigorian war effort in the Border
Systems collapses due to a massive Rift incursion, forcing the Trade
League to deploy the majority of its fleets to assist. This leaves the
League's trade routes vulnerable, and opportunistic pirates and rival
factions exploit the chaos. The crisis sparks debates within the League
about the sustainability of their alliance with Carrigore Space. 1851 -
A Strained Proxy State By 1851, the Trade League of Castiel remains a
vital economic hub for the Carrigorian Pantheon but is weighed down by
centuries of tribute and internal strife. Lord Castiel, still immortal
and indomitable, works tirelessly to hold the League together, but
tensions between its identity as a sovereign trade power and its role as
a Carrigorian proxy threaten to unravel its stability. Though it
endures, the League faces an uncertain future, its loyalty constantly
tested by the cost of its allegiance. In 1851 In 1851 AG, the Trade
League of Castiel is a nation defined by compromise and survival. Once a
proud bastion of balance and martial discipline, the League now
functions as a shadow of its former self, its resources drained and its
society fractured by centuries of servitude to Carrigore. While their
trade networks and alliances with Carrigorian forces ensure their
continued existence, they are widely regarded as a proxy state, their
autonomy eroded by relentless demands. Beneath the surface, dissent
festers among their people, leaving the League at a precarious
crossroads as they face an uncertain future.
